# AILANG Changelog: Stdlib Delegation & CI Gates (v0.32+)

> Current changelog (v0.32.0 onward). Earlier series: [v0.26-v0.31-measurement-cost.md](v0.26-v0.31-measurement-cost.md). Index: [CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed — M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S5 M3: `trace`, `observatory`, `dashboard` and `eval-chains` fold into `chains` (2026-09-18)

Four top-level commands answered the same question — what did a run actually do — over the same
three stores. They are now namespaces under `chains`, which is the one the fleet reaches for
(`git grep` over tracked files: **216** references to `ailang chains`, against 66 for `trace`, 63
for `observatory`, 37 for `eval-chains`, 32 for `dashboard`):

```
ailang chains trace status            ailang chains dashboard spans --provider gemini
ailang chains observatory backfill    ailang chains eval list
```

**Every old spelling still works**, for one release, and is unchanged on stdout. `ailang trace
list --limit 10` is byte-identical to what it printed before: the alias routes *through* `chains`
and straight back out to the same function, so the two spellings are one code path rather than two
that can drift. The four rows are now Hidden — help stops advertising a spelling that goes away
after one release — but they still resolve, so the 93-route D1 fixture
(`commands_spellings_test.go`) passes **unchanged, with no route removed**.

**Removed under D7 — the eight zero-caller `observatory` subcommands**: `seed`, `heatmap`,
`evolution`, `usage`, `tokens`, `outliers`, `metrics`, `hierarchy` (2,398 LOC of deleted files;
**−2,249 net** in `cmd/ailang` excluding the new test, **−1,933** including it). Each was verified
at **zero** references in `Makefile`, `make/`, `tools/`, `scripts/`,
`.github/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/rules/`, with the only citation
anywhere a line in the frozen devtools prompt `prompts/devtools/v0.8.0.md`. `git log -S` puts the
last change to each at `7bfb1e834` (2026-01-22) — itself a mechanical file split — over content
last touched 2026-01-07 … 2026-01-19. (The audit's "2026-04-21" is `de4aa7ba8`, the module-path
rename; the 2026-09-15 touches on `observatory_hierarchy*.go` are S4 sweeps.) `normalizeWorkspacePath`
went with them: its only two callers were among the deleted files, and `internal/server` has its
own copy.

**Kept, with the evidence**: `observatory backfill` (6 exec references), `repair-ids` (2),
`cleanup` and `sync-chat`; all four `trace` subcommands (the `trace-debugger` skill alone holds 58);
`dashboard spans` (4 skill references) and its siblings; all four `eval-chains` subcommands (4 each).
Nothing with a live caller was removed.

- **`ailang chains --help` is real help now.** It was answering from the dispatch table's generic
  four-line block, which cannot name a namespace; `chains` left `helpFallbackCommands` and answers
  for itself, listing its subcommands and the four folded namespaces.
- **Bare-group exit codes normalised** (M1 finding 3). `ailang trace`, `ailang observatory` and
  `ailang dashboard` printed their subcommand list and exited **0**; they now exit **1**, like
  `chains`, `pkg` and `daemon` always did. `models`, `workspaces` and `budget` still exit 0 and
  belong to other milestones.
- **The dashboard UI's copy-paste hints were rewritten** to the `chains` spelling
  (`CliCommandHint.tsx`, `ExecHierarchy/ExecHierarchyPopover.tsx`, `ExecHierarchy/ChatHistory.tsx`).
  The web dashboard itself never used these CLI paths — `internal/server` contains no `exec.Command`
  to the `ailang` binary at all and reads the stores as a library through `handlers_chains.go`,
  `handlers_statistics.go` and `handlers_controlplane_heatmap.go` — but it *printed* the commands,
  and a dashboard that teaches a retired command is the defect Phase 3 item 6 fixes in the docs.
  The checked-in bundles (`ui/dist`, `internal/server/dist`) still carry the old strings and are
  left to the UI build owner.

**A twin comparison cannot see a fault the twins share** — measured here, not theorised. Mutating
the chains dispatcher to hand a namespace its own name back broke `ailang trace status` and
`ailang chains trace status` *identically*, and a 31-pair alias-vs-canonical byte-diff reported
31/31 identical while both printed `Unknown trace subcommand: trace`. The instrument with teeth is
the before/after surface diff against `bin/ailang-before` (it caught the same mutant on 762 lines),
plus a test that pins each route to output it must actually produce. Both are now in
`commands_folded_test.go`, and every test in it was mutation-tested.

### Changed — M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S5 M2: groups and aliases, and `ailang --help` fits one screen (2026-09-18)

`ailang --help` listed **89 top-level commands**; it now lists **17**, which closes the one gate
Phase 3 owns (`commands_top_level` 89 → ≤ 20). Nothing was renamed and nothing was removed. Every
command that is no longer on that list is filed in a hidden group — `dev` (language development and
diagnostics) or `ops` (the message plane, coordinator, mission, chains, models) — and **keeps its
own top-level spelling as well**, because 593 fleet references call `ailang messages`, 332 call
`ailang coordinator` and 216 call `ailang chains` from launchd drivers, make targets, skills and
workflows, and D1 says not one of them changes.

The visible 17: `check docs eval examples fmt help iface init lsp pkg prompt repl run serve test
verify version`, plus a footer pointing at `ailang dev --help` and `ailang ops --help`.

Measured with `tools/cli_surface_snapshot.sh` against a `bin/ailang-before` built from the merge-base
with identical ldflags: **173 of 180 records byte-identical**, and every alias path is in that set.
The seven that differ are the intended help changes listed below. Separately, 38 top-level/group
pairs were byte-diffed against each other (`ailang trace status` vs `ailang ops trace status`, and so
on) — all identical, with a same-route self-comparison as the control.

- **`eval` is a group**: `ailang eval suite`, `eval report`, `eval elo`, `eval paired`,
  `eval browser-profile` and the rest now work, with the subcommand derived by stripping the `eval-`
  prefix so there is no second list to keep in step. All 15 `eval-*` spellings still work, and a
  bare `ailang eval --benchmark fizzbuzz --mock` still runs a benchmark: only a first argument that
  matches a subcommand is routed.
- **`ailang check --verify` absorbs `ai-check`.** Both spellings now reach ONE function,
  `runAICheckReport`; `ai-check` is an alias of `check` and keeps its own flag set, so its bytes and
  its exit contract (`ai_check_exit_test.go`, and the DP7 done-gate that depends on it) are
  unchanged. The verification flags on `check` are `--verify-timeout` and `--verify-recursive-depth`,
  not `--timeout`: `check --timeout` already means a compilation deadline, and two meanings on one
  flag name is the defect M5 exists to fix, not one to add.
- **`pkg` gained the eight verbs that only had a bare spelling**: `ailang pkg add`, `pkg lock`,
  `pkg tree`, `pkg install`, `pkg search`, `pkg publish`, `pkg unpublish`, `pkg docs`. `ailang pkg
  add` did not work before this; `ailang add` did. Both do now, generated from one definition.
- **`ailang help [command [subcommand]]`** is a real command, routed through the same `--help`
  fallback table the dispatcher uses, so `ailang help X` and `ailang X --help` cannot answer
  differently.
- **`internal-dump-iface` is hidden again.** M1's generated help exposed it for the first time; it
  is an internal subprocess entry point (`internal/pkg/iface_subprocess.go` execs it) and was
  deliberately absent from the pre-S5 hand-written help. `debug`, `ast-edit` and `select-best` are
  developer tools, not internal plumbing, so they went to `dev` rather than being hidden outright.
- `policy-check` and `axioms` are in `dev` — **hidden, not removed**. `policy-check` is live
  (`6236deb24` extracted `admitProgram` from it for `ailang run --policy`, and `policyCheckOutput`'s
  JSON field names are part of the M-AGENT-SAFE-RUNNER contract).

Two defects the byte-diff caught that no test would have, because both exit 0 while reading the
wrong argument:

- A group route has to present the **top-level argv**, and the binary has TWO argument sources.
  44 call sites slice `os.Args[2:]`/`[3:]` directly; another family (`trace`, `chains`, `dashboard`,
  `axioms`, `editor`, `budget`, `daemon`) reads the global `flag.Args()`. Rewriting only the first
  made `ailang ops trace status` read its subcommand as `trace` and exit 1 where `ailang trace
  status` exits 0, and made `ailang dev axioms --help` print the whole axiom scorecard instead of
  its usage block. `runAsName` now resets both, in one place, which is what makes the group routes
  identical by construction rather than by 44 separate edits.
- A **language command reached through its group must stay quiet at startup**. `ailang dev builtins`
  emitted the two observatory-retention lines that `ailang builtins` does not, because the startup
  decision only looked at the first word. It now follows one group hop (S1 M6's contract).

`Command.Group` and the language/platform split were one field in M1 and are now two: where a
command is *filed* and whether it is *quiet at startup* are different questions with different
consumers, and collapsing them would have turned `disasm`'s probes on the moment it moved to `dev`.

The D1 fixture (`cmd/ailang/testdata/pre_s5_spellings.txt`, 93 routes) is **generated from the
pre-M2 binary** by `tools/cli_surface_snapshot.sh --spellings`, never hand-typed — the spellings a
person forgets are exactly the ones that break a caller (`msg`, `brain`, `microrag`, `urag`, `serve`,
`serve-api`, `internal-dump-iface`, and the seven bare pkg verbs). Control: the 83 top-level routes
parsed out of that binary's help diffed clean against the independently-measured `COMMANDS` list in
the snapshot script. All seven new guards were mutation-tested — each mutation applied, the failure
observed, the tree restored.

The **caller sweep is explicitly not in this milestone**: no driver, make target, skill or workflow
was edited, which is the point of the aliases.

## [v0.40.0] - 2026-09-17

### Changed — M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S5 M1: the CLI is a dispatch table, and `--help` is answered everywhere (2026-09-17)

`cmd/ailang/main.go` was a hand-written switch with 78 `case` clauses over 83 command spellings, and
`cmd/ailang/help.go` a 505-line hand-maintained `printHelp()` that had drifted away from it. The
switch is now a lookup in `cmd/ailang/commands.go` — `{Name, Aliases, Group, Hidden, Summary, Run}`,
split by group across `commands_language.go`, `commands_eval.go`, `commands_platform.go` and
`commands_pkg.go` — and the command sections of `--help` are generated from the same rows, so the
two can no longer disagree. main.go keeps only flag parsing, the global setup and the lookup: 674
lines to 127, with **no command `case` label left in it**. No spelling changed: all 78 commands and
the five aliases (`msg`, `brain`, `microrag`, `urag`, `serve`) route exactly as before, and the
groups/aliases work is M2.

Measured, not asserted: `tools/cli_surface_snapshot.sh <binary> <outfile>` records exit code, stdout
and stderr for every command and alias (`--help`, and bare where that is safe — `eval-suite` has no
no-op scope, `daemon` used to start a daemon), scrubbed to be byte-stable, and both binaries are
built with identical ldflags because a version stamp once produced 196 false diffs. **154 of 180
records are byte-identical**; the 26 that differ are the intended changes below.

- **`--help` exits 0 at every level.** Twenty top-level commands rejected it before — eight groups
  (`chains`, `dashboard`, `daemon`, `eval-chains`, `observatory`, `pi`, `pkg`, `trace`) with an
  "unknown subcommand" error, and `ast-edit`, `builtins`, `doctor`, `eval-compare`, `eval-elo`,
  `eval-matrix`, `eval-report`, `eval-summary`, `eval-sweet-spot`, `eval-trend`,
  `internal-dump-iface` and `watch` by reading `--help` as a filename, a results directory or an
  unknown flag (`ailang watch --help` started watching a file called `--help`). Two `pkg` verbs
  (`provenance`, `history`) did the same. The dispatcher answers for exactly that measured set
  (`helpFallbackCommands`); every other command keeps its own, richer help.
- **`ailang daemon` with no arguments no longer starts the daemon.** It defaulted its subcommand to
  `run`, which is how a bare `daemon` came to be the most dangerous default in the CLI. It now
  prints the subcommand list and exits 1.
- **An unknown command prints one line and a did-you-mean, on stderr, with nothing on stdout.** It
  used to dump 16,575 bytes of `printHelp()` to stdout before exiting 1, which buried the error and
  polluted every `ailang <typo> | jq`.
- **`ailang pkg --help` works and `ailang pkg` lists all ten verbs.** The hand-written usage block
  listed nine: `pkg cascade` had been undocumented since it landed.
- `isLanguageCommand()` is now the table's `Group` attribute, with the S1 M6 membership list
  unchanged (25 commands). Language commands still run **no** `observatory.CheckHealth` and **no**
  stale-binary probe, and two tests hold that: an in-process counting fake over every route in
  the table, and an end-to-end control that makes both probes observable from outside the process
  (an oversized observatory DB and a source tree newer than the binary) and counts them — 0 for a
  language command, >=1 for a platform one. Mutation-tested: moving either probe into the hot path,
  dropping the language exemption, reclassifying one command, making a bare `daemon` a silent no-op
  and making it block all fail the tests that cover them. The help-exits-0 test is a negative
  control too — `AILANG_CLI_TEST_BIN=bin/ailang-before go test -run HelpExitsZero` fails on exactly
  the 22 routes listed above.


### Added — M-PKG-QUALITY-LADDER Sprint 1: the package quality gates measure something (2026-09-17)

Measured against the live registry on 2026-09-17 (53 packages / 373 versions): **0 contracts had ever been verified** — the validator ran `ailang verify --json <file>` per file, MOD010'd on every flat tarball, and decoded a bare array the command never printed (`sunholo/deontic/settle` alone proves 7/7); the interface hash was blind to signatures (retype = class A = auto-merge) and its v2 had no caller; `ailang pkg quality` had been documented for agents since 09-08 but did not exist. Design: `design_docs/planned/v0_40_0/m-pkg-quality-ladder.md` (D1–D7 ratified by Mark).

- **One package-aware module resolution** (M1): `pkg.ResolveModuleToFile` is exported and gains the canonical-checkout candidate; `BuildCanonicalJSON` resolves through it and relaxes MOD010 in package mode, so `InterfaceHashV2` builds from flat tarballs and `module_prefix` layouts (before: 0/53 published packages built; after, with real dependency resolution: 31/53, and 18 more pass `check --package` in their shipped layout). New `ailang verify --package <dir> [--json] [--wall-cap]` emits the shared `pkg.PackageVerifyReport` (`ailang.package-verify/v1`), whose decoder refuses the old bare-array shape.
- **The validator banks real contract counts and a shadow v2 identity** (M2): `validation.contracts_error` when the run itself fails (never zero); `interface_hash_v2` / `interface_signatures` / `interface_v2_error` on every publish (metadata schema `ailang.package-metadata/v2`, all new fields `omitempty`, v1 records decode); `PUB005` refuses publisher/validator identity skew naming both hashes; `index.json` gains `contracts_total`; `MaxExportedModules` 64 → 128 (`ailang_parse` exports 56).
- **`ailang pkg quality [--json] [--strict] [--no-run] <dir>`** (M3): one `pkg.BuildQualityReport` behind the command, `publish --dry-run`, `publish` and the validator — compile · contracts · interface identity · effects (D4 privilege rank) · release · docs · style (pure-export ratio) · tests · smoke. Sections carry a provenance: `server` (static, bounded, recomputed by the validator, the only registry gates) or `attested` (tests and `_smoke.ail`, executed on the publisher's machine only, sent as a multipart field, banked stamped with the key owner, never a server gate). Gates vs badges split by stability (D3); `--strict` promotes warn-level badges. `publish` refuses on the same `PUBnnn` before building a tarball; the v0.19.0 smoke gate is folded in (`PUB014`/`PUB015`).
- **Every version describes itself** (M4, D2): `[release] kind = security|fix|feature|breaking` and a non-empty `## <version>` section in `CHANGELOG.md` (three heading forms), shipped in the tarball and banked (`metadata.quality.release`, index `release_kind`); `PUB001`/`PUB002` are badges in v0.40.0 and gates from v0.41.0 (`ReleaseGateHardFrom`); `ailang init package` scaffolds both; `pkg versions` prints kind + notes per version.
- **Every published package gets an agent inbox** (M6, Mark 2026-09-17): the plane config declares one `package_agent_template` section (not an agent — it serves no inbox, so a `pkg:` inbox for a package that is not in the registry still bounces as a visible typo); the coordinator derives a per-package agent for every index package the config does not name — id `pkg-<vendor>-<name>`, workspace / merge_branch / subdirectory / artifact_patterns from `metadata.repository` — at load, every 10 min, and in the CLI readouts. Hand-written entries win. 12/53 packages had no inbox at all (a message to them was accepted and never dispatched — the `sunholo/email` incident of 09-07). A package whose `[metadata] repository` is not a GitHub tree URL gets no agent (a guessed workspace clones the right repo and finds nothing) and `PUB021` warns until it is set. **Requires the `package_agent_template` section in `ailang-multivac` `config.cloud.yaml` (follow-up).**
- **Validator deploys on release tags only** (Mark, 2026-09-17): the ONE registry validator behind `registry.ailang.sunholo.com` had redeployed on every push to `dev` (inline trigger `ailang-registry-validator-dev`); the day it started enforcing gates, a `dev` push shipped a v2 identity that folded the upload's temp-dir path into the hash (fixed in `d7bdad2fb` — identity is the DECLARED module) and every publish fleet-wide was refused with `PUB005` for ~40 minutes. The build config is now `cloudbuild-registry-validator.yaml` (image tagged `:vX.Y.Z` + `:latest`, rollback = redeploy the previous tag) and the managed `ailang-registry-validator-release` trigger (ailang-multivac `terraform-triggers`) fires on `^v.*`.
- **Shadow readout** (M5): validator logs `quality: pkg=… v2=ok|fail …` per publish; `GET /api/stats` carries `v2_clean_streak`, `v2_failures`, `packages_with_contracts` — N=20 clean flips `PUB005` to a gate (design D6).

### Fixed — an approval notice addressed to a deleted agent reached nobody

- `notifyApproval` resolved its inbox from the task's agent, which is right while the agent exists and silently wrong the moment it does not: the notice is filed to a string nobody watches, the approval sits in the queue unannounced, and its age is the only signal. Measured 2026-09-17 on `task-c063b6d2` — agent `daneel-design-ailang`, deleted in the 12 Sept revert — the approval waited **seven days** for a design doc PR #1138 had already merged. `ApprovalNoticeInbox` now checks whether anything actually reads the target (an agent serves it, or it is declared human-triage) and otherwise redirects to `approvals`, the plane's declared human-decision inbox, which `humanTriageInbox` already routes to Discord. The card states which inbox it was redirected from and that the approval is in the queue regardless. A declared triage inbox is left alone — redirecting that would move a notice away from its reader. Mutation-tested: restoring the old resolution fails both tests
- Deliberately NOT changed: the completion notices in `daemon_tasks_polling`, `pubsub_completion_handler` and `stale_task_detector` resolve the same way, but a completion is an agent's own durable record rather than a request for a decision — routing those to a human inbox would fill it with output. The rejection-feedback notice in `approval_processor` likewise has no consumer once its agent is gone, and a permanent rejection needs none


### Fixed — `ailang lock` stored path dependencies as absolute paths (core backlog 2026-09-15)

- 43 committed `ailang.lock` files in ailang-packages named `/Users/<someone>/…`, so every other machine's `check --package` on a package with a path dependency failed with "package directory not found" (the Daneel eparse outage). Path deps are now stored relative to the lock's directory (`portablePathDep`); the loader already joined relative paths, and `ValidateContentHashesFrom(dir)` resolves against the lock too. Pinned by `TestLockFile_PathDepsAreRelativeAndPortable`, which moves the whole checkout and re-validates; the absolute-path mutant fails it.

### Fixed — a rejection was blocked by the guard that protects approvals

- `checkRegistryCanDispatch` refuses an approval whose handoffs this process could not fire, because the registry is the only source of `trigger_on_complete` and an inert "approved" is irreversible. It was applied to **both** actions, which made the one case that most needs rejecting the one case that could not be: a rejection fires no handoffs, so an unresolvable agent costs nothing. Measured 2026-09-17 on `task-c063b6d2`, whose agent `daneel-design-ailang` was deleted in the 12 Sept revert — its approval had been pending seven days for a design doc already merged by PR #1138, and the refusal's own advice (point `$AILANG_CONFIG` at the plane's config) could not help, because that agent exists in no registry anywhere. The error even read "refusing to **approve**" for a reject, which is the tell that the action was never considered. Rejection is now exempt; approval of an unresolvable agent is still refused, and both halves are asserted (mutation-tested: removing the exemption fails the test)

## [v0.39.5] - 2026-09-17

### Fixed — `$AILANG_CONFIG` was read as a registry even when it declares no agents (broke every Daneel dispatch since v0.2.5)

- Reported by Daneel 2026-09-17. The daneel user has no `~/.ailang/config.yaml`, so every `messages send` sets `$AILANG_CONFIG` to a **pubsub-only** file — a send publishes its notification only when the sender's config has a pubsub section. `resolveInboxRegistry` then accepted that file as the agent registry. Daneel's dispatch programs run `messages inboxes --all --json` as a pre-flight and refuse unless the registry label starts with `gs://`, so every design dispatch and every ask from 05:17Z was refused, and two of Mark's morning design requests sat deferred for seven hours.
- The mechanism is worse than "an empty registry", and measured rather than assumed: a file with no `coordinator:` section does not yield zero agents, it yields `DefaultCoordinatorConfig()` — a registry holding exactly one agent, `coordinator`. Eight commands share this resolver (inboxes, health, prs, approvals, the send guard, pipeline, lint, agents), so without Daneel's own guard `messages health` would have called every other inbox a config gap and the send guard would have judged routing against a fleet that does not exist.
- `LoadAgentRegistryFromDeclared` returns the registry **and whether the file declared one**; `loadSectionFrom` always knew, the fact was just dropped. `$AILANG_CONFIG` that declares nothing now falls through to the plane's own registry with one line on stderr saying why; an explicit `--registry <path>` that declares nothing is a hard error rather than a silent built-in default.
- New spelling, which Daneel asked for by name: **`--registry cloud`** resolves the plane's own registry regardless of the environment. Verified against Daneel's exact setup — with the pubsub-only file as `$AILANG_CONFIG`, `messages inboxes --json` now answers `gs://ailang-multivac-ailang-config/config.yaml (the plane's own registry)` and the warning goes to stderr, leaving stdout a clean document. The `tools/ailang-messages-plane` shim in the daneel repo (daneel#79) can go


## [v0.39.4] - 2026-09-17

### Changed — a package inbox defaults to the `ailang_only` lane (Mark, 2026-09-17)

- D6 set the fleet default to `full` — "only a deployment that opts in narrows". Mark reversed it for package inboxes: *"lets move to ailang only executors for all the ailang package inboxes at least, they will have it by default."* `GetEffectiveToolPolicy()` now returns `ailang_only` for any agent on a `pkg:` inbox that declares nothing; everything else still defaults to `full`, and an explicit declaration wins in both directions. The direction is what makes a default acceptable here: it REMOVES authority, so a wrong default costs a task that refuses and says why, where `artifact_patterns`' `**/*` — the same mechanism pointed the other way — silently bounds nothing.
- Because a default can now put an agent on the lane, two things fail closed rather than quietly:
  - **dispatch** refuses a task whose effective policy is not `full` and whose `PolicyTOML` is empty. Previously only an *unreadable* `policy_path` was caught; an *absent* one dispatched a job where `ailang_run` default-denies every program, and the model spent its turns discovering it could not execute anything. The log line names the file to add.
  - **`coordinator lint`** gains `lane-has-policy` (7 rules), which reads the EFFECTIVE policy and says whether it was declared or defaulted — otherwise the operator looks for a `tool_policy` line that is not in the file.
- `coordinator agents --json` now carries `tool_policy` and `policy_path` in both `declared` and `effective`. The text view always printed them; the JSON carried neither, so a fleet audit done by machine could not see the lane at all — measured while auditing this very rollout.
- Fleet after the config change (`ailang-multivac` prod): **41 agents, 29 package inboxes, 29 on `ailang_only`, 0 missing a policy**. The sprint chain, triage, the design-doc agents, motoko and website-builder keep their shell — skills, `make` and `gh` are outside the lane

### Fixed — scratch probes reached the approval card but never the branch

- The wrapper's commit excludes `.ailang-scratch/` by pathspec (v0.39.1), but artifact discovery reports staged + unstaged + **untracked** files, so probe programs the branch can never carry were listed as the task's changes. Measured 2026-09-17 on `task-e0d87876`, the first `pkg-sunholo-docparse` run: the card named six files — `Dockerfile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and four `.ailang-scratch/*.ail` — while PR #188 carried exactly the two real ones. With the card/branch check now live, approving that task would have **refused a correct merge on incorrect evidence**. `DiscoverChangedFiles` drops scratch paths segment-wise (`docs/notes.ailang-scratch.md` survives), and `ScratchDir` in the wrapper is now `coordinator.ScratchDirName` so the commit pathspec and the changed-file list cannot drift again. Mutation-tested through the real path: deleting the filter call fails `TestDiscoverChangedFiles_ExcludesUntrackedScratch` — asserting on the helper alone did not, which is the hole that let the two sides disagree

### Fixed — the coordinator guide described a fleet that no longer exists

- `docs/docs/guides/coordinator.md` said `provider` was `"claude" or "gemini"` (gemini was retired in v0.22.0) and `model` was "a Claude model override". The production fleet is **41 agents — 38 `pi`, 2 `codex`, 1 `motoko`, no `claude`**, most pinning an OpenRouter id, and six now on the `ailang_only` lane. The provider row lists the six registered executors (read off the `executor.GlobalFactory().Register` call sites, not memory); the field table gains `tool_policy`, `policy_path`, `executor_variant`, `artifact_patterns`, `merge_branch` and `subdirectory`, with the warning that an unset `artifact_patterns` means `**/*` and bounds nothing; and a dated paragraph says what the fleet runs and that the cloud registry is `ailang-multivac/config/config.cloud.yaml`, not `~/.ailang/config.yaml`

### Fixed — trace rendering is bounded at the render site, and the retention cap counts real bytes (M-V1-MEMORY-FOOTPRINT M1)

"Logging on" multiplied peak RSS: every trace render site called `String()` on the whole
value and only then cut it to 1 KB. The function-call site had been tier-gated since
M-TRACE-TIER-NOT-ENFORCED; the **effect** site in `internal/effects/ops.go` was not, so a
`readFileResult` of a 20 MB file rendered `Ok(<20 MB>)` per call at the default `standard`
tier, and `deep` on an accumulator recursion rendered the accumulator per call.

- **`eval.ShowBounded(v, maxBytes)`** renders exactly what `truncate(v.String(), max)`
  produced — prefix plus `…(+N bytes elided)` — but stops writing at the budget and only
  counts the rest. Every trace site (`effects/ops.go`, both function sites in
  `eval_operations.go`, the `Rand` moded-effect site, the typed evaluator's `boundedShow`)
  renders through `EffContext.RenderTraceValue`, which also produces the redacted
  descriptor from `eval.RenderedLen` without rendering the payload. `String()` itself is
  unchanged: `show`, `println` and errors render as before.
- **The collector's own bound is idempotent** (`truncateValue`, `redactValue`), anchored at
  the budget offset, so a pre-bounded value passes through `record()` unchanged.
- **`eventSize` is honest**: `unsafe.Sizeof` of the event and payload structs plus a header
  per string. It charged 128 bytes plus contents before, under-counting 4x+.
  **`DefaultMaxRetainedBytes` is 32 MB** (was 256 MB; D-B ratified 2026-09-16). Exporters
  read the observer stream, which retention never touches.
- Record update pre-sizes its field map.

Measured (rig, `/usr/bin/time -l`): cons recursion depth 9,000 — untraced 769 MB, `deep`
2,759 MB before → 814 MB after (1.06x); `readFileResult` x40 of 20 MB at `standard` with
`--emit-trace` — 1.14x untraced before → 1.00x after. Pinned by
`cmd/ailang/memprobe_test.go` (deep-tier RSS ratio ≤ 1.25 at GOGC=100) and, for the effect
site, `TestEffectTraceRenderDoesNotMaterialiseLargeResults` in `internal/effects` — an allocation
count (+8 KB per call with the fix, +8,206 KB without). The RSS form of that check read 1.37x on
the ubuntu runner for the same binary: under GOGC=500 peak RSS is GC timing, not the site. Design: `design_docs/planned/v1_0_0/m-v1-memory-footprint.md`.

### Fixed — `Debug.log` streams on arrival under `--log-level`; serve-api requests log into their own context (M-V1-MEMORY-FOOTPRINT M2)

Every `Debug.log` line of a run was retained until the final flush and only then filtered
by `--log-level`, so a logging loop grew without bound and a below-threshold DEBUG line was
formatted, stored and discarded. serve-api ran every concurrent request against ONE shared
accumulator that whichever request finished first flushed and reset.

- `DebugSink.Attach(ctx)` makes a context write through: lines and failed checks reach the
  sink as they arrive, the level filter runs on arrival, nothing is retained. `ailang run`,
  `--batch` and `serve-api` attach it (D-E, ratified 2026-09-16). Hosts that never attach a
  sink — game engines, WASM, generated Go — keep `Collect()`/`Reset()` unchanged.
- `EffContext.Clone` gives each request a `Fresh()` Debug context on the same sink.
- `Severity` decodes a structured line once instead of `json.Valid` + `json.Unmarshal`.
- The typed evaluator's per-call trace keeps the newest 10,000 entries (`Dropped` counts).

Measured (200k structured DEBUG lines at `--log-level error`, GOGC=100): +64 MB retained
before → +11 MB transient after; 2M lines: 1.8 GB → 180 MB. Pinned by
`TestMemprobeDebugLogDoesNotAccumulate` and `TestServeAPI_ConcurrentRequestsKeepTheirOwnDebugLines`.

### Fixed — FS reads take a cap, uploads are streamed to disk, zip entries are read pre-sized (M-V1-MEMORY-FOOTPRINT M3)

- **`EffEnv.FSMaxBytes`** bounds every FS read (`readFile`, `readFileResult`, `readFileBytes`).
  `--fs-max-bytes` / `AILANG_FS_MAX_BYTES` set it on the CLI (default unbounded, D-C); serve-api
  sets it to its upload cap. The guard is the **post-read** length — a stat is only an early
  exit, since pseudo-files report size 0 and files grow — and an oversize read is
  `E_FS_FILE_TOO_LARGE` naming the size and the cap, never a truncated string. Net has had its
  5 MB cap since it existed; FS had none.
- **One byte-size parser** (`config.ParseByteSize`): `--max-memory`, `--fs-max-bytes` and the
  new registry var accept the same spellings (`256MB`, `8G`, `1.5GiB`, bytes); a negative or
  malformed value is an error.
- **serve-api multipart**: the upload cap is enforced on the body with `http.MaxBytesReader`,
  `ParseMultipartForm` gets a 4 MB in-memory threshold instead of the cap, and a file part
  bound to a string param is `io.Copy`'d to its temp file. Before, a 50 MB upload was resident
  three times (parse buffer, `ReadAll`, temp-file write). Measured: parsing a 48 MB upload
  allocates 16 MB (flat, net/http's own threshold buffer) vs 126 MB.
- `_zip_readEntry` pre-sizes its buffer from the entry header instead of `io.ReadAll` doubling.

### Added — `perf-sweep`: a monthly runtime latency, peak-RSS and alloc/op sweep with a banked diff (M-V1-MEMORY-FOOTPRINT follow-up)

`make perf-sweep` (skill `.claude/skills/perf-sweep`) measures the six latency workloads' p95,
peak RSS of five memory-shaped probes under `benchmarks/memprobes/` (live-data `cons`
recursion and its deep-trace ratio, `foldlE` effect results, 200k filtered `Debug.log` lines,
the quadratic string accumulator) and min B/op of the evaluator and builtin benchmarks, banks
`.ailang/state/perf/<date>.json`, diffs against the last snapshot with a per-row tolerance,
lists workloads over their ledger target and each regression with the command that produced
it, prints the `list_large` top allocators, and exits 2 on a same-machine-class regression.
`make perf-sweep-control` plants a 1.7×-depth live-data probe and must report `WORSE`.
Every RSS row runs at GOGC=100 with tracing off; garbage-dominated shapes are B/op rows
(the copy-on-write map probe swung +34% as RSS with no change and is `BenchmarkMapInsert5K`
now). Replaces the April `perf-reviewer` skill; its principles moved under `resources/`.

### Changed — `std/list` docs stop claiming O(1) prepend; `concat` carries its cost (M-V1-MEMORY-FOOTPRINT follow-up)

The module header said "immutable linked lists with O(1) prepend". Lists are a flat array and
both `::` and `++` copy, so a hand-rolled build loop is O(n²). The header now says so and
points at `map`/`filter`/`foldl`; `concat` documents that `concat(acc, [x])` in a loop is
quadratic in time and memory (measured 5,000 records: 78 s / 1.7 GB vs 0.36 s / 294 MB
prepend-then-reverse), which was the one surviving ask from the 2026-09-09 concat report
after v0.36.0 removed its tracer-caused OOM.

### Added — `--max-memory cgroup`, `ailang doctor memory`, and a Memory guide (M-V1-MEMORY-FOOTPRINT M4)

- **`--max-memory cgroup`** (and `AILANG_MEMLIMIT=<size|cgroup>`) derives Go's soft memory limit
  from the container's cgroup limit × 0.9 (v2 `memory.max`, v1 `memory.limit_in_bytes`; `max`,
  the v1 sentinel and a missing file mean "no limit", said on stderr). **Opt-in** (D-D): nothing
  is applied unless asked. It is best-effort GC tuning, not a hard or per-request bound — the
  design doc records the measurement (772 MB live under a 64 MB limit) and the Go semantics
  (trigger = min(GOGC target, limit target)). Both `run` and `serve-api` take it.
- **`ailang doctor memory`** prints the limit a run would resolve and its source, GOGC, the
  cgroup files, and the other caps.
- **`docs/docs/guides/debugging.md#memory`**: every control in one table, what a limit cannot
  fix (build-by-prepend recursion; per-item bindings in a recursive loop stay live until it
  unwinds — 870 MB vs 212 MB for the same 40 file reads via `foldlE`), and the probe protocol.
- `--max-memory` and `--fs-max-bytes` are in `ailang --help`; `GOMEMLIMIT` and `AILANG_MEMLIMIT`
  are in the registry and the generated env-vars page.

### Added — `ailang messages renotify <id>`: publish the notification for a message already in the store

A send publishes its Pub/Sub notification only when the SENDER's config has a pubsub section;
a sender without one (a LaunchAgent, another user account) writes the message and nothing ever
dispatches it. Measured 2026-09-17: Daneel's first live `daneel ask` sat four hours unread on
`daneel-executor`. Re-sending is not a recovery — the sender's records and every completion
title key on the original id. `renotify` refuses a message that is not unread (it was
dispatched or acked; a second notification runs it twice) unless `--force`.

## [v0.39.3] - 2026-09-16

### Fixed — pi runs carry their transcript, so the completion `summary` is no longer empty (M-DANEEL-AILANG-EXECUTOR M3)

`execute-job` sets `completion.Summary = completionSummary(Result.Transcript)`, and the pi
executor filled `Output` but never `Transcript` — measured 2026-09-16: every pi completion on the
plane carried `summary: ""`. The Daneel executor's ratified return path (D1) is that summary, so
it would have answered nothing. pi now sets `Transcript` (= the assistant text it already
accumulates); the fizzbuzz fixture asserts it. Claude/codex already did this.

## [v0.39.2] - 2026-09-16

### Added — `std/web`: web search and page fetch with `{Net}` only, the key never in the program (M-DANEEL-AILANG-EXECUTOR M2)

`webSearch(query, max) -> Result[list[{title, url, content}], NetError] ! {Net}` and
`webFetch(url) -> Result[{title, content, links}, NetError] ! {Net}`, backed by Ollama's web API
(`POST https://ollama.com/api/web_search` / `web_fetch`) behind a `webBackend` seam so a second
provider (Gemini grounding) can follow without touching the builtins or the module. The Go handler
reads `OLLAMA_API_KEY`; the program never holds it, and a sentinel non-leak test walks every
carrier (values, errors, stringified results, 401/500/malformed/refused paths) with a positive
control proving the key did reach the wire — two key-echoing mutants caught. Requests go through
`buildSecureRequest`, so the policy's `net_allow`, DNS/IP checks and body limit apply unchanged
(measured: no `Net` → `policy_violation`, `Net` without `ollama.com` → `DisallowedHost`).
`max` is 1..10; invalid input is `E_WEB_INVALID_INPUT` before any request. Recorded-fixture tests
(`internal/effects/testdata/ollama_web_*.json`), an opt-in live control (`AILANG_WEB_LIVE_TEST=1`),
`examples/runnable/web_search.ail` (skipped by verify-examples; type-check asserted in Go),
stdlib golden frozen. Verified live: 3 results + a fetched page from a program admitted with
`[IO, Net]` and no `Env`.

### Added — `ailang run --policy` binds the AI provider from the policy (M-DANEEL-AILANG-EXECUTOR M1)

`internal/policy.Policy.AIProvider` existed but `run --policy` never applied it: an `AI`-cap program
under a policy used whatever `--ai` the caller passed, or nothing. Now the policy is the lending
boundary for the AI effect too: `ai_provider` feeds `setupAIHandler` exactly where `--ai`/`--ai-stub`
did (`"stub"` = the offline route); `--ai` and `--ai-stub` are refused by name under `--policy`;
`AI` without `ai_provider` and `ai_provider` without `AI` are refused; the admission line carries
`ai_provider`. Table-driven binary tests, binding mutation-checked.

## [v0.39.1] - 2026-09-16

### Added — `ailang_cli`: the rest of the `ailang` CLI, policy-allowlisted, on the `ailang_only` lane (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION follow-up)

The lane's model reached the binary only through `ailang_check`/`ailang_run`; the first
package-repo run (chain 687d6ebc, 2026-09-16) spent 41 tool calls guessing signatures and
probing layout that `ailang iface` answers in one. New tool `ailang_cli({argv})` in
`ailang-exec.ts`: argv is an array (no shell), cwd is the sandbox root, path arguments must stay
inside it, output capped at 64 KB. The allowlist is operator policy — **`cli_allow`** on the
policy TOML (`internal/policy.Policy.CLIAllow`, `process_allow` syntax: `docs:search`) — and an
absent key means the documented default set. `run`/`test`/`exec`/`repl`/`replay`/`watch`/
`select-best` are refused whatever the list says: execution only goes through `ailang_run`.
`AilangCLI` joins `executor.CanonicalTools` and the `ailang_only` profile
(`--tools …,examples_search,ailang_cli`); the lane prompt names the tool, the allowed
subcommands, and the **package-ceiling rule** (a probe written inside a directory with an
`ailang.toml` inherits its `[effects] max` — the live run hit `effect ceiling violation in
package sunholo/test_pkg` three times before moving its probe out).

**The 94-subcommand audit** (top-level `case` labels in `cmd/ailang/main.go`) behind the
default set:

| verdict | subcommands |
|---|---|
| **allow** (read-only, pure, or writes only the sandbox file it is given) | `check` `ai-check` `iface` `fmt` `test` `docs search` `examples` `builtins` `pkg-docs` `tree` `prompt` `agent-prompt` `devtools-prompt` `policy-check` `axioms` `version` |
| **gate only** (execute programs; refused even if listed) | `run` → `ailang_run`; `exec`, `repl`, `replay`, `watch`, `select-best`. `test` is **allowed**: the runner evaluates pure code only (`pure_cluster.go` refuses effectful dependencies, no `--caps`), so it cannot reach FS/Net/Process |
| **deny** (plane / registry / provider / spend / not needed) | `messages` `msg` `coordinator` `daemon` `mission` `chains` `dashboard` `serve` `serve-api` `server` `lsp` `mcp` `observatory` `trace` `storage` `budget` `access-control` `install` `add` `lock` `publish` `unpublish` `pkg` `search` (registry) `init` `pi` all `eval-*` `export-training` `cascade` `design-quorum` `design-review` `brain` `cache` `micro-rag` `microrag` `urag` `notify-upgrade` `workspaces` `browser-profile` `editor` `debug` `doctor` `models` `compile` `disasm` `ast-edit` `generate-extension-registry` `internal-dump-iface` `sandbox-check` `versions` |

Tests: `cliDecision` (pure) covers the default surface, gate-only-even-when-listed, explicit
list replacing the default (empty refuses all), `cmd:sub` narrowing, and sandbox escape via a
path argument; `docker/test-agent-pi.sh` §8 runs the same decisions inside the image.

### Fixed — the cloud wrapper never commits `.ailang-scratch/`; the lane prompt puts probes there

`ailang-packages#64` (2026-09-16) was a correct PR plus the agent's `scratch/` package, lock and
two probes: the lane says "probes outside the package", the model has no delete tool, and
`execute-job` ran `git add -A`. `artifact_patterns` could not help — the wrapper reads them only
for `branchIsAutoMergeable`, never to bound the commit (a config change built on that premise was
reverted: multivac 507d9e2 → b9bf51a; it also duplicated keys and the prod coordinator refused
the file, fail-closed, still serving the previous revision). `stageForCommit`
(`cmd/ailang/coordinator_cloud_scratch.go`) stages with `:(exclude).ailang-scratch` at any depth
and commits only when something is staged; git-backed test, mutation-checked.

### Fixed — `ailang iface std/<module>` works outside the ailang checkout

`pipeline.BuildCanonicalJSON` read the module path literally, so `ailang iface std/fs` failed
with `cannot read file "std/fs.ail"` from any package repo — exactly where the `ailang_cli`
tool is meant to answer signature questions. It now serves the embedded stdlib when the file is
absent (the loader's own fallback), for `std/` only: an unknown std module or a missing user
module is still a read error. In-repo output is byte-identical (verified).

### Changed — Z3 ships in every agent image; `ai-check` verification is asserted, not assumed

No agent image installed `z3` (only `registry-validator` did), and `ailang ai-check` reports
`verify.available: false` **without failing** when it is missing — so every `ailang_only` run
so far was type-checked only, silently. `docker/Dockerfile.agent-base` installs `z3`
(bookworm), and `docker/test-agent-pi.sh` §7 asserts both the binary and
`ai-check … "available": true` on a probe module, so the silent-false cannot return.

### Changed — the teaching prompt names `std/fs` `walk` and `glob` (M-STD-FS-WALK follow-up)

`prompts/v0.16.6.md` (the one unfrozen head — amended in place, `ailang prompt freeze v0.16.6` still
refuses for lack of banked evidence): the FS capability row and the canonical `import std/fs (...)`
line gain `walk(root)` and `glob(root, suffix)` with their signatures, and the rule that a recursive
`listDir`/`isDir` walker is never hand-rolled. Every `ailang_only`-lane transcript before the stdlib
change wrote one. Both registries (`prompts/`, `cmd/ailang/prompts/`) re-hashed; claim verified
with `ailang check` + `ailang run` against a three-file tree.

## [v0.39.0] - 2026-09-16

### Added — `std/fs` `walk` and `glob`: recursive file listing without a hand-rolled walker (M-STD-FS-WALK)

- `walk(root) -> [string] ! {FS}`: every regular file under `root`, recursively, sorted; paths are
  `"${root}/${name}"` joins so a relative root yields relative paths. `glob(root, suffix)` is `walk`
  filtered by `endsWith` — suffix matching only, not fnmatch (`"*.ail"` matches nothing). Composed in
  AILANG from `listDir`/`isDir`/`isFile` — no Go builtin. Motivation: the first `ailang_only` batch
  (six shell-less tasks, 2026-09-16) hand-rolled the same recursive walker in every task.
- Example `examples/runnable/fs_walk_glob.ail` (manifest-pinned stdout; mutation-checked — a `walk`
  that skips directories turns the gate red). Goldens re-frozen via `make freeze-stdlib`
  (`fs.json` only: `+glob`, `+walk`). The canonical `ailang prompt` std/fs row should list
  `walk`/`glob` at its next version — it is served from a frozen copy and not hand-edited here.

### Added — `examples_search` on the `ailang_only` lane: find a working example before guessing a construct (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION follow-up)

- New pi extension `.pi/extensions/examples-search.ts` registering ONE tool,
  `examples_search({query, limit?})` → `{count, matches:[{path, first_match_line, snippet, effects}]}`:
  case-insensitive substring over `examples/**/*.ail`, `runnable/` first, `archive/`, `bugs/` and
  `expected_fail/` excluded (a search tool that returns a deliberately broken program as "an example"
  is worse than none), absolute paths so `read` works from any cwd. The examples dir is found by
  walking up from cwd to a dir holding both `examples/` and `std/`; outside a checkout the tool
  returns `{count: 0, error}` and never throws. Pure helpers unit-tested (`.examples-search.test.ts`,
  8 tests) and the register path probed with a stub `pi`.
- The `ailang_only` profile is now `read,edit,write,ailang_check,ailang_run,builtins_search,examples_search`
  (`ExamplesSearch` canonical; carried via `-e` like the rest of the set, embedded in
  `internal/executor/pi/profile_assets`, drift-gated by `make verify-pi-assets`). The lane prompt tells
  the model to `examples_search` before writing a construct it is unsure of. Motivation: in the first
  shell-less batch the model could only `read` an example whose path it already knew.

### Changed — the resident runs `ailang_only` by default; the profile carries its extensions (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION M5)

- `docker/resident`: `resident-run` and its JSON allowlist are **deleted**; `RESIDENT_TOOLS` is a profile
  (default `ailang_only`, expanded via `ailang pi tool-profile`); the policy arrives as
  `AILANG_AGENT_POLICY_TOML` (`resident-instance.sh --policy-file`) and `boot.sh` materialises it
  read-only outside the sandbox (D4) — with no `bash` there is no `chmod` back; `/health` reports
  `tool_profile` and `program_policy`. `verify` gains the boundary checks (no bash, `ailang_run` live, a
  shell request runs nothing, an admitted program runs). Acceptance 130/0 in Cloud Build.
- **A profile that names `ailang_run` carries its extensions** (`--no-extensions -e …`, embedded in
  `internal/executor/pi/profile_assets`, drift-gated by `make verify-pi-assets`): on the rig an eval
  workspace is an untrusted temp dir and the global dir deliberately lacks the pair, so the lane had no
  execution route at all. Model-visible tools measured: exactly `read, edit, write, ailang_check, ailang_run`.
- `eval-suite --tool-policy ailang_only --policy-file …`; A/B on OpenRouter minimax-m3 (5 benchmarks):
  bash 5/5 vs `ailang_only` 4/5, the miss a 2-turn model stop, no `policy_violation`; not reportable at
  n=5. Failed/diagnostic rows now keep `executor_version` / `tool_policy` / `policy_digest`.
- Docs: `docs/docs/guides/agent-tool-policy.md`; runbook + charter carry the tool-policy lane boundary.

### Added — `tool_policy` on the agent registry: authority the operator can read (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION M4)

- `AgentConfig.tool_policy` (`full` | `ailang_only` | canonical list; **default `full`**, D6) and
  `policy_path` (the program policy an `ailang_only` agent's `ailang_run` is gated by; forwarded as
  `AILANG_AGENT_POLICY`). `ailang coordinator agents <id>` prints it declared and effective.
- Resolution in the coordinator composes task kind × profile by **intersection**: a question on an
  `ailang_only` agent gets `Read, AilangCheck`. The question-kind list is now per executor — on pi it
  was `Read, Grep, Glob, WebFetch, WebSearch`, none of which pi has, so pi ran the question with **zero
  tools and exit 0**; pi now gets `Read + AilangCheck`, and any unmapped name is an error at
  `buildPiArgs`.
- `executor.ProfileTools` / `IntersectTools` are executor-neutral; `internal/executor/pi/toolnames.go`
  owns pi's spellings. `agent_registry.go` split at the 800-line gate (`agent_registry_effective.go`).

### Added — `ailang_run`: the one execution tool for an `ailang_only` agent (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION M3)

- `.pi/extensions/ailang-exec.ts` (embedded via `ailang pi install`): `ailang_run({path, args_json?})`
  submits a program to `ailang run --policy $AILANG_AGENT_POLICY` and returns
  `{admitted, exit_code, decision, policy_digest, stdout, stderr}`. **Default-deny with a readable
  reason**: with the env unset, or a policy that lies inside its own `fs_sandbox` (D4), the tool
  registers but refuses by name — a missing tool is something a model reaches around; a refusal is not.
- `ailang pi tool-profile <full|ailang_only|Canonical,List>` prints the ONE flag expansion
  (`ailang_only` → `--no-builtin-tools --tools read,edit,write,ailang_check,ailang_run`) that `pi.go` and
  the resident read.
- **D7 landed early** (`internal/executor/pi/toolnames.go`): `Task.AllowedTools` is canonical and
  mapped to pi's names; an unmapped name (`Grep`, `Glob`, `WebFetch`, `WebSearch` — pi has none) is an
  **error naming it** instead of pi's silent zero-tool run. Explicit lists now also emit
  `--no-builtin-tools`, so an allowlist is an allowlist.
- Measured live on the rig (pi 0.85.1, `ollama/glm-5.3-flash:cloud`, bash-free): `write` →
  `ailang_check` → `ailang_run` admitted, exit 0, digest banked; a `! {Net}` program →
  `policy_violation`, `missing_from_policy: ["Net"]`, not executed; env unset → the named refusal.
  `docker/test-agent-pi.sh` §6 proves the gate model-free in the image (18/18).

### Added — `ailang run --policy`: one admission gate for agent-submitted programs (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION M2)

- `ailang run --policy <agent-policy.toml> prog.ail` runs the **same** admission path as `policy-check`
  (`admitProgram`: size cap → typecheck → entry export → cap-subset), then derives `--caps`,
  `--net-allow-domains` and `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX` **from the policy**. The caller is an agent: `--caps`,
  `--no-budgets` and `--allow-env` are refused by name (exit 1); an unknown capability in
  `allowed_caps` is refused against the effect registry; a policy that admits `FS` with no `fs_sandbox`
  is refused; a policy with `[budgets]` is refused because nothing enforces them at run time yet
  (source annotations do) — refused rather than silently unenforced.
- Denial: the decision JSON on stdout, exit 2, **nothing executes**. Admission: one `policy: {…}` JSON
  line on stderr (decision + `policy_digest`) so stdout stays the program's.
- Measured and pinned: a clean-row `main` calling an imported `! {Net}` function is `typecheck_failed`
  before the policy is consulted — transitive effects are enforced by the typechecker even under
  `DryLink`, so no import walk was needed (design doc V14 refuted).
- New `error_category` **`policy_violation`** for the `ailang_only` lane.

### Added — the effective tool policy is banked on every agent-mode row (M-AGENT-AILANG-ONLY-EXECUTION M1)

- `executor.Result.ToolPolicy` (the list actually passed to the CLI, or the `<cli default>` sentinel
  when the caller left `AllowedTools` nil; an empty non-nil list is `--no-tools`) and
  `Result.PolicyDigest` (sha256 of the program policy a run was gated by) → `RunMetrics.tool_policy`
  / `policy_digest`, `omitempty`, **absent ⇒ unmeasured**. `Task.PolicyPath` added. Landed before
  any policy changes so the `ailang_only` lane boundary is visible in the data from day one.


### Fixed — `ailang pi status` no longer reports a workspace-provided suite as MISSING

- On a machine where pi runs inside the AILANG repo (the rig, every mission worktree) the global
  `~/.pi/agent/extensions` copies are **deliberately absent**: they would collide with the repo's own
  `.pi/extensions/` and pi refuses to start (`pi_extension_collision.go`, the cloud path removes them per
  task). Status read that as `12 MISSING` and sent an agent to "fix" the rig. It now reports
  `WORKSPACE` when the cwd ships a byte-identical copy, and `MISSING (workspace copy differs …)` when it
  ships a stale one. Nothing on the rig needed installing.

### Changed — the pi boundaries are on the record (M-PI-HARNESS-UPGRADE M5)

- `docs/internal/harness-upgrade-runbook.md` §pi: the pin lives in four places held equal by a test, so
  an upgrade is a code change + fixture re-capture, never `npm i` alone; a boundary table (rig 08-31 /
  ~09-05, cloud 09-16); the rig's 08-31 step sized after the fact from `os-rolling/v0.34.0` — 26 shared
  benchmarks, none worse, `eval-paired` unresolvable (control 91.9%, zero discordant pairs). Charter
  carries the same note under Rig integration. Rollback to the abandoned package is no longer supported.
- Dev Cloud Build `4a6ac6f6`: every pi image read back from the registry at `@earendil-works@0.85.1`,
  Node `22.23.2`. **Prod rolls on the next release** (tag + promote).

### Changed — the fleet's pi images move to `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@0.85.1` (M-PI-HARNESS-UPGRADE M4)

- `docker/Dockerfile.agent-pi`, `Dockerfile.agent-eval`: the abandoned `@mariozechner` package is
  uninstalled first (both publish a `pi` bin; npm's `EEXIST` refusal once shipped the old pi under a
  green build), the pin is installed, and the build **asserts** `pi --version` equals `PI_VERSION` —
  a wrong version fails the build, it does not warn. `docker/resident/Dockerfile` bumps 0.84.4 → 0.85.1
  and asserts both the version and `--session-id`.
- `TestDockerfilesPinExpectedPiVersion` holds the three Dockerfile ARGs equal to
  `pi.ExpectedPackage/ExpectedVersion` — the seam between the run-time assertion and the build-time
  one, mutation-tested against a skewed pin.
- `docker/Dockerfile.agent-base`: Node is pinned by assertion to `NODE_VERSION=22.23.2` and checked
  against pi's ≥ 22.19.0 floor at build time (D8). Nodesource's deb suffix differs per arch, so the
  pin is an assertion rather than an apt `=version`; a nodesource bump fails the build and is bumped
  deliberately, like `PI_VERSION`.
- `docker/test-agent-pi.sh` + Cloud Build step `test-agent-pi` (**not** `allowFailure`): model-free
  acceptance inside the built image — pin, Node floor, the embedded suite present, and the D7 pair
  measured on 0.85.1: a load-time sentinel in the image-owned global extension dir **executes with no
  trust file**, one planted under `<workspace>/.pi/extensions/` **does not**. The step reads the pin
  from the Dockerfile rather than carrying a third copy. Validated on the rig against 0.85.1
  (12/12; a wrong pin fails 2).
- `tools/pi-extensions/sandbox/index.ts`: value imports renamed to `@earendil-works` — the one real
  runtime break the drift audit found (V15).
- Runbook probe re-run on 0.85.1 from a clean `ailang pi install`: real `tool_execution_start/end
  quota_report` events — and again from the rig's checkout, where the suite loads from the repo's own
  `.pi/extensions/` (byte-identical to the embedded assets, root trusted).

### Changed — the two signals pi 0.84+ added are wired; fixtures are one pair per wire version (M-PI-HARNESS-UPGRADE M3)

- `usage.reasoning` → `Result.ReasonTokens`, with `OutputTokens = usage.output − reasoning` (D5). Verified
  on a live capture that reasoning is inside `output` on the wire (`totalTokens` excludes it), so the
  pi lane no longer double-counts reasoning in cost and `IsReasoningStall` can finally fire on a pi run.
  Absent on 0.73.1 → `ReasonTokens` 0, output untouched.
- `rawStopReason` (the provider's own value) is banked as `ProviderData.pi_raw_stop_reason` and consulted
  for the finish reason **only** when pi's normalised `stopReason` is unrecognised.
- Fixtures: `testdata/v0_73_1/` (the wire the cloud ran until the cutover) and `testdata/v0_85_1/` (the
  pin), both captured live 2026-09-16; the 0.70.2 pair — a version no plane had run for months — is
  deleted. Summation tests patch known non-zero `cacheWrite`/`cost` into the pinned fixture
  (`patchAssistantUsage`) instead of relying on a hand-written stream. Dual-version replay asserts one
  parser produces the same Result shape on both wires.

### Changed — pi drift is recorded or fatal, never silent (M-PI-HARNESS-UPGRADE M2)

- `PiExecutor.HealthCheck` **asserts** the running `pi --version` against the pin
  (`pi.ExpectedPackage@ExpectedVersion` = `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@0.85.1`); a mismatch is an
  error naming both, with the fix in the message. The install hint no longer names the abandoned
  `@mariozechner` package.
- Unrecognised event types are counted into `ProviderData.pi_unknown_events {type: count}`; non-JSON
  lines into `pi_unparsed_lines`. Both absent on a clean stream.
- **Fatal (D4):** an assistant `message_end` with no `usage` fails the run as `FinishWireDrift`
  (`"wire_drift"`) → new `error_category` **`wire_drift`** — the record is wrong, not the model — instead
  of banking zero cost. Never `api_error`.
- pi's internal auto-retry loop (0.84+) is banked as `pi_retries {count, max_attempts, exhausted}`;
  `attempt == maxAttempts` with `success:false` is a named outcome, not a slow success.
- `--thinking max` accepted. `first_attempt_ms` now fires on pi's real lowercase `write`/`edit` tool
  names — the capitalised comparison never matched, so this metric fell through to first-text on every
  pi run to date.
- `scripts/mission_pi_run.sh`: the forensic snapshot is a bounded **rolling window** (1..`SNAP_EVERY`
  records). At 0.84+ `message_update` is a ~300 B delta, so the old single-record snapshot had become
  forensically empty while its header still promised "full forensics".
- Fixtures: `internal/executor/pi/testdata/v0_85_1/{fizzbuzz,tool_use,reasoning}.ndjson` captured live
  on the rig 2026-09-16. The 0.85.1 wire matches the 0.84.4 drift table on every row (design doc V33).

### Added — the harness version is banked on every agent-mode row (M-PI-HARNESS-UPGRADE M1)

- `executor.Result.ExecutorVersion` → `AgentBenchmarkResult.executor_version` → `RunMetrics.executor_version`
  (`omitempty`). The value is what the CLI itself printed for `--version`, as `<cli>@<version>`
  (`pi@0.85.1`) — never what the build believed it installed. **Absent means UNMEASURED**, not "the
  current one": every row banked before this lacks it, and the rig/cloud pi split (0.73.1 vs 0.85.1
  since 2026-08-31) was invisible in the corpus for exactly this reason.
- `executor.VersionProbe` — shared once-per-executor capture. `PiExecutor.HealthCheck` already ran
  `pi --version` and discarded the output; it now caches it and `Version(ctx)` exposes it. codex and
  opencode discard theirs the same way — adopting the probe there is a follow-up, two lines each plus
  result stamping.
- `internal/executor/pi/pi.go` split at the 800-line gate: wire types + parser → `events.go`, argv →
  `args.go`. No behaviour change.


### Changed — `messages health` judges a WINDOW, so old debt stops reading as today's outage

- The banner said `DEGRADED 15` on a plane that had dispatched everything it received for 24 hours: the newest of those fifteen was 35 hours old and the oldest fifteen days, and nothing in the output said so. A counter that cannot reach zero is one readers learn to skip — the exact failure this command was built to prevent (`m-coordinator-execution-trust` V34 makes the same point about the previous version of the same number).
  - `--since` (default `24h`, accepts `7d`/`90m`/`0` for all time) sets what the **verdict** judges. Live faults are counted inside the window; everything older is reported as `BACKLOG n` on its own line, with the three commands that act on it. `--strict` follows the windowed verdict, so the morning report fails on a new fault and not on standing debt.
  - Every breakdown row now carries `new`/`backlog` and its **oldest** age, and rows sort new-first — a single message that stalled an hour ago cannot hide under nine days of backlog.
  - A throughput line: `arrived 69 · work 9 · agent output 60`, plus bounces filed in the window. Zero undelivered and zero arrivals is an idle plane, not a healthy one, and the old output could not tell those apart. The arrivals query deliberately drops `Collapsed` and filters duplicates in memory: `dup_of` + `created_at` together need a Firestore composite index that does not exist, and a health command that needs new infrastructure to answer its own question is not one.
  - `--json` emits the whole judgement (`healthy`, `live_faults`, `backlog`, `arrived`, per-bucket rows) with ANSI stripped, for hooks and agents; the human banner goes to stderr so stdout carries only the document.
  - A closing pointer names the coordinator half — `approvals`, `prs`, `pipeline` — because this command sees the message plane and nothing downstream, and answering "is the pipeline trustworthy" took six commands on 2026-09-16

### Changed — Phase 2 closed: one of each (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S4 close-out)

- Sprint 4 lands the second half of Phase 2 and closes every row of the program's one-of-each table. `os.Getenv` outside `internal/config` is 0 and `forbidigo` keeps it there; every variable the binary reads is documented (`docs/docs/reference/env-vars.md`, generated, plus the `DEBUG_*` knobs and `AILANG_LIVE_NET` in the debugging guide). `make simplicity-metrics` counts registry constants as read names, since reads now go through the registry and a literal census would see almost none.
- Full suite on the merged tree: two packages (`internal/lsp`, `internal/pkg`) failed once under the 128-package parallel run on context deadlines / a 500 ms survival window and passed 8/8 and 2/2 on re-runs under deliberate load; neither was touched by this sprint. Recorded, not hidden.

### Changed — Sprint 4 close-out: the env migration is complete and enforced; D8, D9 (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S4 M4)

- refactor(config): the 95 `os.Getenv`/`os.LookupEnv` sites the M2 census left to the other lanes are migrated — `cmd/ailang/coordinator_cloud.go` (24), `internal/ai/**` (25), `internal/eval_harness/**` (15), `cmd/ailang/eval_*.go` (5), `internal/messaging/embedder.go` (7), `internal/runner/secret_approver.go` (4), `internal/module/**` (4), `internal/loader/loader.go` (2), `internal/coordinator/*` (5), `ai_check.go` (1), plus one the grep census could not see: a function-value reference (`lookup = os.Getenv`) in `internal/mission/dispatch/run.go`. New areas and files: `ai.go` (`OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `LYCEUM_BASE_URL`, `ZAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENROUTER_HTTP_REFERER`/`X_TITLE`/`CATEGORIES`, `AILANG_AI_HTTP_LOG`, the Browserbase names), `ollama.go` (`OLLAMA_HOST` and every `AILANG_OLLAMA_*`; the `HTTP_TIMEOUT_SEC` meaning flip under `V1_STREAM=1` — `0` = no timeout buffered, rejected streaming — stays with the two resolvers in `internal/ai/ollama`, which is why that getter is raw), `embed.go` (`AILANG_EMBED_PROVIDER`, `AILANG_OLLAMA_MODEL`/`ENDPOINT`, the two embed-model names), `eval.go` (`AILANG_BIN`, `AILANG_EVAL_MAX_RSS`, `AILANG_EVAL_TRAPS_CARD`, `AILANG_EVAL_PERSIST_PROMPT`, `AILANG_AGENT_OUTPUT_DELIVERY`, `AILANG_UV`/`AVER`/`MOON`, `CARGO_HOME`, `MOON_HOME`, `OBSERVATORY_ENDPOINT`); 15 more `AILANG_*` of the cloud-job contract on `job.go`; the coordinator's D3 names and `AILANG_APPROVAL_URL`/`AILANG_COORDINATOR_URL`/`AILANG_APPROVAL_TOKEN` on `coordinator.go`; `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY` on `providers.go`; `AILANG_PATH`, `AILANG_STDLIB`, `GOPATH` on `paths.go`. Runtime-chosen names (`ai.EnvVarForProvider`, `${VAR}` auth-header interpolation, the Browserbase key/project names) read through `config.Raw`. `config.Registry` 159 → 222 names, 20 → 24 areas; `docs/docs/reference/env-vars.md` regenerated. Package-level `Env*` constants in `ai`, `gemini`, `coordinator`, `runner`, `eval_harness`, `messaging` are aliases of the config ones, so their tests and error text are unchanged
- Census after: `getenv_outside_config` = **0** (`tools/simplicity_metrics.sh`'s `getenv_all` now counts calls, not the two `ExampleDoc` string literals in `internal/gen/golang/effects.go`; `internal/statedir` stays by rule, name registered)
- chore(lint): **forbidigo** in `.golangci.yml` refuses `os.Getenv`/`os.LookupEnv` outside `internal/config/`, `internal/statedir/`, `internal/testutil/` and `_test.go`, with the `DEBUG_*` reads exempted by a source pattern; `os.Environ` is deliberately not forbidden (it builds child environments and the Env effect). Proved with a canary before the first run: a planted `os.Getenv` and `os.LookupEnv` in `internal/strutil` were flagged, the `DEBUG_` read beside them was not. The exclusion block is rewritten in the v2 shape (`linters.exclusions`): `golangci-lint config verify` shows the v1 keys `issues.exclude-dirs`/`issues.exclude-rules` the file carried were rejected by the v2 schema and ignored by `run`, so none of those rules had been applying. `make lint`: 0 issues
- fix(ollama): `ollama.NewClient` no longer exports `OLLAMA_HOST` for the whole process as a side effect of constructing one client. The resolved endpoint (`OLLAMA_HOST` wins over `WithEndpoint`, else `http://127.0.0.1:11434`) is handed to `ollamaapi.NewClient` explicitly through `parseHost`, a copy of the SDK's `envconfig.Host` normalisation; `host_test.go` pins it against the SDK for 18 endpoint shapes plus the precedence and the closed seam. `internal/loader`'s Windows `%TEMP%`/`%TMP%` branch is dropped — `os.TempDir` already is that lookup. Deliberate small changes: `internal/eval_harness/microrag_mode.go`'s `ResolvedState` reports the trimmed `AILANG_MICRORAG_ENABLED` (what the engine reads); `cmd/ailang/ai_check.go`'s `AILANG_RELAX_MODULES` truthiness is `config.RelaxModules` (the sixth copy of the same rule)
- fix(mission): **D8** — the prod message-plane pin in `internal/mission/dispatch/run.go` is `config.MissionMessagePlaneProject`, a fixed constant that is deliberately NOT a registered `Var` and not overridable by any variable: a dev shell cannot re-route a stage's cross-mission messages. Comment at the site references D8. No behaviour change; the two Go tests assert the constant and the pi integration script still checks the literal independently
- fix(eval): **D9** — standard mode now banks `stdout_ok = runtime_ok && GradeStdout(...)` (`internal/eval_harness/repair.go`), the gate every agent-mode lane already applied, bank-forward from 2026-09-15. A run that crashed after printing the expected bytes, or whose expected stdout is empty, banks `stdout_ok=false`. Nothing re-banked; published rates do not move because the D2 read-side `Passed` predicate already required all three flags — this makes the STORED flag mean the same thing in both modes. `TestD9StandardModeStdoutOkGatedOnRuntime` and `TestD9ExpectedEmptyStdoutDoesNotPassACrash` pin it (both fail with the gate removed); a dated D9 `OSDataNote` joins the D2 note in `eval-publish`'s `notes`. `TestD2DiscordantCount` re-run over the tracked baselines: every per-version row unchanged (v0.4.1 −0.90pp worst, `print_missing_effect` = all 30 discordant rows)
- refactor(executor): `internal/executor/codex/codex.go` and `codex_test.go` (806 lines each on `origin/dev`, `make check-file-sizes` red) are split by pure move — `Capabilities`/`HealthCheck`/`Close`/`getModel`/`requireModel`/`Register`/`init` to `lifecycle.go`, the `TestHealthCheck_*`/`TestRegister_Idempotent` tests to `lifecycle_test.go`
- Leftover, not this milestone's: `internal/messaging/embedder.go` still exports `OLLAMA_HOST` for the embedder's own SDK client (an `os.Setenv`, not a read — the same explicit plumbing as `ollama.NewClient` would close it)

### Changed — the remaining silent fallbacks are deprecated defaults, or errors (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S4 M1)

Ruling D3 applied to the config audit's leftover sites: each default is still served, `config.DeprecatedDefault` prints ONE stderr warning per process naming the variable to set, and `AILANG_STRICT_CONFIG=1` refuses today with `config.ErrDeprecatedDefault` (the v1.0.0 behaviour). Every site has a test for both halves. New variables are documented in `docs/docs/guides/debugging.md`'s env table.

- **Provider `"claude"`** (`coordinator/daemon_tasks_exec_run.go`, `daemon_tasks_budget.go`): one resolver, `Daemon.taskProvider` — agent `provider:` → coordinator `default_provider` → **`AILANG_DEFAULT_PROVIDER`** → deprecated `claude`. The two literal copies mislabelled cost/provider in the observatory and keyed the wrong per-provider spend cap. Strict marks the task failed.
- **Model `"haiku"`** (`eval_harness/agent_runner.go`): **removed outright, no env var.** `DefaultAgentConfig().ClaudeModel` is empty — a non-empty value is treated by the agent path as an explicit `--agent-model` override that skips the `models.yml` lookup, so the default was a silent override of the benchmark's configured model, not a fallback; no production caller used the constructor; an empty value routes through the registry and fails loudly at the point of use (M6 D2(a)). The stale "executor defaults to haiku" comment in `cmd/ailang/coordinator_cloud.go` is corrected.
- **Workspace `"default"`** (`cmd/ailang/coordinator_cloud.go`, `coordinator/daemon_tasks_init.go`): **`AILANG_WORKSPACE`**; constants `coordinator.EnvWorkspace` / `DeprecatedWorkspaceDefault`. Under strict the execute-job refuses BEFORE Pub/Sub exists (no partition to publish a failure to) and the daemon's broadcaster fails to init.
- **Embedding models** (`messaging/embedder.go`): **`AILANG_EMBED_OPENAI_MODEL`**, **`AILANG_EMBED_GEMINI_MODEL`** (env > `embeddings.<provider>.model`). Resolved in `NewEmbedderFromConfig` — the seam every caller reaches and the one that can return an error — with a second warning line saying WHY: the model fixes the vector dimension, and a brain indexed under another model returns silently wrong neighbours. `LoadEmbedConfigFromEnv` now leaves the two models empty (pinned by test) so nothing bypasses the gate.
- **Vertex AI location `"global"`** (`ai/gemini/client.go`): `WithLocation` > **`GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION`** > deprecated `global`; options apply before the fallback so an explicit location never warns. The API-key client no longer carries an implied `global` (unused on that path).
- **Loader stdlib `"."`** (`loader/loader.go`): **an immediate error, no deprecation window.** `resolvePath` was a second implementation of stdlib resolution beside `StdlibResolver`; with `AILANG_STDLIB_PATH` unset it named `./std/<module>.ail` relative to the process cwd. It now delegates `std/` to the same resolver `Load` uses (then the embedded copy) and returns an error when nothing resolves; `CanonicalPath` propagates it. The loader imports nothing new.
- **`AILANG_BIN` → PATH `ailang`** (`eval_harness/agent_validation.go`): the PATH binary is looked up and its RESOLVED path served through `DeprecatedDefault`, so the warning names the binary actually grading (the stale-binary trap); strict refuses to grade with an unpinned one. No `ailang` on PATH is an error either way. (`runner.go`'s `NewAILANGRunner` carries the same fallback and is outside this milestone's files.)
- **Approval timeout `0 → 1h`** (`coordinator/approval_checkpoint.go`): **`AILANG_APPROVAL_TIMEOUT`** (a Go duration; set-but-unparseable is an error, never `1h`). The strict refusal is deferred into `RequestApproval` so only a request that NEEDS the default is rejected; the store-backed variant's duplicate of the seam now shares `applyDefaultTimeout`.
- **Spend cap that disappears** (`coordinator/daemon_tasks_budget.go`): every path that ran a task uncapped — an unreadable `budgets:` config, zero limits at both levels, a failed spend lookup — logs why and is the deprecated "unlimited" default unless **`AILANG_BUDGET_UNLIMITED=1`** acknowledges it; strict refuses (task marked failed) rather than running unlimited.
- **Secret approver on the shared plane without an endpoint** (`runner/secret_approver.go`, the M-SECRET-REMOTE-APPROVAL-WIRING M2 note): still un-gated with a warning naming **`AILANG_APPROVAL_URL`**; under strict a denying `deferredPlaneError` approver is installed, so every `secret()` fails with `config.ErrDeprecatedDefault` while programs that never call it are untouched.
- **From S3 M2 — cost integrity:** `executor/codex` and `executor/managed_agents` bank `CostProvenanceUnknown` (not the lane's `metered`/`list-price-equivalent`) when the resolved `CostModel` is `Unpriced` — a `$0` with a billed provenance was a fabricated free run; fixture tests carry a priced positive control. The observatory OTLP receiver's two token-pricing sites call `PriceTokens` and stamp **`ailang.cost.unpriced=true`** (+ `_reason`) on the span before storing `cost_usd=0`. `eval_harness/agent_runner_multi.go` and `mission/dispatch/run.go` build `Task.Pricing` through `executor.CostModelFromPricing` — both hand copies had dropped `CacheWriteCost`, so the banked cost and the budget that spared the run disagreed on every cache-writing stage.

The last process-group copies outside `internal/proctree` are gone

Output is unchanged at every site: `eval_analysis`'s copy had strutil's own `max-3 + "..."` policy (display columns of 25–38), so the arguments carry over; `eval_analyzer`'s copy kept N chars **then** appended `"..."` (N+3 wide), so its 16 sites — LLM-prompt and design-doc payload caps of 200–8000 — now pass `N+3` literally (`strutil.Truncate(code, 1000+3)`) with the translation noted at the first site in each file. `eval_analysis/dashboard_io.go`'s `writeJSONAtomic` is left as is: its name already says file-and-atomic, which is not what `httpjson.Write` says
- refactor(strutil): the two `Truncate` delegates S3 M5 left as names — `display.Truncate` and `telemetry.Truncate` (both a4b9903e2) — are deleted, and their 40 callers (`internal/ai/{anthropic,gemini,ollama,openai,openrouter,configdriven}`, `internal/executor/{claude,codex,pi,opencode,motoko,managed_agents}`, `internal/pipeline`, `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark*.go`, `display`'s own JSON/first-line/output helpers, `telemetry`'s source-snippet helper) call `strutil.Truncate` directly: import + call, nothing else in those files. Their delegate tests go with them (`strutil` carries the policy tests). `cmd/ailang/exec.go`'s dry-run line calls `strutil.Truncate` too. `dup_symbols.sh 2` before: `3 Truncate internal/display internal/strutil internal/telemetry`, `2 truncate internal/eval_analysis internal/eval_analyzer`, `2 sortedKeys cmd/ailang internal/eval_analyzer`; after: none of the three rows — what remains are honest different names (`truncateURL`, `truncateOutput`, `truncateIfNeeded`, `truncateID`, ×2 each, other owners)
- refactor(strutil): `cmd/ailang/messages_util.go`'s `truncateString` (3b7d9832d) is deleted. The S3 note said `exec.go` was its last caller; five more were found (`messages_send`, `observatory_sync_chat`, `observatory_hierarchy_print` ×2, `trace_local`) — import + call at each, in its own commit because those files sit outside the eval lane
- refactor(eval): `tools/gen-anchor` and `tools/direction-fit` build their ELO trials from `r.Passed()` instead of an inline `CompileOk && RuntimeOk && StdoutOk` — the same predicate, now spelled once (D2)
- refactor(eval): `tools/os-release-snapshot.sh` copies `notes` (the dated `OSDataNote` caveats `eval-publish` writes into `os/latest.json`) into each `os/history.json` entry, so the D2 boundary note survives into the longitudinal series next to the numbers it qualifies (`cd … || exit 1` for SC2164 while there). `benchmarks/events.yml` gains `kind: taxonomy` at `v0.38.8`, label `"D2: pass = compile∧runtime∧stdout (−0.12pp over history)"` — loads through `LoadSuiteEvents`. **Tick note:** D2's reader (0fbbcb9d2) was committed after the `v0.38.9` tag, on the day both releases shipped; D2 is read-side, so every dashboard published from here recomputes the whole series — the tick means "series published from v0.38.8 on use D2", which the yml comment says. Move it if a later release is judged the truer boundary
- `cmd/ailang/eval_cache_warmup.go`'s warm-up comment no longer names the deleted `getAPIKeyForProvider` (3da415336); the credential resolves in `factory.New` via `newProviderAdapter`
- refactor(eval): `eval_harness/prompt_frozen.go`'s `FrozenHashMismatchError`/`MutableHashMismatchError`/`FrozenUnenforceableHashError` (ed5600da6) are deleted — zero callers since S3 M4 moved the teaching text into `prompt.verifyHash`; `FrozenMarker`/`IsHexSHA256` stay for `prompt_freeze_core.go`. `error_categorizer.go`'s `isQuotaExhaustion` (6f0dfcebc) is deleted: the categoriser calls `ai.IsQuotaExhausted`, the same predicate `ai.ShouldRetry` already consulted, so `isRetryableError` is `ai.ShouldRetry` alone — one predicate, no second list to drift. `TestTwoClassesOf429BankDifferently` pins both instruments on one table: spent-quota 429s (Ollama session/weekly, OpenAI `insufficient_quota`) bank `quota_exhausted` and are not retried; throttle 429s (bare, `rate_limit_error`, prose) bank `rate_limit` and are retried
- fix(ai-check): `ailang ai-check` runs THE verification loop — `smt.Verify`, the one `ailang verify` runs — instead of a 230-line private copy (`runVerification`, 8bd64968c's leftover) that had drifted in three measured ways. **Shared now:** (1) `ImportedPrograms` reach the encoder, so cross-module callees resolve — `cross_module_functions.ail` went from `ai-check` = 3 errors / exit 1 (`unknown constant double`) to 3 verified / exit 0, what `verify` always said; (2) a per-function `@verify(depth: N)` overrides the global depth — `fibonacci` now reports `bounded_depth: 5`, not the flag's 2. **Deliberate difference, kept:** (3) an unsupported construct (a list-pattern match, #757) stays a verifier ERROR in ai-check (`verify.errors: 1`, exit 1) where `verify` skips it — via the new `smt.VerifyOptions.UnsupportedConstructIsError`; the exit-code contract has always surfaced it that way and the harness banks `verify_errors` from it, so flipping it is a bank-forward ruling.
