Also unchanged: ai-check's `results` carry only contract-bearing functions (`verify`'s `uncontracted` rows are projected out — a fifth status value in a machine contract is announced, not slipped in). `ai_check_exit_test.go` pins all three plus the option set and the projection (`TestAICheck_CrossModuleCalleesAreShared`, `TestAICheck_PerFunctionDepthOverrideIsShared`, `TestAICheck_UnsupportedConstructIsAnError`, `TestAICheckVerifyOptions_Contract`, `TestAIVerifySectionFromReport_Projection`); flipping the option red-lights two of them

### Changed — Sprint 3 leftovers: storage, prompt, handoff, observatory (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S4 M3B)

- refactor(simhash): the last two private copies are gone — `platform/sharedmem/sqlite.go`'s `hammingDistance64`/`cosineSimilarityF32` and `storage/firestore/messaging_search.go`'s `simhashSimilarity` now call the `internal/simhash` leaf. One deliberate semantic change: sharedmem's cosine compared the SHORTER prefix of two vectors of unequal length (2ad1b8f2d), so a 2-dim embedding from one model scored 1.0 against a 3-dim one from another — a silent search corruption, not a need; `simhash.Cosine` scores a dimension mismatch 0 and `TestSearchByEmbedding_DimensionMismatchScoresZero` pins that through the public search API. Census: Hamming ×1, cosine ×1.
- refactor(mapval): `internal/mapval` (stdlib leaf; `String`/`Bool`/`Int`/`Int64`/`Float`/`Strings`) is the one typed reader of a `map[string]interface{}` however it was decoded — `int`, `int32`, `int64` (Firestore), `float32`/`float64` (encoding/json), `json.Number` (UseNumber). The Firestore converter's private `getString`/`getInt`/`getInt64`/`getFloat64`/`getBool`/`getStringSlice` and the server bridge's `getString`/`getInt`/`getFloat`/`getBool` (no `json.Number`/`int32` cases) are deleted; 463 call sites across `internal/storage/firestore` and 19 in `server/ailang_bridge.go` read through the leaf. Not touched (other owners): `cmd/ailang/dashboard.go`'s S3-fixed copy and `cmd/ailang/cache.go:440 getInt64`, which still reads only `float64` — a Firestore/UseNumber map reads 0 there.
- refactor(prompt): `internal/agentprompt` and `internal/devtoolsprompt` (thin var-aliases since S3 M4) are deleted; `cmd/ailang/{agent_prompt,devtools_prompt,eval_suite_agent}.go` call `prompt.Agent.*`/`prompt.DevTools.*` and `main.go` calls `prompt.SetEmbeddedFS` once (the other two calls were the same function). Verified by building both binaries and diffing `ailang agent-prompt` and `ailang devtools-prompt` plain/`--list`/`--info`/`--compact`: stdout byte-identical for all eight, exit codes identical, stderr identical apart from the observatory retention log lines `main()` emits before either command runs. `make check-prompt-freeze` green.
- refactor(repo): `internal/repo` (stdlib leaf) holds the one project-root walk, `repo.FindRoot(markers...)`; `internal/module` (core) and `internal/prompt` (language root) import neither each other nor statedir's per-user concerns, so a new leaf was the only place both could reach. Each keeps its own marker policy (module: `go.mod`/`.git`/`ailang.yaml`/`.ailang`; prompt: `go.mod`/`.git`/`prompts`); the no-marker fallback is the working directory for both, where the prompt copy returned `"."` and the module copy the absolute cwd. Language closure 44 → 45 (the leaf itself; `ARCHITECTURE.md` regenerated).
- refactor(coordinator): **one handoff sender.** `handoff{Source, Target, Task, Artifacts, IssueNumber}.inboxMessage()` is the one envelope — `FromAgent` coordinator, `ToInbox`, `MessageType` `InboxTypeHandoff`, `Title` `handoffTitle` (prefix once), `Payload` `handoffContent` (names the artifact), `CorrelationID`/`ParentTaskID` = task id, `ChainID`, `Status` unread — and `handoffSender{msgStore, notify}` delivers it from both the daemon's auto-approve path (`Daemon.sendHandoffMessage`, notifying through the daemon publisher) and the approval path (`sendAgentHandoffMessage`, notifying through `notifyInboxMessage` and reporting `errHandoffNotDispatched`). Deleted: `Daemon.deliverHandoffToInbox`, the daemon's own body (which quoted raw executor output and stacked `Handoff: ` prefixes), and the thread-trail `CreateMessage` written with an EMPTY thread id — SQLite rejects it (`thread not found: `), so it had logged a warning and recorded nothing on every auto handoff since 775028c1e, and its metadata keys (`handoff_source`, `source_agent`, `target_agent`, `session_id`) had no reader in `internal/`, `cmd/` or `ui/`. `TestHandoffSender_AutoAndApprovalPathsProduceTheSameEnvelope` drives both real entry points against separate stores and asserts the rows are equal field for field; `pipeline_stage_crossing_test` and `server/handlers_coordinator_approval_test` stay green. Still two more producers, other owners: `finalizer.applyHandoff` (`task_finalize_approval.go`, shares `handoffContent`/`handoffTitle` but sets no `CorrelationID`) and `triggerHandoffsFromApprovalRecord` (`approval_processor.go`, its own wording with a 500-char truncation).

### Schema — coordinator.db data migrations; observatory v21 (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S4 M3B)

- **coordinator.db** gains `PRAGMA user_version`-keyed data migrations (`store_sqlite_schema.go`; the additive `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`/ignored `ALTER TABLE` steps stay unversioned). **v1 re-indexes `tasks.fingerprint`** for rows inside `DedupWindow` (24h) from `tasks.content`, writing only where the stored value differs from `simhash.Hash` — the retired ASCII-only variant (5731a1f38) disagrees for content holding a non-ASCII rune or a one-character token (`a`, `I`, `1`) and agrees otherwise, so ASCII multi-character-word rows are untouched and rows outside the window are not read. The test writes rows with a copy of the retired variant to prove both directions and that a recomputed row now suppresses a survivor-hashed duplicate. An older binary sharing the file still reads it (the migration rewrites values, never a column's meaning). **Firestore: no re-index, by decision** — a fingerprint only matters while its row is inside the 24h window, so the only rows a re-index could touch are those written in the day before the survivor first ran and they age out on their own within a day; the failure inside that day is a missed suppression (one extra run), never a false one; a once-per-project run would need a `_meta` marker plus a start-up hook the daemon lacks. Documented at `FindDuplicateTask` in `coordinator_transitions.go`.
- **observatory v21**: `chat_messages` and `chain_stages` gain `cache_read_tokens`/`cache_creation_tokens` (same names and default as `spans`), so `ailang chains import-motoko` stores the cache tokens S3 M5 could only report — per assistant turn and summed on the stage. `ChainStage`, `ChatMessage` and `AgentStatsResult` carry them; the SQLite stage/chat readers scan them (`COALESCE 0` for older rows); `GetChainStatsByAgent`, the rollup behind `ailang chains stats`, sums them (the table in `cmd/ailang/chains_stats.go` does not print the new totals yet — other owner). Firestore maps the fields on stages and chat messages; no cloud migration is needed since the fields are new and absent reads as 0. `CurrentSchemaVersion` 20 → 21.

### Changed — every operator env var has one reader and a generated reference (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S4 M2)

- refactor(config): `internal/config` now owns every environment read outside the DEBUG_* knobs, `internal/testutil` and the stdlib-only leaf `internal/statedir`: one file per area (`compiler.go`, `paths.go`, `trace.go`, `telemetry.go`, `coordinator.go`, `job.go`, `server.go`, `executor.go`, `providers.go`, `mission.go`, `rig.go`, `pubsub.go`, `pkgregistry.go`, `models.go`, `mcp.go`, `microrag.go`, `apiserver.go`, `notify.go`), each variable an `Env*` constant plus a `Var{Name, Default, Where, Doc}` in `config.Registry` (159 entries), and each getter with a default reading it from the Registry so the reference cannot drift. The precedence flag > env > file > default is written once in `doc.go`. `make docs-env` (`tools/gen-env-docs`) renders `docs/docs/reference/env-vars.md` from the Registry (in the reference sidebar; `env_vars_documented_pct` is now by construction). `TestEveryGetenvNameIsRegistered` fails on an unregistered `Env*` constant, an entry with no constant, or a name read as a string literal. `config.Raw` is the one escape hatch for names chosen at runtime (`--api-key-env`, a provider's declared credential variable, the iterated OTLP endpoint keys).
- Census: `os.Getenv`/`os.LookupEnv` sites outside config/testutil/DEBUG_* 301 → 91. The 88 that remain are in files owned by the other Sprint-4 lanes (`cmd/ailang/coordinator_cloud.go` 24, `internal/ai/**` 25, `internal/eval_harness/**` 15, `internal/messaging/embedder.go` 5, `cmd/ailang/eval_*.go` 5, `internal/runner/secret_approver.go` 4, `internal/loader/loader.go` 3, `internal/module/**` 4, `internal/coordinator/daemon_tasks_init.go` 2, `ai_check.go` 1, ...) and are listed in the sprint's close-out; the other 3 are `internal/statedir` (a leaf below config, name registered) and two `ExampleDoc` string literals in `internal/gen/golang/effects.go` that are not reads. The forbidigo rule waits for the close-out so `make lint` stays green while those lanes land.
- Behaviour changes, all deliberate: `AILANG_MESSAGES_PROJECT` is trimmed everywhere (one of its two readers already did); `USER` is trimmed in `browser_profile` and `coordinator approvals` (the authority label already did); the two raw `COORDINATOR_MODE == "cloud"` compares in `daemon_http.go` and `coordinator_lifecycle.go` use the plane-validated `coordinator.IsCloudMode`, so a mode that contradicts the plane — which the daemon refuses to start on anyway — no longer registers the Pub/Sub push routes or builds the Cloud Run dispatcher; the five copies of the `AILANG_RELAX_MODULES` truthiness switch (check, compile, verify, run, runner) are `flag || config.RelaxModules()`. Defaults that lived beside their reader moved into config with the old constant aliasing it: `DefaultTopicPrefix`, `DefaultRigSharedDir`, `DefaultRigLockStaleMin` (360), `DefaultModelsPublishedDir`, `DefaultRegistryURL`, `DefaultRegistryValidatorURL`, `DefaultRegistryObject`, `DefaultMicroRAGUserPromptFloor`; `apiserver`'s rate-limit defaults and `otel`'s `getEnv` helper (and its test) go. Variables a parent sets for a child (`os.Setenv` in `embed`, `smoke-motoko`; `cmd.Env` in the executors) are not reads and are unchanged — the rig's `launchctl setenv` path sees exactly what it saw.
- fix(test): `TestSendViaHTTP_PostsCorrectShape` clears `COORDINATOR_API_KEY` and `HOME` — on a rig it discovered the real key through the rendered plist and printed the Authorization header in its failure message. It reports a byte count now.

### Changed — Sprint 3 close-out: one of each, part 1 (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M6)

- A shell that still exports a retired plane selector (`AILANG_MESSAGES_STORE=gcp`, the recipe every session used until today) no longer breaks `ailang run`: the plane-resolution error is deferred into the secret approver, so it denies the first `secret()` with the exact error and leaves programs that never touch a secret alone. Ops commands still refuse the retired name outright. `eval-*`'s "ignores AILANG_CHAINS_READ" warning is gone — a second, softer route to a hard error.
- `make simplicity-metrics`: `closure_internal_packages` splits into `closure_nonleaf_packages` (gated, 30 ≤ 36) and `closure_leaf_packages` (14) — a leaf adds code, not coupling, and every consolidation into one was reading as growth; `backend_switches` counts the one resolver plus any package that still reads a selector itself (1).
- Headline numbers vs the 2026-09-15 snapshot: backend switches 6 → 1, duplicate symbol names 32 → 17, `os.Getenv` sites outside config 340 → 300. Accepted, named regressions: `instruction_surface_bytes` +307 B and `tracked_files` +37 (Phase 4), `binary_internal_packages` +5 (new leaf packages, ungated).
- **Operator step before the next rig coordinator restart** (from M5's fail-closed auth): run `tools/launchd/install_coordinator.sh` so the plist carries `COORDINATOR_API_KEY`; the installed plist has none today. Cloud Run already binds it from Secret Manager.
- Sprint 4 plan (`design_docs/planned/m-v1-simplification-s4-sprint-plan.md`) carries every leftover the five agents reported, and the two items that need a ruling.

### Changed — one process-group recipe, and it is a leaf (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M5)

- refactor(proctree): `internal/proctree` (stdlib + syscall only, `TestProctreeIsALeaf` with a positive control) replaces `internal/executor/proctree` and the three byte-equivalent `setProcessGroup`/`killProcessGroup` copies in `executor/motoko` (5ba1a6d51), `internal/smt` (9253ec8a8) and `internal/pkg` (096939f6a). `smt` and `pkg` are language-core packages and could never import the executor copy — that boundary is why the copies existed. Every executor (`claude`, `codex`, `pi`, `opencode`, `motoko`), `mission/iteration`, the Z3 runner and the package smoke/iface subprocesses now call `proctree.Configure`; motoko's port-8080 orphan sweep calls `proctree.KillGroup`/`KillProcess`. One semantic change, deliberate: a group that is already gone (`ESRCH`) is no longer an error — `smt` already treated it so, and `pkg`'s subprocesses gain a bounded `WaitDelay` they did not have. `internal/eval_harness/process_unix.go` (`SetProcessGroup`/`KillProcessGroup` + the RSS sampler) is M1-owned and left for Sprint 4. Census: `setProcessGroup` ×4 → 1, `killProcessGroup` ×4 → 1

### Changed — one SimHash, one Hamming distance, one cosine (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M5)

- refactor(simhash): `internal/simhash` (stdlib leaf) is the single fingerprint and the single pair of similarity measures. **Survivor: the builtins algorithm** (FNV-1a over lower-cased Unicode letter/digit tokens), moved bit for bit — it is the one whose hashes are persisted (`inbox_messages.simhash`, the Firestore `simhash` field, `brain_frames.simhash`, and every `_simhash` builtin result). `TestHashIsBitIdenticalToPersistedValues` pins eight vectors captured from the pre-move implementation at 79da32b60, so no store needs a schema bump or re-index. Deleted: the MD5 SimHash in `docsearch/search.go` (0569a701b — never persisted: `DocFrame.SimHash` is recomputed from content on every load and the JSON caches key on SHA-256, not on it), the ASCII-only FNV variant in `coordinator/analyzer.go` (24f13880e), four Hamming distances (`builtins`, `effects`, `docsearch`, `coordinator`; `bits.OnesCount64` now), and `messaging.CosineSimilarity` + `effects.cosineSimilarity` with its hand-rolled Newton `sqrt` (`simhash.Cosine` in [-1, 1] and `simhash.CosineUnit` on [0, 1]; generic over float32/float64; degenerate inputs still score 0, never 0.5). `simhash.Similarity` replaces the six inline `1 - d/64` formulas.
- **Hash-space note, coordinator:** `tasks.fingerprint` (SQLite and Firestore) was written by the ASCII variant, so rows from before this change live in a different space from rows after it. `FindDuplicateTask` matches by exact equality inside `DedupWindow` (24h): a pre-switch task can fail to suppress a post-switch duplicate for at most that window and can never false-match. A re-index of the stored column needs the M3-owned stores (`coordinator/store_sqlite.go`, `storage/firestore/coordinator_transitions.go`) — listed for Sprint 4. Not touched (other owners): `storage/firestore/messaging_search.go`'s `simhashSimilarity` (M3) and `platform/sharedmem/sqlite.go`'s `hammingDistance64`/`cosineSimilarityF32` (M3; its "shorter-length" cosine is a third semantic to retire then)

### Fixed — the coordinator HTTP API was fail-OPEN when its key was unset (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M5)

- **Security.** `requireAPIKey` in `internal/coordinator/daemon_http.go` (09f4b4941) admitted every request when `COORDINATOR_API_KEY` was unset — "local mode" — so a deployment that lost its secret binding silently exposed `/status`, `/pending`, `/chains/*` and the `/api/messages` ingestion path; and the token check was a plain `!=`, which leaks the key's length and prefix through timing. It now **fails closed** (unset → `401 {"error": "COORDINATOR_API_KEY is not set …"}`, and the daemon logs `SECURITY: … unset` at start) and compares with `crypto/subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`. `TestRequireAPIKey_FailsClosed` proves an unauthenticated request is rejected when the key is unset, when it is unset and a token is sent anyway, and when it is set but the token is wrong/truncated/extended/wrong-scheme. `/health` and the OIDC-guarded `/instances/*` routes are unchanged — the two auth rules now agree on fail-closed. Local installs keep working: `tools/launchd/install_coordinator.sh` renders a `COORDINATOR_API_KEY` into the plist (env → the key already installed, so re-installs do not rotate it → `openssl rand`), and `ailang messages send --requires` reads it from the rendered plist when the shell has none (the daemon's environment is launchd's, not the shell's). **Deploy precondition:** a Cloud Run coordinator whose service has no `COORDINATOR_API_KEY` bound will reject its REST clients after this ships — verify the secret binding in the multivac config before promoting. Documented in `docs/docs/guides/debugging.md`'s env table, `coordinator-workers.md` and `cloud-messaging-integration.md`

### Changed — one HTTP JSON writer, one WebSocket hub, one meaning per path (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M5)

- refactor(httpjson): `internal/httpjson.Write(w, status, v)` / `Error(w, status, msg)` (stdlib leaf) replaces the private `writeJSON` in `coordinator/daemon_http.go` (3b0aafa22), `observatory/api.go` (968b81aa4 — this one called `http.Error` after the status line had gone out, a superfluous WriteHeader), `apiserver/handler.go` (2f2c7b6b0), `server/handlers_secret_approvals.go`'s `writeJSONStatus` (defe892fa) and `apiserver/routes_dispatch.go`'s `writeJSONBody` (0df8af499, inlined — it wrote no header or status). `cmd/ailang/prompt_freeze_core.go`'s `writeJSON` was a buffer helper that only shared the name; it is `appendJSONString`. `eval_analysis/dashboard_io.go`'s `writeJSONAtomic` is a file writer, M1-owned — Sprint 4. Census: `writeJSON` ×4 packages → 0
- refactor(websocket): `internal/observatory/websocket.go` (968b81aa4, 431 LOC) is deleted. It duplicated `internal/websocket/server.go`'s origin/token check character for character and shared one server — and **had no producer**: nothing in the tree ever called its `Broadcast*` methods, so the control-plane UI's `/ws/observatory` socket only ever carried a heartbeat. `/ws/observatory` now mounts the one hub (`internal/websocket.Server`, 13 consumers); the UI's `useObservatoryWs` still connects, ignores event types it does not know, and its "last event" indicator now reflects real hub traffic instead of never firing. `internal/observatory` no longer links `gorilla/websocket`
- `/api/messages` meant thread messages on `internal/server` (port 1957) and inbox messages on the coordinator daemon (port 8765). The thread route — no UI or CLI caller — is now `/api/thread-messages`; the coordinator keeps `/api/messages`, its documented REST contract. `docs/docs/guides/collaboration-hub.md` updated

### Changed — one span classifier, one span-tree linker, one motoko decoder (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M5)

- refactor(observatory): `observatory.ClassifySpanNodeType` (`span_tree.go`) replaces three classifiers that disagreed about what a turn or a tool span was called — exact-match in `store_aggregates_hierarchy.go` (5fe4a3170), prefix-match in `server/handlers_controlplane_task_hierarchy.go` (b538a01b1), substring-match in `cmd/ailang/trace_local.go` (b538a01b1). The union is keyed on the span names the executors actually emit: `*.execute` is an executor (pi, opencode, motoko, codex, managed_agents and cloud_job were "other" to two of the three), `exec.turn`/`exec.turn.N`/`turn.*` a turn, `exec.tool_use*`/`claude_code.tool.*`/`*.tool.*`/`tool.*` a tool. `observatory.LinkSpans` (generic, input-ordered, orphans as roots) replaces four flat-list→tree linkers (`hierarchy.go`, the server handler ×2, `cmd/ailang/observatory_hierarchy.go` ×2 (7bfb1e834), `trace_local.go` — the last walked a map, so `ailang trace local` printed roots in a different order every run). `TestClassifySpanNodeType` and `TestLinkSpansKeepsInputOrderAndOrphansAsRoots` pin both
- refactor(observatory): `ImportMotokoSession` decodes with the executor's `motoko.SessionEvent`/`ParseSessionLine` (now exported, plus typed `ToolCall`/`ToolResult`) instead of its own `motokoEvent` (427505a26), which had no cache-token fields — so an imported chain reported `cache_read=0` for a run the adapter had measured. `MotokoImportResult` gains `CacheReadTokens`/`CacheCreationTokens` and `ailang chains import-motoko` prints them; `chat_messages` has no column for them yet, so they are reported, not stored (`TestImportMotokoSessionKeepsCacheTokens` on the executor's own fixture)
- fix(cmd): `dashboard.go`'s `getInt`/`getFloat` accepted `float64` only, so an `int64` (a Firestore document field) or a `json.Number` read as 0 — a span count of 0 in `ailang dashboard stats` with nothing saying the number was unreadable. Both now read every numeric representation (`TestGetIntReadsEveryNumericRepresentation`). `server/ailang_bridge.go` and `storage/firestore/coordinator_convert.go` carry the same helper (the latter M3-owned) — Sprint 4

### Changed — the small helpers live in one leaf (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M5)

- refactor(strutil): `internal/strutil` (stdlib leaf) holds `Truncate` (display width in runes, `"..."`, `max <= 0` = no cap, no room for the mark = bare cut), `TruncateBytes` (payload size in bytes, rune-safe, caller's mark), `FileExists` and the generic `SortedKeys`. Eleven `truncate`/`truncateString` copies carried three semantics: `s[:max-3]+"..."` (dashboard, coordinator `event_handler` 9a718e136, server `handlers_claudehistory` 577a87fd6, cmd `messages_util`), `s[:max]+"..."` (observatory `api_enrichment` 7bfb1e834 — every call site's argument is now `+3`, so the printed width is unchanged), and byte caps with a mark (daemon's `"…"`, comms' `"…[truncated]"` 086995d5b — both `TruncateBytes`; the daemon copy could split a rune, now it cannot). `TestTruncateMatchesTheOldByteSemanticsForASCII` proves the ASCII outputs are byte-identical; only multi-byte input changes, from invalid UTF-8 to a whole-rune cut. `display.Truncate` and `telemetry.Truncate` are one-line delegations — their callers sit in `internal/ai/**` (M4) and `cmd/ailang/eval_*.go` (M1) — with one policy: `max <= 0` now means no cap in both (telemetry returned `""`), and `max <= 3` returns the bare prefix in both (display returned `"..."`, wider than its own cap). Four `sortStrings` hand-rolled insertion/bubble sorts (`cmd/ailang/doctor.go` b4ae7acd6, `gen/golang` c005f0748, `internal/testing` fd75ce8d4, comms) are `sort.Strings`. `fileExists` ×3 and `sortedKeys` ×2 (`types`, `pkg`) call the leaf; `cmd/ailang/messages_util.go`'s `truncateString` survives as a one-line delegate only because `cmd/ailang/exec.go` (M4) still calls it
- `pubsub.TopicPrefixFromEnv()` is the one reader of `AILANG_TOPIC_PREFIX`; the five two-line fallbacks in files this milestone owns (`pkg_publish`, `server`, `coordinator_cloud`, `coordinator_cloud_executor`, `feedback/publisher`) call it. Every Pub/Sub client construction already resolves its project through `config.CloudProject` (S2) — no precedence change was needed; `cmd/ailang/daemon.go`'s is env-pinned by design (M3)
- **Left for Sprint 4 (other owners):** `truncate` in `eval_analysis/formatter.go` and `eval_analyzer/analyzer.go` (M1; the latter is the `s[:max]+"..."` semantic), `sortedKeys` in `eval_analyzer/analyzer.go` and `cmd/ailang/eval_matrix_sections.go` (M1), `fileExists` in `eval_analysis/loader.go` (M1), `writeJSONAtomic` in `eval_analysis/dashboard_io.go` (M1), `SetProcessGroup`/`KillProcessGroup` in `eval_harness/process_unix.go` (M1), `getInt`/`getString` in `storage/firestore/coordinator_convert.go` (M3) and `server/ailang_bridge.go`, the `AILANG_TOPIC_PREFIX` fallbacks in `coordinator/daemon_tasks_init.go` (M3) and `cmd/ailang/coordinator_lifecycle.go` (M4), and the `display.Truncate`/`telemetry.Truncate` call sites in `internal/ai/**` (M4) and `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark*.go` (M1). Census (`dup_symbols.sh 3`): 32 rows → 25; `setProcessGroup`, `killProcessGroup`, `writeJSON`, `truncate`, `truncateString`, `sortStrings`, `sortedKeys`, `fileExists` all leave the ≥3 list; `Truncate` ×3 enters as the leaf plus its two delegates

## [v0.38.9] - 2026-09-15

### Changed — one pricing source: every token→dollar figure goes through `modelreg` (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M2)

- Four things priced tokens without the registry: `observatory/pricing.go` (a second cwd-relative `models.yml` loader that ignored `AILANG_MODELS_PATH`, the published registry and the embedded floor; a private ~20-entry alias map; cache reads/writes hardcoded at 10%/125% of input for EVERY provider — Anthropic's schedule), `executor.CostModel` fed by literal dollar tables in `claude/cost.go` (Haiku's rates for every Claude model) and `codex/cost.go` (gpt-5-codex's for every codex model), `quorum.estimateCost`, and `observatory/seed.go`'s rate map (still carrying Haiku 3.5's $0.25/$1.25). All four now call `modelreg`: `Pricing.Cost` is the one formula, `ModelsConfig.Resolve` the one name→row lookup (key → api_name → `aliases:` → dated/dotted normalisation → harness wire name, refusing with `ErrAmbiguousModel` when rows sharing a wire name disagree on price), `CostForName` the one entry point. `executor.CostModel` is a thin adapter (`CostModelFor`/`CostModelFromPricing`; `MinimumCharge` deleted — no row or caller ever set it) and bills cache WRITES, which its own arithmetic ignored. Anthropic rows now declare `cache_read_per_1k`/`cache_write_per_1k` (0.1x/1.25x) so their figures are unchanged.
- **Measured.** Prod observatory, every token-bearing `LLM Generation` span 2026-09-01..13 (72 rows: z-ai 55, moonshotai 15, deepseek 2): old generic path **$0.0000**, new **$0.8473** — the generic OTLP path never tried `api_name`, so every OpenRouter slug was stored at `cost_usd=null`. Rig, last 200 token-bearing stages: $1.0934 → $1.1872, the +$0.0937 being two pi stages whose wire names (`openrouter/deepseek/…`, `ollama/…-cloud`) never resolved; the 2 rows still unpriced are a quorum composite name, correctly not a model. Synthetic cache-bearing fixture (21 rows, every priced provider): anthropic **+$0.0000** (4 rows), non-Anthropic +$0.2197 on $1.05 (google +0.0189, openai +0.1507, lyceum +0.0201, zai +0.0151, openrouter +0.0084, ollama-cloud +0.0065) — the 10%/125% hardcode was under-billing everyone else's cache.
- Unpriced is now explicit: `observatory.PriceTokens` returns `ErrUnpriced`, `ResolveCostFromTokens` keeps `resolved=false` (the chain rollup's `unknown_stages` + incomplete-data warning), the float entry points the OTLP receiver still calls log `UNPRICED model …` once per model instead of nothing, executors return an `Unpriced` card rather than another model's rates, and a seed model the registry does not know is an error. Five `cost_classify_test.go` tests that had been `t.Skip`ping on a stale `eval_harness/models.yml` path now run.

### Changed — one eval row, one loader, one pass predicate (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M1, ruling D2)

- **Ruling D2 (Mark, 2026-09-15): a benchmark PASSES iff `compile_ok && runtime_ok && stdout_ok`.** That is now `(*eval_harness.RunMetrics).Passed()` and every rate, leaderboard, ELO fit, A/B join, comparison and sweet-spot read calls it. Two formulas had been in use — the conjunction at ~12 sites and `.StdoutOk` alone at ~86 — and `eval_analysis/paired.go` carried a comment claiming its copy was "the same three-way definition the rest of the pipeline uses". It was not. They differ because standard mode grades stdout without gating on runtime (`repair.go`), while agent mode gates it (`agent_validation.go`); the write side keeps that distinction (`stdout_ok` genuinely means "the output matched" there; `Passed()` is the read-side question).
- **Measured, not assumed** (`TestD2DiscordantCount`, run over every dir under `eval_results/baselines/`, 26,758 valid deduped rows): the two formulas disagree on **31 rows, −0.12pp overall**. 30 of the 31 are the retired `print_missing_effect` benchmark, whose empty expected stdout graded a compile/runtime failure as `stdout_ok=true` (those rows already carried `compile_error`/`runtime_error`). Per version the delta is 0.00 for 30 of 42 dirs including v0.30.0 and v0.32.0; the worst is v0.4.1 at −0.90pp; the only post-v0.5 movement is v0.29.2 at −0.04pp. Nothing is re-banked. The OS rotation summary always used the conjunction, so OS leaderboard/history rows are unaffected; the boundary is carried as a dated `notes` entry inside the JSON `eval-publish` writes (`OSDataNote`, `docs/static/benchmarks/os/latest.json`) — the v0.30.0 cost caveat had lived only in a gitignored `CAVEATS.md`.
- **`eval_analysis.BenchmarkResult` embeds `eval_harness.RunMetrics`** instead of mirroring ~40 of its JSON tags by hand. The copy had drifted: `reason_tokens`, `llm_wall_ms`, `ttft_ms`, `compaction_*`, `verify_*`, `resolved_profile`, `agent_tool_histogram` and the finish-budget fields existed only on the write side, so every analysis read them as zero. `RefusalDetected` is the one read-side annotation left on the outer struct.
- **One loader: `eval_harness.LoadRows(dirs, LoadOptions)`** (validity filter ON by default, per-slot newest-wins dedup, optional `SuiteLayout` walk bound) replaces five: `eval_analysis/loader.go`'s walker, `eval_analyzer/analyzer.go:loadAllMetrics`, `eval_harness/cost_tally.go:resultFilesIn`, `eval_harness/rotation_summary.go`'s glob, and `cmd/ailang/eval_skip_existing.go`'s decoder (`git log -S loadAllMetrics -S resultFilesIn`). **`eval-analyze` therefore stops counting harness crashes as model failures**: its loader had no validity filter, so every `api_error` row (a harness crash by definition) was analysed as a model issue; it now reports `Excluded (not measurements): N` instead. It also walks the `standard/`/`agent/` subdirs it previously never saw, and collapses re-runs. The cost tally keeps every file (a paid-for re-run is still spend) and `--skip-existing` keeps duplicates too (its question is per-file). Absent `validity` still means valid — pre-v0.31.0 `api_error` history is not retroactively quarantined.
- Also fixed on the way: `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go` stored `FirstAttemptOk: metrics.StdoutOk` on the chain assessment instead of `metrics.FirstAttemptOk`.

### Changed — one AI client factory, one error classifier, one HTTP helper, one prompt loader (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M4)

- **A. AI client.** `internal/ai/factory.New(name, opts...)` resolves credential, endpoint and auth lane in one place and replaces the five env-key→`NewClient` switches (`cmd/ailang/{exec,ai_handlers×2,coordinator_lifecycle}.go`, `eval_harness/ai_provider.go`, `mission/quorum/call.go`), which had drifted: `exec.go` read `GEMINI_API_KEY` where the others read `GOOGLE_API_KEY`, pinned its own ollama endpoint, and only two of five honoured `OPENAI_BASE_URL`. The ollama endpoint is decided once, in `ollama.NewClient` (`OLLAMA_HOST`, else `http://127.0.0.1:11434`, IPv4-pinned); `AILANG_OLLAMA_*` semantics are untouched. Google is Vertex ADC first, then `GOOGLE_API_KEY` (or the models.yml `env_var`; `GEMINI_API_KEY` accepted as the alias `ailang exec` used); a pinned `gcp_project` is Vertex-only, so a quorum reviewer can never slide onto an AI-Studio key. Anthropic goes through `ai.ResolveAnthropicCredential` everywhere (metered key, else OAuth), so standard-mode `ailang run --ai claude-*` now works wherever agent mode does. `ai.GetAPIKey` (a second copy of the env-var switch, no callers) is gone
- **429 fix.** `ai.ClassifyError` now routes a `*ProviderError` carrying an HTTP status through `ClassifyHTTPError`; a config-driven provider's 429 used to bypass it and land as `CodeInternal`, never `CodeRateLimit` (`TestGenerate_429ClassifiesAsRateLimit`). The quota-exhaustion carve-out lives in `ai.IsQuotaExhausted`: a 429 whose body says the window is spent (Ollama Cloud session/weekly, `insufficient_quota`; HTTP 402) is `CodeBudgetExhausted` and never retried, on the structured, HTTP and string paths. `ai.ShouldRetry` is the one retry predicate — the eval harness's and the coordinator's substring matchers are wrappers over it; it refuses unknown errors, where `ClassifyError` keeps its retryable default for AILANG-level callers
- **HTTP.** `ai.DoJSON` is the one JSON round trip (build, headers, Do, ReadAll, status, envelope hoist via `ai.ErrorEnvelopeMessage`, Unmarshal, wall time) behind the ten non-streaming client sites; `ai.RecordSpanError` replaces three per-client span recorders and three `asAIError` unwrappers. `openrouter` embeds openai's now-exported `Chat*` wire types instead of redefining them (field order preserved; golden-body tests unchanged). `internal/ai` −400 lines net
- **B. Prompt loader.** `internal/prompt` is parameterised by `prompt.Kind{Syntax,Agent,DevTools}` with `NewLoader(kind, WithManifestFile/WithRoot/WithVerify)`; `WithVerify` is the eval harness's frozen/PLACEHOLDER hash check (D-41c). `internal/agentprompt` and `internal/devtoolsprompt` (byte-for-byte copies differing in one literal) are thin aliases of `prompt.Agent`/`prompt.DevTools`; `eval_harness.PromptLoader` wraps `prompt.Loader` with verification. `ailang prompt|agent-prompt|devtools-prompt` and their `--list`/`--info`/`--version` variants are byte-identical before/after (126 captured surfaces); `make check-prompt-freeze` OK

### Changed — one storage plane switch; four scoped selectors removed (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M3 A)

- **`AILANG_STORAGE=local|gcp|hybrid` is the ONE switch**, with per-store overrides `AILANG_STORAGE_MESSAGING`, `AILANG_STORAGE_COORDINATOR`, `AILANG_STORAGE_OBSERVATORY` (each `local|gcp`). All four are resolved in one place, `config.StoragePlane()`, which returns each store's mode **and its source**; `storage.NewBackendsForSelection` opens store-by-store from it and every command goes through it. Six independent selectors used to disagree silently — `ailang storage status` read only `AILANG_STORAGE` while the inbox could be on Firestore.
- **REMOVED — hard errors for one release, each naming its replacement:** `AILANG_MESSAGES_STORE` → `AILANG_STORAGE_MESSAGING=gcp`; `AILANG_COORDINATOR_REMOTE` → `AILANG_STORAGE_COORDINATOR=gcp`; `AILANG_CHAINS_READ` → `AILANG_STORAGE_OBSERVATORY=gcp`; `AILANG_CHAINS_CLOUD` → `AILANG_STORAGE_OBSERVATORY=gcp`. A value that is now ignored must not look honoured, so `ailang messages …`, `coordinator --remote`, `chains …`, `chains post-iteration`, the daemons and even `ailang run` (via the secret-approver gate) refuse with `AILANG_MESSAGES_STORE was removed in v1.0.0 …; set AILANG_STORAGE_MESSAGING=gcp`. **Update your shell profile and the CLAUDE.md session-start export** — the SessionStart hook now names a retired export in its banner rather than reporting an empty inbox.
- `ailang storage status` prints **one line per store**: `messaging  gcp  (AILANG_STORAGE_MESSAGING)  project <p> (<source>)` / `coordinator  local  (AILANG_STORAGE)  <path>`, plus the coordinator-mode verdict. `hybrid` is reported honestly: every store in SQLite on the shared plane (it never reached BigQuery).
- `COORDINATOR_MODE` is read in `config.CoordinatorMode()` only and **validated against the plane**: `cloud` requires the coordinator and messaging stores in Firestore, else the daemon refuses to start naming what to set (`COORDINATOR_MODE=cloud` with `AILANG_STORAGE` unset used to open SQLite and publish to Pub/Sub about it). It is deliberately NOT derived from the plane: the rig's live coordinator runs `AILANG_STORAGE=gcp` with no `COORDINATOR_MODE` — a local-execution daemon on the shared plane — and deriving cloud from gcp would have flipped it into Cloud Run Jobs dispatch.
- `hybrid` now gets the cloud secret approver it silently lacked; an unresolvable plane there is an error rather than a skipped gate.
- The per-store override now reaches every reader, daemons included (before, the CLI read the scoped selector and the daemons read only `AILANG_STORAGE`). Docs: CLAUDE.md, `docs/docs/guides/agent-messaging.md`, `docs/internal/message-plane-topology.md`, the agent-inbox skill, the mission-control Gate-3 resource, `scripts/hooks/session_start.sh`, the mission and docs-router drivers.

### Changed — one SQLite opener, `internal/sqliteopen` (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M3 B)

- Nine openers become one: `sqliteopen.Open` applies WAL, `busy_timeout=5000`, `foreign_keys=ON` and `synchronous=NORMAL` on **every pooled connection** (through the DSN, not a post-open `Exec` that reached one connection), caps the pool at one writer, creates the directory unless the file must already exist, and pings so "cannot open" fails at the call site. `observatory.db` was opened two ways with different pool caps and foreign-key settings; `NewSQLiteBackendFromPath` now wraps `OpenStore` and a test pins identical PRAGMAs (pool 1, FKs on). `coordinator.db` keeps foreign keys OFF on purpose — the schema declares them but the store never enforced them and violating rows exist; enforcement is a migration, not an opener flag. The five bare `sql.Open` calls in `cmd/ailang` (chains data/diagnostics, eval confidence/saturation) had no WAL or busy timeout and returned nil on error; they open read-only and report the error instead of printing an empty report. `messaging.DatabaseExists` is read-only, so an existence check no longer creates the file it asks about. Dropped: messaging's `PRAGMA temp_store=MEMORY` (not a DSN option).

### Changed — one `~/.ailang/config.yaml` loader, `config.Load` (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S3 M3 C)

- Three loaders and eight re-parses become one: `config.Load()` resolves `AILANG_CONFIG` (else `~/.ailang/config.yaml`) for **every** reader — `messaging.LoadConfig` used to read a hard-coded home path and ignore it — parses once per process per path (re-read when the file changes on disk), and hands sections to callers through `File.Section` with typed errors (`ErrConfigNotFound`, `ErrConfigInvalid`). A file that does not parse is now an error from every loader, never a default. `coordinator.getDefaultRepo` turned every failure into `"sunholo-data/ailang"`; it now returns an error and GitHub sync / label resync log and stop rather than syncing the wrong repository. `config.CloudProject`'s `pubsub.project_id` lookup shares the parse.

### Fixed — the approval card and the branch could describe different changes (approve A, merge B)

- Measured 2026-09-15 on task-c0ca6301: the card said `design_docs/planned/ailang-core-backlog.md | 7 +++++++`; what merged was `design_docs/planned/ailang-core-triage/coordinator-completion-wrong-ref.md` (+11), a file the card never named. Nothing was wrong at the merge — the branch was right throughout. Run 1 created the approval, the task was re-dispatched two minutes later, and run 2 replaced the work **without replacing the card**: `CreateApprovalIfAbsent` reports "already exists", and `ClassifyApprovalCollision` read a PENDING row as a replay on the grounds that "the decision has not been made, so the work it describes is about to be judged either way". The second half of that is false — the card IS what it is judged on. **This was 11 of 11**: every reopened `ailang-core-triage` approval named the shared backlog file while every branch carried a per-report file. Three changes, because one is not enough:
  - `CollisionStaleCard` joins the collision table, and finalisation refreshes a pending card in place (`Store.RefreshPendingApproval`, SQLite + Firestore, guarded on `status = 'pending'` so it can never edit the evidence behind a decision someone already made — that case stays `ReopenApprovalForNewWork`'s).
  - `DecidePR` now takes the card's file list alongside the PR's and **refuses the merge when they disagree**, naming both sides. Sets, not sequences: GitHub and the approval record do not order files the same way, and a check that called that disagreement would refuse every merge, which is how a safety check gets switched off. An approval that recorded no diff cannot be checked, and the merge reason says so rather than implying it passed.
  - `ailang coordinator approvals --refresh-cards [--apply]` repairs rows that are *already* stale — the branch is the authority, the old patch and work id are dropped rather than half-kept, and the card records `card_source: re-derived from PR #N`. The re-derived stat says line counts are unavailable instead of inventing them. Ten prod cards repaired; a second pass reports 0 stale

### Added — a triage row cannot land contradicting its own estimate

- The `ailang-core-triage` rubric is countable (`DIRECT_FIX_MAX_LINES = 2`, `DIRECT_FIX_MAX_FILES = 1`) and was still only text the model reads: a run whose clone **contained** the rubric wrote `Estimate: ~15–30 lines` and `Recommend: direct-fix` in the same file. `internal/coordinator/triage_rubric.go` + `TestTriageRowsInTreeObeyTheRubric` check every row in `design_docs/planned/ailang-core-triage/` against the rubric, with the thresholds **read from the skill document** rather than copied — a checker with its own numbers is the two-implementations fault this pipeline keeps producing, and tuning the skill would otherwise stop tuning the gate. A range reads as its largest end (`~15–30` is 30): a bound that believes the optimistic half is not a bound. The gate runs in `make test`, and the coordinator merges with `gh pr merge --auto` and no `--admin`, so a violating row leaves its PR open instead of landing. The one row already in the tree was corrected to `design-doc` per its own estimate, with the correction stated in the file

### Fixed — `coordinator reopen` could only ever act on this machine

- `ReopenTask` was a method on `*SQLiteStore` that the `Store` interface never mentioned, and `coordinator reopen` opened a hardcoded `~/.ailang/coordinator.db`. So a cancelled CLOUD task could not be reopened at all — and because the command printed no store line, that failed as "task not found", which reads as "the task does not exist" rather than "you are reading the wrong database". `ReopenTask(ctx, taskID)` is now on the interface with a Firestore implementation (`internal/storage/firestore/coordinator_reopen.go`, transactional: the rejected/cancelled guard is a read, and two operators reopening at once would otherwise both pass it), the command uses the same `openCoordinatorStore` resolver as approve/reject/list, prints the plane before mutating it, and `--remote` is now a declared flag on `reopen` rather than one the guard refuses. `TestReopenTask` pins the semantics both backends implement — refuse anything not rejected/cancelled, clear `completed_at`, reset the task's approval to pending (create it if absent, addressed by `task_id` so a pre-`ApprovalIDForTask` row is reset rather than duplicated). Verified on prod: the eleven `ailang-core-triage` tasks cancelled by a stray `approvals --clear-orphans` are back in the approval queue (25 → 36 pending), with their artifacts and chain intact

### Fixed — `messages forward` rewrote the row and told nothing

- `ForwardInboxMessage` changes `to_inbox` in place and stops. On a cloud plane Pub/Sub push is the only delivery path, so a forwarded report arrived at an inbox with an agent, sat unread, and no task was ever created — the "FILED, NOT DISPATCHED" outcome the command exists to repair. `messages forward` now publishes the notification after the rewrite and runs the same undispatchable-inbox warning as `send`. The row keeps its original `created_at`: the message really was sent then, and back-dating it to make dispatch work would hide when the report arrived

### Changed — the language core is a leaf of the platform (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M5)

- `internal/diag/closure_expected_violations.txt` is **empty**: nothing under the thirteen language roots reaches a platform package or a heavy third-party root (sqlite, OTel SDK/exporters, grpc, websocket, GCP firestore/pubsub/storage/trace). That is precondition 1 of the D1 two-binary split. `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s language-closure section is now generated from `go list -deps` (`scripts/gen_architecture_closure.sh`) and gated in CI (`make check-architecture-closure`). `telemetry.GoogleCloudProject()` reads through `config.TraceProjectFromEnv()` — env-only on purpose, since a non-empty value is what switches Cloud Trace export on.
- Accepted, named regressions in `make simplicity-audit` vs the 2026-09-14 snapshot: `instruction_surface_bytes` +302 B (the CLAUDE.md instrument row) and `tracked_files` (+ new packages, − deletions net positive) — both are Phase 4's diet, not hidden here. The closure gate moves 40 → 42 because the `config` and `statedir` leaves now sit inside the core by design.

### Changed — compiler and runtime logic leaves `cmd/ailang` (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M3)

- refactor(cmd): `ailang verify`'s surface-AST → SMT translation, ADT/record extraction, callee-sort gate and the per-function verification loop move to `internal/smt` (`from_ast.go`, `filter.go`, `smt.Verify` in `verify.go`); `ailang check --package`'s unresolved-reference and STRICT_FALLBACK_001 analyses, source discovery and the per-file loop move to the new `internal/check` (`CheckPackageFiles`); the whole `ailang run` pipeline — capability resolution and suggestions, effect-handler wiring, entrypoint execution, the bytecode VM dispatch with evaluator fallback, batch mode — moves to the new `internal/runner` (`runner.Run(ctx, Options)` returns the exit code instead of calling `os.Exit`). The four cmd files become flag parsing plus one call each: `verify.go` 692 → 155, `check_package.go` 651 → 200, `main_run_exec.go` 663 → 172, `run_helpers.go` 773 → removed. Output is byte-identical over `examples/` for `run`, `check` and `verify`; `ai_check.go`'s exit-code contract is unchanged (it keeps its own loop, which differs from `verify`'s in three measured ways and is left for its own change). The AI handler and routing-policy wiring stay in cmd (they read the model registry) and reach the runner as `Options.AIHandler` / `Options.RoutingPolicy`

### Changed — language core no longer links the OTel SDK (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M1)

- refactor(telemetry): `internal/telemetry` is API-only (`StartSpan`, `Tracer`, helpers, trace-context propagation, enable flags); `Tracer()` resolves the OTel API's global provider, a no-op until one is registered. The SDK, the OTLP and Cloud Trace exporters and the `Init*` family move to `internal/platform/otel` (package `otelplatform`), called from `cmd/ailang` and `internal/server` at startup — one binary, same spans. Five entries (`otel/sdk`, `otel/exporters`, GCP exporter, grpc, `cloud.google.com/go/trace`) leave `internal/diag/closure_expected_violations.txt`; the last two (sqlite, websocket) went in M2 below — the list is empty

### Changed — the language core no longer links cgo sqlite or a websocket library (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M2)

- refactor(effects): the persistent brain cache and the ws:// Stream transport are opened through registration seams (`effects.RegisterSharedCacheOpener`, `effects.RegisterStreamTransport`); the implementations moved to `internal/platform/sharedmem` (SQLite) and `internal/platform/streamws` (gorilla), registered once from `cmd/ailang/platform_init.go`. With nothing registered, `NewBrainStore` and a ws:// `Stream.connect` return `effects.ErrBackendNotRegistered` (typed, `errors.Is`-able) — no nil cache, no panic. `go-sqlite3` leaves `closure_expected_violations.txt`; `gorilla/websocket` joins the closure leak roots so it cannot return. `ailang cache put/show/promote/search/list/stats` output unchanged.

### Changed — one resolver each for the cloud project and the state directory; prod defaults deprecated (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M4)

- refactor(config): new leaf `internal/config` (stdlib + yaml, leaf-tested). `config.CloudProject(ctx)`
  resolves `AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT` > `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` > `~/.ailang/config.yaml` `pubsub.project_id`
  > GCE metadata (500 ms, cached, off under `AILANG_NO_METADATA`) and returns the typed
  `ErrNoCloudProject` instead of a guess. All 36 `os.Getenv` project reads under `internal/` and
  `cmd/` route through it (the one in `internal/telemetry` moves with the M1 seam); the
  `GCP_PROJECT` alias and gemini's private metadata client are gone. Two process-wide `os.Setenv`
  leaks are removed: `coordinator --remote` and the notify daemon hand their discovered project to
  `storage.NewBackendsForModeProject` explicitly, and the quorum caller's
  `os.Setenv("GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT")` had no reader. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M4)
- refactor(config): **D3 deprecation, ruled 2026-09-15.** The hard-coded production defaults
  (`"ailang-multivac"` for the project, `"europe-west1"` for the region) are now served by
  `config.DeprecatedDefault` / `config.Region()`: still honoured, but with ONE stderr warning per
  process — `<VAR> is unset; using deprecated default <value>. Set <VAR>. v1.0.0 will refuse to
  start without it.` `AILANG_STRICT_CONFIG=1` makes that refusal an error today (wrapping
  `config.ErrDeprecatedDefault`) so a plist or Cloud Run env can be proven complete before the
  release that enforces it. Sites: `coordinator config` bucket, `coordinator config roll`,
  `coordinator agent-check`, the cloud dispatcher region. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M4)
- refactor(statedir): new stdlib-only leaf `internal/statedir`. `statedir.Dir()` = `AILANG_STATE_DIR`,
  else `$HOME/.ailang/state`, and an ERROR — never `""` or a relative `.ailang/state` beside the
  process — when neither resolves. All 34 `filepath.Join(home, ".ailang", "state")` sites route
  through it, so `AILANG_STATE_DIR` is honoured by the coordinator daemon, `NewSQLiteStore("")`,
  heartbeats, the observatory and collaboration default paths, `mission.Paths` (new `StateDir`
  field), the mission comms gh-issue file, quota ledgers, ollama observations, the rig lock, the
  ai request-log sentinels, and every `cmd/ailang` DB path. `chains post`: with one resolver a
  `local`/`hybrid` remote target is always this node's own observatory (that was the two-resolver
  disagreement the audit found), so it is rejected flat — `gcp` is the only elsewhere.
  `statedir.Project(root, …)` names the repository-scoped `.ailang/state` tree explicitly (tracked
  sprints, the project brain) and is untouched by the variable. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M4)
- chore(metrics): `getenv_outside_config` excludes `internal/statedir` alongside `internal/config`;
  388 → 342 on this change. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S2 M4)

### Fixed — opencode timeouts orphaned the server and MCP children

- fix(executor): opencode spawns in its own process group and kills the group on timeout, like pi and codex (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M2)

### Removed — verified-dead code deleted for v1.0.0 (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M4)

Each cluster is its own commit with the zero-caller grep re-run and a `git log -S` note in
the body, so any one can be reverted alone. 7,448 lines removed in total.

- chore(cleanup): coordinator `HandleApproval` / `HandleRejection` / `requestHandoffApproval` /
  `processHandoffApproval` / `triggerEmbeddedHandoffs` scaffolding in `daemon_approval.go` — never
  wired (the live path is `ProcessApprovalRequest`). 588 LOC. `sendHandoffMessage` was KEPT: the
  audit missed that `handleAgentHandoffs` calls it on the auto-approve path. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M4)
- chore(cleanup): eval_harness Claude-headless runner — `runHeadlessSession`, `checkClaudeCLI`,
  `determineSuccess`, `getErrorMessage`, `getSolutionFilename`, `ClaudeHeadlessResult` (a copy of
  `executor/claude`'s type), all of `agent_runner_streaming.go`, and the stream-scan fmt-hook
  classifier only it used; dead since the `ailang-agent` deprecation (2026-01). 930 LOC.
  (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M4)
- chore(cleanup): observatory Jaeger / GCP-Trace / composite backends and the
  `AILANG_ENABLE_GCP_TRACE` branch in `server.WithObservatoryDB` — the flag was born gated and nothing
  ever set it; the observatory is local SQLite only. 1,917 LOC. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M4)
- chore(cleanup): `internal/cognition/` — no commit ever imported it since it landed (2026-05); the
  one docs link that pointed at it is dropped. 3,687 LOC. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M4)
- chore(cleanup): root `testutil/` (never imported; `internal/testutil` is the live one), root
  `benchmark/` ("temporary", last touched 2025-11) and tracked `output/*.txt` (already
  gitignored). 326 LOC. (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M4)

### Added — scoped registry keys: write only your own packages, read everything (M-PKG-MULTI-NAMESPACE-AUTH)

The registry validator authorised every write against ONE shared key; `// Step 5: Namespace
auth — deferred` had sat in `cmd/registry-validator/main.go` since the registry shipped, while
the Firestore database terraform provisioned for it was read by nothing. Daneel needing to
publish without the superuser key was the trigger.

- Every `X-API-Key` now resolves to a principal: the `REGISTRY_API_KEY` env is the
  **superuser** (scope `*`); an `ailr_…` key whose SHA-256 is a document in the
  `ailang_registry_keys` Firestore collection is that document's owner with its scopes.
- Scopes are `path.Match` globs over the package name (`daneel/*`, `sunholo/daneel_*`),
  enforced on `/publish` AND `/unpublish`; `/rebuild-index` and `/admin/*` are superuser-only.
- `POST|GET /admin/keys`, `DELETE /admin/keys/<id>` mint, list and revoke. The plaintext key
  is returned once and never stored. CLI: `ailang pkg key create|list|revoke`.
- `published_by` is stamped with the key owner for scoped publishes, so metadata records WHO.
- No silent fallback: with `FIRESTORE_DATABASE` unset the validator says "scoped keys not
  configured" on every `ailr_` key rather than "invalid key"; a Firestore error is a 500, not
  a 403.
- Reads were already open (bucket `allUsers` objectViewer, `/api/*` unauthenticated) — unchanged.

Design: `design_docs/planned/m-pkg-multi-namespace-auth.md`. Guide:
`docs/docs/guides/package-publishing.md`.

### Fixed — a dashboard approval now fires the handoff, same as the CLI path

- fix(server): dashboard approve/reject now goes through ProcessApprovalRequest, so handoffs
  fire from the dashboard too (M-V1-SIMPLIFY-S1 M3). `POST /api/coordinator/{approve,reject}/{id}`
  resolved the approval record by hand and returned `{success:true}` — it never merged, never
  handed off, and left the task `pending_approval` — a week after the CLI path was fixed for the
  same bug (2026-09-07). Both HTTP surfaces now share one `processTaskApproval` helper; the
  response shape is unchanged. Regression test follows the work across the HTTP seam into the
  next stage's inbox (`internal/server/handlers_coordinator_approval_test.go`). With no
  coordinator store the route is now a loud 503 instead of a half-approval.

### Fixed — claude's token cap weighed 62 tokens against a cap of 20,000

The per-harness accumulation table said claude emits CUMULATIVE usage (so assign the latest
value) and that `cache_creation_input_tokens` sits outside the usage block the `message_delta`
handler reads (so the in-flight kill was abandoned). Both were inference; no claude-code
stream had ever been recorded in this tree. A capture under the executor's own flags
(`internal/executor/claude/testdata/claude_stream_partial.ndjson`, six turns) shows the
counters are cumulative **within** a turn and **reset at every `message_start`** — a run
total is the SUM of the per-turn finals, which reproduce the result event exactly (50 input,
49,214 cache creation, 315,648 cache read, 706 output) — and that cache creation is present
in flight, in both `message_start.message.usage` and `message_delta.usage`.

Compounded, the two errors left the thrash guard comparing **62 against a cap of 20,000** on
a run that processed 49,970, so no claude run could ever be killed for a runaway. Fixed by
`usageGuard` (`internal/executor/claude/usage_cap.go`), which owns per-turn accumulation,
charges the cost budget only the increment, and kills on breach — tested at `message_start`
too, so a turn large enough to breach is caught before its output is generated. Two further
defects surfaced once the kill worked: a thrash kill was banked as `FinishReason: "stop"`
(now `killFinishReason` on every return path, so cap kills are attributable), and the kill
path reported `InputTokens`/`OutputTokens` only — omitting the cache bucket it was killed
for. `claude.go` 864 → 765 lines, under the 800 gate.

pi needed no new capture: `tool_use.ndjson` already carried four turns and 9,264 tokens of
`cacheWrite`, only an assertion was missing. `TestPiCapCountsCacheCreation` fires a 3,000
cap on a run whose entire input+output is 235, so it can only pass if cache creation is
inside the guard. opencode, codex and motoko rows still have no recorded stream behind them.
Record: `design_docs/verification/mission-iteration-reliability/claude-stream-fixture.md`.

### Fixed — a stale driver pin reported drift 0: it now reports its age behind origin/dev

`tools/launchd/lib/pin-root.sh` measured `PIN_DRIFT` as "commits the SOURCE CLONE is behind the ref",
so under any `AILANG_DRIVER_REF` that is a commit already contained in the clone the reading was 0 by
construction — the 2026-09-07 SHA pin sat 266 commits behind `origin/dev` while every fire logged
`driver pin drift: 0`. The helper now also computes `PIN_AGE` = `rev-list --count <target>..<origin/dev SHA>`
against an immutable `origin/dev^{commit}` captured once after the gate refresh (`PIN_AGE_BASE_SHA`),
transports both across the re-exec as `AILANG_DRIVER_AGE` / `AILANG_DRIVER_AGE_BASE_SHA`, reads them
back on the pinned pass and prints them in `PIN_NOTE`; unknown is never zero. `mission-control.sh` gets
an independent age decision (own state file `mission-<name>.pin-age`, threshold `AILANG_DRIVER_AGE_WARN`
default 25 with the same non-positive floor, dedupe-until-doubling) and a sibling `pin-age` notice
naming the pinned ref, target SHA and baseline SHA. A pinned target whose helper predates the
instrument produces a loud pre-handoff `driver pin age: unknown (?)` diagnostic via the
`PIN_AGE_SUPPORTED` handshake. `test_pin_root.sh` 81 → 117 assertions (incl. a PATH `git` shim that
moves `origin/dev` between baseline capture and `rev-list`), `test_driver_notify.sh` 50 → 81;
22-mutant drill recorded in `design_docs/planned/m-pin-drift-blind-under-sha-pin-mutation-audit.md`.

### Fixed — the stderr-capture helper closed the pipe read end before the copier drained it

The `capturePipelineStderr` test helper in `internal/pipeline/pipeline_module_phases_test.go`
teardown closed the pipe's read end before the copier goroutine had drained it, so on a loaded
macOS CI runner the `[CACHE]` capture was truncated and `TestPipelineModulePhases_DebugCacheFormsAndCounters`
reported truncated stderr (4 of the last 25 `Build and Release` runs). Teardown now runs inside a
panic-safe named-return `defer` that closes the writer → waits for the copier (bounded, 10 s) →
closes the reader, propagates the copy error instead of discarding it, and is pinned by four helper
tests plus a six-mutant drill.

## [v0.38.8] - 2026-09-15

Routing AILANG core reports into the pipeline, and the three faults that
surfaced doing it.

### Fixed — a re-run of a task could never be approved again

Eleven triage tasks finished their work and became unapprovable: rejected,
re-dispatched, ran again, and each settled into `pending_approval` behind the
previous attempt's `rejected` approval record — invisible to
`coordinator approvals`, and refused by `approve` because an already-resolved
approval cannot be resolved again.

Two causes in series, and either alone strands the task. `FinalizationLedger`
lives on the task record, so a re-dispatched task carried the previous run's
ledger and the approval effect was skipped as `already done`; it is now cleared
in `MarkTaskQueued`, where a new execution begins. And the approval id derives
from the task id alone, so two executions address one row — approvals now carry
a `work_id` (changed files + diffstat, not the patch, which differs between
identical re-runs), and a collision is superseded only when the work matches. An
absent work id on either side means unknown and changes nothing, because reading
it as "different" would reopen a human's decision on every replay.

### Fixed — a duplicate PR is the idempotency signal, not a failed task

Two of fifteen parallel runs reported FAILED with their work pushed and their PR
open: the task opened PR #1223, was re-executed, and the second execution
treated GitHub's 422 "A pull request already exists" as fatal. Re-execution is
normal, so PR creation now finds the existing PR and returns it — matched on the
message, because 422 is also "No commits between …", which is a real failure and
stays one.

### Fixed — `messages forward` filed without dispatching

It rewrote `to_inbox` in place and told nothing. On a cloud plane push is the
only delivery path, so a forwarded report landed on an inbox with an agent, sat
unread, and no task was created — the exact "filed, not dispatched" outcome the
command exists to repair. It now publishes the notification, and validates its
target the way `send` does.

### Added — the ailang-core-triage agent

Reports about AILANG itself accumulated where nothing acted: 23 of 32 messages
in `user` were engineering items with 21 unread, and 10 more sat in
`ailang-core`, which no agent served. Routing them straight at
design-doc-creator would cost ~$1-3 and ~20 minutes of full-size GLM each. A
flash model now writes one triage file per report — class, recommendation,
searched terms, and an estimate — and does NOT route: escalation stays a human
approving the row.

Its rubric is countable rather than aesthetic: `DIRECT_FIX_MAX_LINES = 2`,
`DIRECT_FIX_MAX_FILES = 1`, with "more than one acceptable way" and any
semantics/surface/format/gate change outranking size. Under the first, vaguer
prose it called 11 of 14 `direct-fix`; under the rubric, 2 of 15 — and every
`direct-fix` must state an estimate, so the claim is checkable against the
eventual diff.

### Changed — `messages inboxes` names the repository

`design-doc-creator` sounds generic and serves only sunholo-data/ailang, while
`daneel-design` serves sunholo-data/daneel. The listing now says which, and one
function builds that line instead of the two that had already drifted.

### Changed — `agent-check` looks where cloud agents actually look

AGENTS.md line 19 names `.agents/skills/`, and that is what the cloud executor
reads; the check inspected `.claude/skills/` only, so it could report "skill
present" for a file a cloud run cannot see. It now tries `.agents/` first and
says plainly when only the other tree has it. (33 of 38 skills differ between
the two; the drift itself is being fixed separately.)

## [v0.38.7] - 2026-09-14

The agent pipeline completed a chain for the first time. Four stages —
design-doc-creator → sprint-planner → sprint-executor → sprint-evaluator — each
receiving one message and acting on it. Getting there took six root causes,
every one measured in production rather than reasoned about.

### Fixed — the Go-toolchain executors were OOM-killed at 2Gi

sprint-executor died twice on the same task, at 3m24s and 9m55s, both reporting
`codex task failed: signal: killed` with `turns=1, tokens=0+0` — the model never
answered because the container was already dying. Cloud Run logged seven
"Out-of-memory event detected in container" lines during the second attempt.

The discriminator was the VARIANT, not the model: sprint-planner ran the same
executor on the same model and completed. All seventeen executor jobs shared one
`agent_memory`, so the images that COMPILE were sized by a number chosen for
images that do not. The six `-go` variants now have `agent_memory_go` at 8Gi;
the eleven others keep the 2Gi bound. (terraform, ailang-multivac)

### Fixed — a notification we cannot read is not a task

The Pub/Sub adapter hydrates title and body from Firestore. When that fetch
failed it proceeded with its placeholder and dispatched a task whose entire
content was the string `msg_20260914_153340_b787f96a`. Sixteen such tasks were
created for sprint-planner in four minutes; eleven "completed", each firing a
handoff into sprint-executor on a task with no request in it.

A fetch ERROR now NACKs so Pub/Sub redelivers; a fetch returning nil acks and
says so. Neither dispatches.

### Fixed — a long line is not a failed run

`bufio.Scanner: token too long` killed whole runs AFTER the agent had done the
work. The caps were four arbitrary numbers — pi 8MB, claude/codex/opencode 1MB,
motoko and the SSE reader on the 64KB default — so identical output killed a
task on one harness and not another. `executor.LineReader` replaces all of them:
one cap, and exceeding it truncates rather than failing.

### Fixed — sprint state JSON could never be committed

`.gitignore` excluded all of `.ailang/`, yet sprint-planner declares
`.ailang/state/sprints/*.json` as an artifact and sprint-executor REFUSES to
start without it. The handoff between those two agents was impossible to
complete. Older sprint JSONs are in the repo only because they were tracked
before the rule — which is exactly why the gap stayed invisible.

### Fixed — `--remote` was accepted and ignored by nine subcommands

`coordinator list --remote gcp` answered from local SQLite and returned a task
from May 2026 while production held live work. A guard now refuses an unknown
flag at the dispatch point, naming where the flag IS real, and `list` opens the
plane it is told to.

### Fixed — PR titles reverse-engineered from the prompt

`[agent] pkg-sunholo-testing-utils: You are an autonomous AIL…`. Third shape of
one mistake: a description derived from a template-wrapped prompt yields a new
wrong answer every time the template changes. The task's own title is now
carried (`AILANG_TASK_TITLE`), with derivation as the fallback.

### Added — an agent can say "I could not start", and why

`BLOCKED: <reason>` / `BLOCKED_ON: <thing>`. Until now the job classified purely
mechanically, so a blocked precondition, a refusal on principle and a discovered
impossibility all collapsed into `no_changes` — which also means "I did the work
and nothing needed changing". `TaskCompletion` additionally carries `Summary`,
the agent's own account, because the reason used to live only in a GCS
transcript nobody read.

### Added — the coordinator can resolve a pull request

It had created PRs since cloud execution existed and had no way to merge or
close one. 24 open `coordinator/*` PRs: 22 belonged to already-rejected or
failed tasks, one to a task approved twenty minutes earlier. approve/reject now
reconcile the PR inline, bounded by the agent's declared artifact_patterns, and
`ailang coordinator prs` sweeps the rest (dry run by default).

### Added — `ailang coordinator pipeline` and `lint`

`pipeline` answers "did the chain run, and which stage stopped it?", discovering
the topology from `trigger_on_complete` rather than asking an operator to retype
it. `lint` validates the whole registry: edges that name no agent, handoffs no
code path can release, cycles, unbounded auto-merge, near-duplicate inboxes.

### Testing — the chain is walked, and the walk was proven able to fail

A single test now walks all four stages through the code production uses,
asserting at each boundary that the next stage has a message where dispatch
looks, that the task built from it survives dedup, and that it names the
artifact. Verified by reintroducing four real bugs — the first version of the
test caught only two, and both misses were holes in the test rather than the
code.

## [v0.38.6] - 2026-09-14

Four measured defects on the message plane and the agent pipeline, all found by
running work through production rather than by reading code.

### Fixed — a handoff was ALWAYS deduplicated against the stage it followed

The agent pipeline has never completed a chain, and the approval-path fix of
v0.35 was only half the reason. Measured on the first handoff that ever fired:
design-doc-creator completed `task-08032ebc`, the approval dispatched
sprint-planner, and one second later the new task came back
`{"status":"deduplicated","original_task_id":"task-08032ebc","original_task_status":"completed"}`.

It was suppressed by its own parent. `sendAgentHandoffMessage` embeds the
predecessor's request verbatim, the fingerprint is `simhash(content)` alone, and
a parent at handoff time is always `completed` — which suppresses. So every
handoff ever dispatched simhashed to the task it came from and died on arrival.

Dedup now decides content equality only within a `DedupScope`: a different agent
is different work, and a task is never a duplicate of its own declared parent.
An absent agent on either side decides nothing — older tasks predate `agent_id`,
and reading `""` as "matches anything" would silently change suppression.

### Fixed — `--remote` was accepted and ignored by nine coordinator subcommands

`coordinator list --remote gcp` answered from local SQLite and returned a task
from May 2026 while production held live work. Nine subcommands parse arguments
with a `switch` and no default case, so an unknown flag was skipped in silence —
while `approve`, `reject` and `approvals` DO honour it, which is the asymmetry
that made the flag trustworthy right up until it wasn't.

`rejectUnknownCoordinatorFlags` now runs once at the dispatch point, turning a
wrong answer into a refusal that names where the flag is real, with a test that
reads the dispatch switch so a new subcommand cannot inherit the silence.
`list` additionally opens the plane it is told to and prints `store:` on every
listing; interactive drill-down stays local-only and says so.

### Fixed — PR titles were reverse-engineered from the prompt (third shape)

`[agent] pkg-sunholo-testing-utils: You are an autonomous AIL…`. The directive
reaching the job is the template-wrapped prompt, so its first line is harness
boilerplate. The v0.38.5 fix corrected the JSON shape of the same error and the
one before it the unbounded shape — three fixes, one cause. The task already has
a human title; `AILANG_TASK_TITLE` now carries it, with derivation kept as the
fallback for a dispatcher too old to send one.

### Fixed — `messages health` judged the wrong registry and miscounted

It loaded `~/.ailang/config.yaml` unconditionally, so a laptop holding 2 agents
reported a 177-message "config gap" against a plane whose real gap was 54. It
also classified by inbox alone, counting agents' own completions as
"routable and never dispatched" — prod read `DEGRADED 87` where the real number
was 4. Health now shares `resolveInboxRegistry` with `messages inboxes` and
`coordinator agents`, and buckets agent output separately.

### Added — `ailang coordinator lint`

Relational validation of the whole registry, which `agent-check` structurally
cannot do: chain edges that name a non-existent agent, handoffs no code path can
release (`skip_approval` plus a non-auto edge has neither), cycles, unbounded
auto-merge, incoherent deploy keys, near-duplicate inboxes. Wired into
`agent-set`'s pre-commit validation so the config fast path cannot deploy a
registry the linter would reject.

### Added — unknown inboxes are refused at send time

`messages inboxes` could say "did you mean daneel-design?" — but only afterwards,
to whoever thought to look. Five design requests went to `daneel-design-ailang`
on 13 September: all filed, all bounced, each bounce itself addressed to an
unregistered inbox. The check refuses only when judging against the registry the
plane dispatches from, and otherwise warns and sends, because refusing against a
laptop's two-agent fallback would block every legitimate message.

## [v0.38.5] - 2026-09-13

### Fixed — the wrapper pushed a branch NAME, so work on any other branch was lost

Tasks 70b77905 and 1063e5fd (design-doc-creator-daneel). The wrapper creates
`coordinator/task-<id>` at the clone point; the agent then ran `git checkout -b
design-doc/<slug>`, committed there and pushed that branch itself. Nothing in its
skill told it to — it simply did. `git push origin coordinator/task-<id>` then
pushed that unmoved ref, exited 0, and GitHub refused the PR:

```
pr create failed: 422 "No commits between main and coordinator/task-1063e5fd"
```

The design docs existed the whole time, on a branch nobody was told about; a
human opened and merged the PRs six hours later.

v0.38.4 made the PR step unconditional but left the deeper fault: `hasWork`
measured `clonePoint..HEAD` and **did** find those commits, while the push sent a
different ref. The check and the push were talking about different commits. The
push is now `HEAD:refs/heads/<branch>` — HEAD is the one thing that means "what
the agent produced", whichever branch it chose to stand on.

### Fixed — "git push exited 0" and "the work is published" were the same claim

The completion went out as `completed`, `changed_files: [the document]`,
`error_msg: ""` — about a branch that contained neither. Self-contradictory, and
nothing checked it.

`assertRemoteMatchesHead` now runs after every push: `ls-remote` the branch,
compare to local HEAD, and fail loudly naming where the work actually is. This is
the arm that keeps the class visible even if the refspec fix ever regresses.

Two further honesty fixes from the same incident, both raised by Daneel:

- **A PR failure on a branch that carries work is no longer best-effort.** The
  caller only reaches that line when `hasWork` held, so no PR means no reviewer.
  Paired with a bounded retry: 5xx and transport errors get two more attempts,
  every 4xx stays a hard answer (`422 "No commits between …"` must not be retried
  — it is a verdict, not a hiccup).
- **`head_commit` and `base_commit` now travel on the inbox completion payload.**
  They were on the Pub/Sub struct and dropped at the inbox boundary, so a poller
  could read `changed_files` and had no way to verify the branch carried them.

### Known gap — the git guardrail is enforced for ZERO cloud agents

Found while diagnosing the above. `AILANG_GIT_MODE` is declared per agent, set by
the dispatcher and defaulted by the wrapper — and **nothing in Go reads it**. Its
only reader is `git_guard.sh`, a Claude Code **PreToolUse hook**. Measured against
`config.cloud.yaml`: 35 agents on pi, 2 on codex, 1 on motoko, none with a hook
surface. The guard that exists to block `checkout -b` and off-branch pushes — the
exact behaviour above — has never run for any of them.

Not fixed here; it needs a shim or a credential change, not a hotfix. Design:
`design_docs/planned/m-git-guardrails-hookless-executors.md`.

## [v0.38.4] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed — a pushed branch got no PR when the AGENT did the pushing

design-doc-creator-daneel's first run (task-389b7a51) pushed its branch and no PR
appeared; the design doc sat on a branch nobody was told about and a human opened
the PR by hand six minutes later.

`auto_merge` was not the cause. The wrapper asks `git log origin/BRANCH..HEAD`,
the agent had already pushed, so that came back empty, it logged `no commits to
push` and returned — and the `openCascadePullRequest` call lived **inside** that
push block. An agent that does its own pushing is not an agent that wants no
review. Pushing and reviewing are now separate questions (`hasWork`,
`branchNeedsPush`, `branchWantsPR`), and a direct-push agent committing onto its
own base branch is skipped explicitly rather than logging a 422 for working as
designed.

### Fixed — the deploy-key push probe ran before the clone, so it could not run

Attempt 3 of the daneel-writer task (task-98301715):

```
deploy key push check failed on sunholo-data/daneel-memory: exit status 128:
fatal: not a git repository (or any of the parent directories): .git
```

`git push --dry-run <remote> HEAD:refs/...` resolves HEAD, a **local** ref, so it
needs a repository; `git ls-remote <url>` does not, which is why the read half
passed. The key was never exercised and the message judged it anyway — the third
time in two days that a broken check was mistaken for the thing it checks.

READ now runs before the clone, WRITE from inside it; both still precede any
agent work. `pushProbeVerdict` is a tested seam, because the two ways to be wrong
are symmetrical: calling a broken check a bad key, and calling a bad key a broken
check. **Writing that test caught a second bug** — GitHub's wording is `has been
marked as read only`, with a space, and the code matched only `read-only`, so a
genuinely read-only key fell through to the generic arm.

### Fixed — no executor image could say which release it was

`ailang --version` inside the promoted prod `agent-pi:v0.38.3` reported `AILANG
dev`. agent-base, dashboard and mcp all built with `-ldflags="-s -w"` and no
version, so an image could only be identified by digest. The version now comes
from `std/VERSION`, which is in the build context and which the smoke gate
already asserts equals the tag.

`Dockerfile.registry-validator` had the same bug in its second form: it stamped
`github.com/sunholo/ailang/...` when the module is `github.com/sunholo-data/ailang`.
A `-X` against a path that does not exist sets nothing and reports no error —
proven with a control before fixing (correct path → `AILANG v0.38.3`, wrong path
→ `AILANG dev`). Same family as the release-manager skill's documented
`-X main.Version` trap.

### `agent-check` — a skill may live in the shared plugin

`checkSkill` looked only in the workspace repo. `sunholo-data/daneel` has no
`.claude/` at all, yet design-doc-creator-daneel runs, because the skill is in
`ailang_bootstrap`, pre-cloned into every executor image. The decision is split
out as `skillVerdict` and tested: a definite 404 from **both** places is a miss,
while 403 or 5xx is UNKNOWN.

## [v0.38.3] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed — the executor images had no `ssh`, so no deploy key could ever work

Found in production on the first real dispatch of a deploy-key agent
(daneel-writer, 2026-09-12): `No ssh binary in the executor image`.

`docker/Dockerfile.agent-base` installs `ca-certificates git curl gnupg` with
`--no-install-recommends`, and **openssh-client is a *Recommends* of Debian's
git, not a dependency** — so it was never there. Every executor image derives
from agent-base, so none of them could run `ssh`. The key, the `~/.ssh/config`
host alias and the pinned `known_hosts` were all written correctly onto an image
that could not use them, and git surfaced it from inside a clone as
`cannot run ssh: No such file or directory` — which reads as a key or permission
problem and sends you to Secret Manager and GitHub deploy-key settings, neither
of which is wrong.

Two changes, because the package alone would leave the next instance of this
just as hard to read:

- `openssh-client` in agent-base, the single root of all executor images.
- `configureSSHDeployKey` now checks `exec.LookPath("ssh")` **before** fetching
  the secret, and says exactly what is missing and which Dockerfile to fix. The
  test asserts the ordering as well as the message: a secrets error on an image
  whose real defect is a missing package is the wrong first thing to see.

Note this could not have been caught by `agent-check` — the config was correct.
It is an image property, and the in-job pre-flight is the right place for it.

## [v0.38.2] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed — a retry of a FAILED task was deduplicated against its own failure

Reported by Daneel 2026-09-12. Attempt 2 of a task that had failed at `git push`
was answered:

