- **It needs no setup at all.** After the env vars, the resolver reads
  `~/.claude/.credentials.json` — the *same* file the `claude` CLI reads. This closes the gap that
  made the two modes feel arbitrarily different: agent mode "just worked" because it execs the CLI
  and the CLI reads that credential in its own process, while standard mode makes the HTTP call
  in-process and so had nothing to read and had to ask a human for a token. Now standard mode works
  wherever agent mode already works. `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (the fleet's existing cloud
  convention, M-CLOUD-OAUTH) is honoured too. An expired token is rejected up front with a refresh
  hint rather than sent — a bare 401 would bank every benchmark in the run as `api_error` for an
  invisible cause.
- `internal/ai/anthropic_auth.go` (new): `ResolveAnthropicCredential()` is the single source of
  truth. Order is `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` then `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, matching the Anthropic SDKs and
  the `claude` CLI — so if both are set, the **metered key wins**. That precedence has already
  caused one real mis-billing here, so `Lane()` exists to be logged. Neither set is a loud error,
  never a default lane.

**Billing integrity is the point, not a side effect.** The token counts and arithmetic are
identical on both lanes, so the only thing separating "we were charged $12" from "we spent quota"
is the `cost_provenance` label that every cost-per-pass comparison and budget guard reads.
`standardModeCostProvenance` now returns `list-price-equivalent` for an Anthropic row on the OAuth
lane — following the precedent already set for Ollama Cloud — via the same resolver the client
uses, so the label cannot disagree with the lane that actually ran. Pinned by tests covering all
four env combinations, plus that an OAuth token must not relabel a non-Anthropic row.

### Changed — Claude Fable 5.1 replaces Fable 5 in extended_suite and the mission designer lane

Mark, attended 2026-09-02: "lets replace fable 5 with 5.1 in our suite evals executor etc."
Pricing is **identical** ($10/$50 per 1M), so this is a same-cost generational refresh, not a
contested promotion — the match-or-beat-the-incumbent bar exists to justify a cost delta and
there is none.

Swapped at every live site: `extended_suite` membership; the mission controller ladder
(`MISSION_MODEL_PREFS` default `claude-opus-5,claude-fable-5-1`); the designer lane
(`MISSION_DESIGNER_MODEL` default `claude:claude-fable-5-1` — the claude-CLI full-id form, since
the bare `fable` alias silently falls back to opus under an opus-first controller); the routing
and controller-chain test fixtures that pin those strings; and the `--full` baseline descriptions.

`claude-fable-5` is **retained opt-in** as the Fable-generation longitudinal anchor — it holds the
only banked Fable core+frontier numbers (28/31 at v0.32.0). Its `reasoning_anthropic.go` and
`cache.go` entries stay; `claude-fable-5-1` needs no `cache.go` row of its own because
longest-prefix matching already resolves it to the 512-token Fable entry.

**Measured 2026-09-02** (standard mode, metered key, core+frontier, N=1, candidate alone):
**29/31 (93.5%)**, $5.5870, zero `finish=length`. The only two failures are `docx_reimplement`
and `markdown_reimplement` — the `input_files` harness gap, excluded from `anchor_v1` as artifacts
and failed by every model measured — so **29/29** on benchmarks that can actually be passed.
Anchored ELO placement **2554.4**.

Against Fable 5 (28/31 banked at v0.32.0, failing `docx` + `markdown` + `gauntlet_10`), 5.1
additionally passes `gauntlet_10`, which among every model measured only `claude-opus-5` had
cracked. The seat swap is now supported by data rather than by identical pricing alone. Caveats
kept with the number: N=1, and the comparison spans AILANG versions (v0.32.0 vs v0.34.0), so
language improvements are a confound on the +1.

Also surfaced (and since fixed, below): **`reason_tokens` read 0 on every Anthropic row** — a
harness gap, not a model that skipped thinking. Treat this run's per-row token *split* as
unavailable; pass/fail and cost are unaffected.

### Fixed — Anthropic runs recorded 0 reasoning tokens; the real figure was 77% of output

`internal/ai/anthropic` never parsed `usage.output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens`, so
`ReasonTokens` was 0 on every Anthropic row. The code said so deliberately: *"Anthropic reports no
separate thinking-token count … any split here would be fabricated."* **That was true when written
and false by the time it mattered** — the field exists and populates (live-probed: 298 thinking of
1160 output, blocks `["thinking","text"]`). Nothing tested the claim, so an always-on thinking
model banked zeroes.

Fixed at all three mapping sites — `client.go` (Generate), `step.go` (agent/step) and
`streamstep.go` (the terminal `message_delta`). Fixing only the first would have left the
streaming path reporting 0 while the non-streaming path told the truth, which is worse than a
uniform zero.

Re-measured after the fix, `claude-fable-5-1` on `gauntlet_10`: **48,993 reasoning of 63,223
output tokens — 77.5% of output was thinking**, previously banked as 0. Cost math is unchanged and
was always right (Anthropic bills thinking inside `output_tokens`), so this decomposes that number
rather than adding to it; a test asserts `thinking ≤ output` so the two are never summed. The
wire-shape test pins a verbatim live response body, so the next time this claim goes stale it
fails loudly instead of silently zeroing.

### Added — Claude Fable 5.1 registered (opt-in; core-tier gate PENDING)

Anthropic released Claude Fable 5.1 on 2026-09-01. New `claude-fable-5-1` row in
`internal/modelreg/models.yml`: API id `claude-fable-5-1` (dash form; OpenRouter mirrors it as
`anthropic/claude-fable-5.1`, which `vendorMirror` already rewrites, so the pricing cross-check
resolves with no special case), 1M context, 128K vendor max output capped to the family's 64000,
and pricing **unchanged from Fable 5** at $10/$50 per 1M — verified against both the vendor
models overview and the OpenRouter endpoints API (3 upstreams, all 1M/128K).

Registered alongside it: `internal/ai/reasoning_anthropic.go` gains
`{Adaptive: true, CanDisable: false}` — 5.1 keeps Fable 5's always-on adaptive thinking, so an
explicit `{"type":"disabled"}` is still a 400 at any effort — and the headroom test's
`ceilingLimited` map gains the 64000 entry.

Membership is **opt-in only**. Smoke is a floor, not a ranking, and fable rows are exempt from
smoke anyway; whether 5.1 takes `claude-fable-5`'s `extended_suite` seat is a core-tier
head-to-head that has not been run (~$16/model, needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — standard mode is
direct HTTP with no OAuth path). Since pricing is identical, a core tie is sufficient to promote;
the command is recorded in the row's notes.

### Added — Tencent Hy4 preview registered, smoke gate PASSED 23/23 (opt-in; core gate pending)

`or-hy4-preview` (`tencent/hy4-preview`) — 770B MoE, 49B active, 1M context, text-only, vendor-
positioned for coding agents and tool-use workflows. $0.83/$2.50 per 1M, roughly 12x cheaper on
input than `claude-fable-5-1` at the same context size.

**Smoke gate PASSED 23/23** on the canonical `--tier smoke`, standard mode, first pass, $0.8634
for the tier. Zero `finish=length` across all 23 runs, so the 64000 ceiling was never binding —
capability, not a budget artifact. The `or-glm-5-3` control in the same command scored 22/23; its
one failure was `api_error` with `cost_usd=0` and passed on re-run, so the tier was healthy.

For context on the result: on 2026-05-04 zero open-source candidates passed even the 3-benchmark
subset, with `WRONG_LANG` (model emits Python instead of AILANG) the dominant failure. Hy4
produced no `WRONG_LANG`, no syntax errors, and no wrong-output.

Registered with a verified hard ceiling of 64000 (`/endpoints` reports a single Tencent upstream
at `max_completion_tokens` 64000, so the 65536 fleet target is unreachable and there is no
provider order to pin), and a reasoning probe recorded on the row: it thinks by default (402
reasoning tokens of 626 on the primes probe) but reasoning IS disableable, hence
`default_thinking: "on"` rather than `always_on`. Thinking is not free — 133,278 reasoning tokens
over the tier, 2.4x the anchor, billed as output.

**In no suite.** Smoke is a floor, not a ranking: passing qualifies the row, it does not rank Hy4
against an incumbent. That is a core-tier question, and the command is recorded in the row notes.
`tencent/` added to `openrouterVendorPrefixes` as forward-compat for ad-hoc `--ai tencent/...`.

### Fixed — the prompt-time brain hooks ran unbounded on macOS and timed out on every prompt

Both UserPromptSubmit hooks (`brain_on_prompt.sh`, `microrag_userprompt.sh`) bound their
search with `gtimeout`/`timeout` — neither of which macOS ships — so the fallback ran the
embedding search unbounded and the hook harness killed it at its 4s cap: measured 28/28 prompts
in a 50-session window paying ~4s of blocking wait for output that was then discarded. The
brain itself was healthy (warm-path 0.8s/1.7s); the timeouts were GPU-contention tails with no
inner bound. Both scripts now fall back to `perl -e 'alarm shift; exec @ARGV'` — the same
portable bound `session_start.sh` already used, and the trap its comments already documented.

### Changed — context files right-sized per Claude 5 context-engineering guidance

Reviewed CLAUDE.md, `.claude/rules/`, and the SessionStart hook against Anthropic's Claude 5
context-engineering guidance (judgment over rules, progressive disclosure, no repetition across
layers). The always-loaded baseline drops roughly in half with no measured knowledge deleted —
detail moved to where it loads on demand:

- **SessionStart hook no longer contradicts its own traps**: the inbox banner recommended
  `messages read --all-unread` (drains the unread queue as a side effect) and `ack --all`
  (sweeps outbound cross-mission inboxes); it now points at `list --unread --json` + per-id ack.
- CLAUDE.md's six messaging traps moved to `docs/internal/message-plane-topology.md`
  ("Session-triage traps"); CLAUDE.md keeps the exports, the store-header tell, and a pointer.
- `dev-workflow.md`'s `AILANG_OLLAMA_*` essays and launchd two-delivery-sites narrative
  deferred to `docs/docs/guides/debugging.md`, which gains the `AILANG_OLLAMA_NUM_CTX` section
  it was missing.
- `coordinator.md` keeps its gotchas (TRACEPARENT, `worker_tags` ≠ `--caps`, executor
  contract), drops command tutorials duplicated by skills and `--help`.
- `cloud-endpoints.md`'s resolved OTLP/JSON ID incident compressed to its live symptom line.
- Absolutes retained only for irreversible actions (git destruction, account identity);
  other principles rewritten as judgment with rationale.
### Added — mission slots now report the gate where they stopped

The scheduled mission driver now records a durable, per-attempt `mission-heartbeat` and a bounded
200-row verdict history. Clean exits without a `complete` stamp are classified as reaped at the
last observed gate, while crashes, watchdog kills, legitimate aborts, missing instrumentation,
and pre-Gate-0 deaths remain distinct. Each `slot-verdict` is retained for deriving the reaped-slot
rate. Reaped notices are episode-gated and use the driver's
existing bounded network wrapper.

### Added — the production `run_lane` process-group kill now has a behavioural gate

`tools/eval/motoko_connection_probe.sh`'s `run_lane` timeout path kills the driver's whole
process group, but nothing tested it: reverting that hunk left
`tools/eval/test_motoko_connection_probe.sh` green at 40/40, because the only gate over the
production script was `bash -n`, which cannot tell a group kill from a single-PID kill. The
self-test's fixture-cwd + real-`lsof` survivor observable is now attached to the production path
as arm 36, "production run_lane timeout kills wrapper grandchild": a stub driver `cd`s into a
unique fixture directory, spawns a long-lived `sleep`, and publishes an atomically-renamed ready
file carrying its wrapper PID, child PID and verified cwd — so the deadline never starts before
the grandchild exists. The whole probe runs inside `run_bounded` as an emergency cap set ten
seconds beyond `run_lane`'s own deadline, recorded as a distinct marker that must not fire. The
survivor oracle resolves an absolute real `lsof` *before* the hermetic PATH stub is installed, so
the stub can never make the verdict vacuously green; a missing real `lsof` is a hard failure on
Darwin, and only non-Darwin platforms may structured-skip the arm.

### Fixed — a message written to one project was announced in another

`messages send` resolved its Pub/Sub target from config/env only — never from the store the message
was actually written to. So a message written to `ailang-multivac-dev` could be announced on the
`ailang-multivac` topic: the write succeeded, the publish succeeded, both reported ok, and the work
was invisible to the only coordinator that could see it.

Measured 2026-08-31 on a live probe, twice over. First the project: `pubsub.project_id` pinned prod,
so a dev-bound message woke the prod coordinator instead. Then the topic prefix: even pointed at the
right project, prefix `ailang` targets `ailang-messages`, while dev's topic is `ailang-dev-messages` —
terraform sets the prefix per environment alongside the project.

The notification now follows the store, deriving project AND prefix together, and reports when the
config disagrees rather than silently honouring it. An unrecognised project keeps its configured
prefix instead of receiving a guessed one — a wrong topic is an undelivered message, which is the
failure class this whole area exists to remove.

`ailang messages health` gains the matching check, printing `SPLIT` when the publish target and the
store disagree. Both facts were already on that screen, one line apart; reading them as a pair is
what a health command is for.

### Fixed — the fail-loud regression guard read the developer's real config, and its crash masked the failure

`TestRun_FailsLoudlyWhenTaskProcessingCannotInit` was RED on a workstation and green on CI. Neither
the guard nor `initTaskProcessing` had regressed: the daemon loads its agent registry from
`AILANG_CONFIG`, falling back to `~/.ailang/config.yaml` — the developer's real file. A local
`coordinator` agent whose workspace is a genuine repo hands `initTaskProcessing` a working worktree
manager, so the failing fallback the test depends on is never reached and `Run()` proceeds. The test
now points `AILANG_CONFIG` at an empty file, so the reproduction is the launchd condition and
nothing else.

Underneath it, a second defect made the first hard to read. `Close()` releases the task store and
sets the field to nil; the test's deferred `Close()` runs while the `Run()` goroutine is still
ticking, so the next tick dereferenced a nil store and took the **whole test binary** down with a
nil-pointer panic — which is what the run reported, instead of the assertion that actually failed.
`executeTaskQueue` now reports a missing store as an error rather than dereferencing it. An error,
not a quiet `nil`: "the store is gone" and "there is nothing to do" are different facts and only one
of them is normal.

The `internal/coordinator` suite is green again. The guard still guards — making the fatal path
non-fatal turns it RED with its original message.


### Fixed — the backstop sweep would never have run on the host it was written for

Found by deploying it. The cloud coordinator is a Cloud Run service with `minScale=0`: Pub/Sub push
wakes it, idleness kills it. Measured in dev minutes after the sweep first shipped, consecutive
instance lifetimes were **34s and 12s** — so a 10-minute ticker inside that process fires zero times.
The sweep would have logged `started (interval=10m0s, mode=report)` on every wake and done nothing,
which is precisely the succeed-loudly / fail-silently shape the whole design doc exists to remove.

It now sweeps once on startup, before the first tick — startup being the only moment that process is
reliably alive. The ticker stays for long-lived hosts (local mode, the rig). `off` still means off,
including the startup pass.


### Added — `ailang messages health`: the number that should always be zero

M-MESSAGE-PLANE-TRUST M3. Splits unread messages three ways, because "unread" is not by itself a
fault: a declared human-triage inbox SHOULD accumulate unread, and an unrouted inbox is a config
question. Only a **routable** message sitting unread means work was filed and never dispatched —
and in a healthy plane that count is always 0, because push delivers everything.

Also reports whether THIS machine could announce a send at all (the `pubsub` config that, when
absent, made `messages send` print a green tick over a message nothing would ever pick up), and
names which agent registry the routing verdict was computed from. That last part is load-bearing:
the cloud coordinator reads its agents from a GCS bucket, not from this machine, and the two had
already diverged (41 agents locally vs 34 in prod). A verdict computed against the wrong registry is
worse than none, so the source is printed every time and never assumed.

`--strict` exits non-zero when not HEALTHY, for CI and the morning report. A missing Firestore
composite index is reported as an actionable infrastructure finding with the `gcloud` command to fix
it, rather than an opaque gRPC dump — the same class of gap that made every cloud chain read
"0 stages".


### Added — backstop sweep: an undelivered notification is now latency, not silence

Cloud intake reads the Pub/Sub adapter ONLY (`pollAndProcessTasksCloud` never queries Firestore), so
a message whose notification was never published is invisible forever rather than late. The
send-side hole is closed separately; this is the receive-side floor.

A periodic sweep lists unread messages, keeps only those whose inbox resolves to a registered agent
(skipping declared human-triage inboxes), and hands them to the SAME drain the push path feeds — so
dedup, agent resolution and the feedback gate apply identically rather than recovered work taking a
second, differently-guarded route into task creation. `Collapsed: true` means a message already
marked `dup_of` is never resurrected.

`AILANG_BACKSTOP_SWEEP` selects `report` (default), `dispatch`, or `off`. Report-only is the default
deliberately: the sweep's own risk is double-dispatching work push already did, so the first thing it
has to earn is a measurement of how often it would fire at all. An unrecognised value resolves to
`report` — the safe reading of an unclear instruction is the one that cannot double-run work. The
sweep is silent when it finds nothing, so any output at all is the signal that push dropped
something.


### Fixed — a timed-out task now closes its execution chain

`StaleTaskDetector` marked the TASK failed and stopped there. Chain closure happens otherwise only
on the approval/rejection path (`approval_processor.go`), which a job that died before completing
never reaches — so a chain opened at dispatch stayed `active` forever. Measured 2026-08-31 in prod:
**92 of 99 chains were `active`, the oldest since 2026-04-27**, which makes "running right now" and
"died in April" the same reading. A stuck chain is invisible precisely because it looks busy.

The detector now drives the chain to `failed` alongside the task, naming the chain in the log. A
chain-close failure is reported but does not block the task outcome or its notification: a chain
left open is a reporting defect, not a lost result. Tasks with no chain id, and callers with no
observatory attached, are unaffected.


### Fixed — a Cloud Run job could dispatch an executor whose binary is not in its image

`ExecutorVariant` selects the Cloud Run Job and therefore the image (the Jobs API has no
per-execution image override), while the separate `AILANG_PROVIDER` env var selects which executor
runs INSIDE it. Nothing tied the two together, so a mismatch was discoverable only by the container,
at the END of its setup. Measured 2026-08-28: `task-a0628a5f` dispatched to
`ailang-agent-executor-codex`, logged `running opencode executor (unified path)`, and died on
`exec: "opencode": executable file not found in $PATH` — after cloning 24,032 files and cutting its
branch. Every such dispatch burned a full container start and repo clone to learn something knowable
before launch.

`Dispatch` now refuses a variant/provider pair whose binary cannot exist in the image, naming both
and pointing at `config.cloud.yaml`. The compatibility table is grounded in
`docker/Dockerfile.agent-*`; `agent-eval` (which installs all five CLIs) and the binaryless
`managed_agents` provider are never refused. A table-vs-`knownVariants` drift test keeps the two
from separating.

### Fixed — `pi-go` was built and deployed but undispatchable

`docker/Dockerfile.agent-pi-go` and Cloud Run job `ailang-agent-executor-pi-go` both exist, but
`pi-go` was absent from `knownVariants`, so `jobSuffixForVariant` refused it as an unknown variant.
Added to both the accepted set and the compatibility table.


### Fixed — `messages send` printed a green tick for messages that would never be dispatched

The Pub/Sub notification was gated on the SENDER's local config
(`cfg, _ := messaging.LoadConfig()` — error discarded), and the cloud coordinator's intake is
Pub/Sub ONLY: `pollAndProcessTasksCloud` reads the Pub/Sub adapter and never queries Firestore.
So a send to a GCP store from a machine with no `pubsub` config wrote the document, published
nothing, and printed `✓ Message sent` — the message was filed and invisible to the coordinator
permanently, not slowly. Measured 2026-08-31: three `pkg:sunholo/ailang_parse` reports sat unread
with no task and no job, while the agent for that inbox WAS correctly registered.

`send` now reports the config error instead of discarding it, and prints an actionable
FILED, NOT DISPATCHED warning naming the inbox, the project and the exact config block to add.
Local and hybrid stores stay silent by design — that daemon polls the store, so no notification is
required for work to start. Verified end to end after enabling the config: publish → push →
Cloud Run cold start → `received 1 message(s) from Pub/Sub` → routed, in 23 seconds.


### Fixed — session capture: a tool event whose session predates the server was dropped for the life of that session

`session_tools` carries a FOREIGN KEY onto `sessions`, and only the `SessionStart` hook created the
parent row. Any Claude Code session already running when `ailang server` starts therefore had EVERY
tool event rejected with `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` — while `/api/hooks/claude` still answered
`200 {"status":"ok"}`, so nothing upstream could tell capture from loss. Measured on this laptop:
`session_tools` held **2** rows, both from 2026-06-16, against 6,508 sessions. `PreToolUse` now
upserts the parent session first (`ensureSessionForHook`); the upsert is idempotent and COALESCEs the
coordinator's correlation ids, so it cannot overwrite what `SessionStart` recorded. Verified live —
the session making this change went from 0 to 21 captured tool events with start/end times.
Regression arms: the ensure-session upsert, correlation passthrough via the `X-Ailang-*` headers, and
the no-`cwd` boundary (skip rather than invent a workspace).

### Fixed — Firestore chains reported "0 stages" however many stages they held

`ObservatoryStore.UpdateStageStatus` never incremented the parent chain's `stages_completed`, while
the SQLite store does (`store_chains.go`). This backend's `ListChains` REPORTS that denormalized
field rather than counting stage documents, so every cloud chain read as empty: measured 99 of 99
prod chains at `stage_count=0` while `obs_chain_stages` held their stages with real agent ids, costs
and statuses. A completed stage now increments the counter via `firestore.Increment(1)`, matching the
SQLite path. A counter failure is returned named rather than swallowed, so it cannot silently
reappear as a zero.


### Fixed — the ollama tool-less path routes through /api/generate: schema-enforced local calls drop from ~17s to ~0.9s

`std/ai.callJson` against a local ollama model used `/api/chat` (the api lib's `Chat`). On the rig
(ollama 0.33.1, gemma4:e4b) the byte-identical schema-enforced request takes 15-26s via `/api/chat`
and ~0.6s via `/api/generate`, with server-side runner time ~1-2s in both cases — the gap is in
ollama's chat-endpoint scheduling. A public report (fb_2dbfd79dbe2d1d3c) diagnosed the symptom as a
dropped `format` schema; a wire capture disproved that, but reproduced the latency. `Client.Generate`
now builds an `ollamaapi.GenerateRequest` (Prompt/System carry the same fields; options, seed, num_ctx
and the `format` schema passthrough are unchanged), measured end-to-end warm: 17.6s → 0.9s.
The tool paths (Step, streaming Step) still use `/api/chat` — multi-turn messages and tool
definitions cannot be expressed on `/api/generate`. New wire-shape regression test
(`TestClient_Generate_WireShape`) pins the endpoint, the verbatim schema passthrough, and the
prompt/system mapping.

### Added — std/fs renameFile + renameFileResult: atomic file publish
Pure AILANG can now publish a file atomically: write a temp file, then
`renameFile("run.json.tmp", "run.json")`. POSIX readers of the destination see either the
old or the new file, never a partial one (non-Unix platforms: best-effort replacement, per
Go's `os.Rename` documentation). Cross-filesystem renames surface `EXDEV` — there is no
copy fallback. Both paths are sandbox-resolved when `AILANG_FS_SANDBOX` is set, so rename
cannot smuggle a path across the sandbox boundary. The Result-returning
`renameFileResult` wraps fs failures as `Err(string)` for agent runtimes that recover
instead of crashing. Closes the gap where agents writing `run.json`-style files had to
shell out to `mv` via `Process` (#897).

### Added — `ailang pi install`: the binary distributes the pi extension suite

The ailang binary now embeds the pi extension suite (`pi_assets`) and ships
`ailang pi install|uninstall|status` (mirroring `ailang editor install vscode`).
Install materializes the suite into `~/.pi/agent/extensions/` (global — every
repo on the machine) under a managed-file contract: a `.ailang-managed.json`
manifest records what ailang installed; user-modified and unmanaged files are
never clobbered (our version is offered under `.ailang-suggested/`);
uninstall keeps modified files. `ailang pi status` reports per-file freshness.
Binary users get the suite with zero extra installs; upgrades ride the binary.
The Cloud Run fleet images gain it via `RUN ailang pi install` (closes the
M-DX-PI-HARNESS F5 gap). `make pi-assets` / `make verify-pi-assets` keep the
embedded copy in sync with `.pi/extensions/` (the source of truth).

### Added — mechanical session-protocol gate + dev-harness extensions for pi

A project-local pi extension suite (`.pi/extensions/`, ships via git) now covers both AILANG
work modes:

- **Governance + attribution**: `session-protocol-gate.ts` blocks `edit`/`write` (absolutely) and fail-closes
  `bash` to a read-only allowlist until `session_protocol_ack` succeeds — human `confirm` keypress
  in TUI, verifiable protocol steps in session history in headless mode. Ack persists across
  `/resume`, fails closed on ambiguity. Fail-open register F1–F8 signed in the design doc.
  Allowed `git commit` commands are additionally attributed with
  `Co-Authored-By: pi (<provider>/<model-id>)` — per-harness AND per-model, distinguishing pi
  commits from Claude Code's (Claude trailers) and the human's (own account).
- **Repo-side guards** (Stream A): `binary-freshness` (FRESH/STALE/DIRTY/UNKNOWN vs HEAD,
  fail-closed), `sprint-steward` (mechanical constrained-modification on sprint JSONs),
  `unowned-dirty` (git-authority warning for cross-agent sweeps), `builtin-sprint`
  (golden + **stdlib freeze** + verify + doctor ceremony — the freeze step exists because the
  repo has TWO stdlib snapshot mechanisms and missing the second turned dev CI red).
- **Quota management**: `provider-quota.ts` (`quota_report` tool + `/quota`) queries the
  OpenRouter `/api/v1/key` endpoint (documented in CLAUDE.md) for live budget headroom —
  thresholds CRITICAL ≥95%, WARN ≥80% — plus ollama local status and the current session's
  provider lane. Discovered during enablement of the OpenRouter lane: the shared key was at
  $197.53/$200 (98.8%). Ollama-cloud quota has no public API (reported as unknown).
- **Language tools** (Stream B): `ailang_check` returns structured {code,message,file,line,col,hint}
  diagnostics; `builtins_search` filters the real builtin inventory. Both wrap the `ailang` CLI
  under a bounded-wait Subprocess Contract — no bash parsing, no hangs.

All extensions wrap — never re-implement — the `ailang` CLI, and are unit-tested via node
strip-types (dotfile tests, discovery-skipped).

### Changed — git subprocesses begin migrating to one absolute-path resolver

Added the concurrency-safe `internal/gitexec` resolver, which caches successful resolution but
retries after failure, and migrated the four Sonar-flagged calls plus the stale-binary probe.
A new AST-based CI ratchet inventories the remaining bare-name calls, checks its own fixtures,
and reports the intentionally uncovered variable, shell, syscall, and non-Go classes.
The coordinator sweep now routes all 42 remaining Git subprocesses through that resolver while
preserving every command argument, working-directory convention, and existing error path. The
ratchet consequently drops from 89 bare-name sites to 47.

### Changed — prompt freeze integrity now covers every registry entry

`ailang prompt freeze --check` now validates hash enforceability, source-file existence and bytes,
mirror-file existence and bytes, and mirror-only registry entries for mutable as well as frozen
prompt versions. Missing source and mirror files are named integrity violations instead of generic
I/O aborts, including compound corruptions where both applicable violations are reported. Completed
checks now print `checked N registry entries` to stdout so registry enumeration is observable, and
`--migrate` refuses a stale active-entry hash before writing either registry.

### Fixed — an omitted OpenAI/OpenRouter `usage` block is now distinguishable from an all-zero one

A provider failure can arrive on the wire as a *successful empty completion*: `finish_reason:"stop"`,
`"content":null`, and no `usage` key at all. `ParseChatStepResponse` returned no error, empty text,
zero tool calls and zero tokens — indistinguishable from a model that legitimately said nothing.

The reason no guard could be written for it was the representation: `ChatStepResponse.Usage` was a
value type, so an absent `usage` key unmarshalled to the zero struct and `absent.Usage == allZero.Usage`
was **true**. `Usage` is now `*ChatStepUsage`, so absence (nil) and present-but-all-zero (non-nil,
zeroed) are finally different values.

This is a representation change only — `ParseChatStepResponse` returns exactly what it returned before
for every input, a nil `Usage` yielding the same zeros the zero struct did. Deciding a *policy* from the
distinction is deliberately deferred: the Anthropic and Gemini paths do not share this parser and
streaming usage delivery is opt-in on some providers, so a uniform guard would turn a legitimate empty
completion into an error — the same defect pointed the other way. The standing negative control for any
future guard is a legitimate tool call, which also carries `content:null` and *does* report usage.

Blast radius is wider than the report suggested: `internal/ai/ollama` builds an `openai` client against
`<endpoint>/v1`, so every ollama tool-calling turn parses through this path too. Reported at issue #842.

### Added — `std/string` gains total character accessors

`charAt` was the only partial accessor in the stdlib that aborts instead of returning `Option`:
`head`, `nth`, `stringToInt`, `asString` and `bytes.slice` are all total. Its type —
`(string, int) -> string` — gives no hint, so it reads as safe.

Reported externally against `v0.34.0-57-g816bc80d3`, and the reported failure mode is worse than
the crash: the reporter's absent date fields arrived as `""`, `charAt` aborted mid-`map`, and
because an abort exits non-zero their negative tests — which assert rejection *by* a non-zero
exit — all stayed green while the program crashed on every input. A panicking accessor collapses
"rejected correctly" and "crashed" for any harness that grades on exit code.

```ailang
charAtOpt(s: string, i: int) -> Option[string]        -- None out of range, incl. charAtOpt("", 0)
charAt_or(s: string, i: int, default: string) -> string
```

Both are rune-indexed and agree with `charAt` on every in-range index. `charAt` is unchanged in
behaviour, but its doc comment now states the abort and the exit-code consequence, so
`ailang docs std/string` carries the warning. Whether `charAt` itself should become total is a
breaking change deferred to a prompt-version boundary — see
`design_docs/planned/v0_35_0/m-partial-accessor-shape.md`, decision D1.

Interfaces re-frozen deliberately (`make freeze-stdlib`); `string` was the only module to drift.

### Fixed — `ailang verify` reports the functions it did not verify

`verify` skipped contract-less functions with a bare `continue`, so they never entered `results`
and never entered the total. A module with 25 exports of which 11 carried contracts reported
`11 functions: 11 verified` — true, with a denominator chosen to make it true — and said nothing
about the other 14.

```
  ✓ VERIFIED compute  21.7ms
  ○ NO CONTRACTS main

  2 exported functions: 1 verified, 1 without contracts
```

`--json` gains `uncontracted` and `total_exported`. Names are listed so a function that was meant
to carry a contract and silently lost one is auditable without diffing source. This is the metric
the teaching prompt asks agents to maximise ("maximize the surface area of verified code"), which
the tool previously refused to report the denominator of.

`--strict` is deliberately unchanged: an uncontracted function is not a verification failure, and
counting it would turn `--strict` red on every module with a helper.

### Fixed — `ailang docs` attributes descriptions to the right function, and documents types

Two defects in the stdlib doc renderer, both reported against `std/process`:

- The description was **assigned** on every comment line, so an export got the *last* line of its
  comment block rather than its summary. `exec`'s block ends with the `}` closing the `match` in
  its own example, so `exec` documented itself as `}`; `spawnProcess` documented itself as a call
  to `closeProcessStdin`. The first summary line now wins, blank lines terminate a block, and
  `-- =====` banners cannot become a description.
- Only `export func` was matched, so **no exported type in any stdlib module was documented**.
  `ProcessOutput` — whose `.stdout` is `bytes`, not `string` — was discoverable only by printing
  the record and reading a hex dump. `ailang docs` now renders `## Types` ahead of `## Exports`,
  preserving multi-line record and sum-type shapes.

`parseModuleFile` moved from `bufio.Scanner` to an index-based scan: both the multi-line
`export func` signature and the multi-line `export type` declaration need lookahead, and a
Scanner can only over-read.

### Fixed — `ailang prompt --version` explains the version namespace instead of "not found"

`ailang prompt --version 0.34.0` on a `v0.34.0` binary answered
`version "0.34.0" not found in versions.json`, which reads as eighteen minor versions of drift.
It is a category error: prompt versions are their own curated series and have never tracked the
binary's release version. The request still fails — serving a version other than the one asked
for, even with a warning, is the silent fallback `CLAUDE.md` §2 prohibits — but now says why,
names the active version, and points at `--list`. The namespace paragraph appears only when the
requested version sorts above every prompt on record, so an in-series typo does not collect the
hint as noise.

`ailang prompt --help` no longer hardcodes `v0.16.0` as the active version, and no longer claims
each prompt "corresponds to a specific AILANG language version" — the claim behind the confusion.

### Fixed — the teaching prompt's multi-param lambda advice works for inline callbacks

The "What AILANG Does NOT Have" table offered only `func(a: T, b: U) -> R { body }`, which cannot
be used as an inline `foldl` callback — so an agent following it lands in
`expected '.' after lambda parameter`. The row now names the curried form `\a. \b. body`,
verified by running `foldl(\acc. \x. ..., 0, xs)` rather than by inspection. `v0.16.6` is the
single mutable registry entry; both `versions.json` hashes are updated and the embedded mirror
re-synced.

Not fixed here: the multi-line lambda error's *location*. The parser emits the correct
diagnostic first and then buries it under a cascade — that is
[#934](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/934), not a lambda-specific defect.


### Fixed — the served teaching prompt now identifies its own version

`ailang prompt` serves the active version `v0.16.6`, but line 1 of that file read
`# AILANG v0.16.2`, so the highest-leverage document in the toolchain misidentified itself to
every agent reading it. Reported externally against `v0.34.0-57-g816bc80d3`; reproduced at
`v0.34.0-114-gcaea1f9e1`. The served bytes were always the correct `v0.16.6` ones — the
reporter's "serves v0.16.2" framing was wrong on mechanism — but the header was not.

The registry `hash` for `v0.16.6` is updated in both `prompts/versions.json` and
`cmd/ailang/prompts/versions.json`. That update is mandatory because
`internal/eval_harness/prompt_loader.go` hash-verifies *mutable* versions too; note the freeze
gate itself cannot see a stale hash, so `make check-prompt-freeze` is not the instrument here.

Only `v0.16.6` was touched. It is the single mutable registry entry (58 of 59 are frozen) and
carries zero banked corpus citations across 17,343 baseline files, which is what licenses
editing it in place. The other 14 header mismatches are on **frozen** files and are deliberately
left alone: their headers are historical artifacts, and editing them is precisely the violation
`make check-prompt-freeze` exists to catch.

### Fixed — `create_prompt_version.sh` no longer propagates the previous version's header

The script copied the base prompt and never rewrote line 1, which is how 15 of 54 prompt files
came to misidentify themselves (`v0.16.3`/`v0.16.4`/`v0.16.5`/`v0.16.6` all claimed `v0.16.2`).
It now rewrites the version token after the copy and before the hash is computed, and **refuses
loudly** rather than emitting a mislabelled prompt. The drifted `.agents/` twin of the script,
which had never received the earlier mirror-write fix, is brought byte-identical.


### Added — frozen prompt CI gate

`make check-prompt-freeze`, wired both into `make ci` and as its own required CI step, rejects
edits to prompt versions that were
already frozen at the `origin/dev` merge-base. It also verifies that each frozen prompt's source
bytes, embedded mirror bytes, and marker-carrying registry entries agree.

### Fixed — the injected `AGENTS.md` no longer lands in every agent PR

`injectAgentsMD` writes the harness's own instruction file ("AILANG Cloud Agent Instructions") into
the workspace so the agent knows how to behave. It was untracked and unignored, so the commit step
swept it up: four ailang-parse PRs (#26, #27, #28, #30) each carried an identical
`AGENTS.md +60/-0`, and **two carried nothing else**.

That is worse than an empty PR. A run which produced no work looked like a change, so "the agent did
nothing" was hidden behind a plausible diff — the reason these read as "empty PRs being made" rather
than "agents silently failing".

The injected copy is now added to `.git/info/exclude` before the agent runs. `info/exclude` rather
than `.gitignore` because `.gitignore` is tracked content, so writing to it would itself be a change
the agent commits — the very problem being fixed. The git dir is resolved with
`git rev-parse --git-dir`, since in a worktree `.git` is a file and the naive path would silently
write somewhere git never reads.

A repo that ships its **own** AGENTS.md is untouched: injection already skips, and only the copy we
inject is excluded.

**The cascade-only guard is deleted.** It skipped injection when `AILANG_CASCADE_ROOT_PACKAGE` was
set, because "AGENTS.md ... just clutters the cascade PR" — true, and equally true of every other
PR. It fixed one caller and left the rest committing the file (CLAUDE.md Principle 3).
### Removed — `model_routing` from the coordinator config; the registry resolves roles now

M-MODEL-REGISTRY-SINGLE-SOURCE M7, superseding M-PIPELINE-RECONCILIATION M5.

`coordinator.ResolveModel(agent)` no longer takes a routing table — an explicit `model:` pin wins,
else the agent's `role:` resolves through `internal/modelreg` on the cloud lane, else `("", nil)`
and M6's executor-side check handles it at execution. A role the registry does not know is loud and
names the roles it does.

- `internal/coordinator/model_routing.go` → `model_resolution.go`; the `ModelRouting` type and the
  `model_routing` config key are gone. This is the coordinator's **first** dependency on the
  registry (V7 flagged the cycle risk), safe only because M1 made `modelreg` a leaf.
- `ailang coordinator routing` is retired but **forwards** to `ailang models role` with a note,
  rather than 404-ing on a table that no longer exists. Output shape differs — friendly name, wire
  string, harness — so parsers must read field 2.
- The deletion was proven inert *before* it was made, and the proof was then strengthened: the
  fixture originally exercised **zero** role resolutions (all 34 agents carry pins, which win), so
  it now drops the pin from each role-bearing agent and requires the registry to name exactly what
  the table named — 4 verified, with a negative control.
### Removed — the ten hardcoded model defaults in `internal/executor` (D2(a))

M-MODEL-REGISTRY-SINGLE-SOURCE M6. No executor names a model any more.

The defect was **ten** literals in **two layers**, not five: `factory.go`'s `DefaultConfig()` seeded
`ClaudeModel`/`CodexModel`/`OpenCodeModel`/`PiModel`/`MotokoModel`, and each executor then
re-defaulted the same values if its field was still empty. Removing only the per-executor half
would have been a no-op. Four of the ten were `claude-haiku-4-5`: an agent whose pin was dropped
silently ran a model nobody chose, on an account nobody was watching — invisible until an invoice
or an outage.

**Correction (same day).** An earlier draft of this entry said the fleet moved off Anthropic
"because Claude-CLI OAuth for headless agents is being retired". That is wrong. Anthropic announced
that change for 2026-06-15 and **paused it that day**; `claude -p` still draws on subscription
limits and the separate API-rate credit was never issued. Cloud Run jobs also **do** run Claude on
subscription OAuth, via `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in Secret Manager. The defect never depended on
that premise.

- Unresolvable model ⇒ typed `UnresolvedModelError` naming the agent, the config field, and the
  roles the registry knows.
- **The check is at execution entry, not construction.** A first cut failed at construction and
  broke the coordinator, which builds an executor *before* it knows the task and supplies
  `Task.Model` per task. `requireModel` now runs first in `Execute`/`ExecuteStreaming`, where both
  sources of a model are known.
- **Path defaults survive; model defaults do not.** Finding `codex` or `motoko` on PATH is a lookup
  that decides nothing; choosing a model decides billing and availability.
- New guard test scans all of `internal/executor/` **including `factory.go`** — a guard scoped to
  the five subpackages would have passed while the upstream half kept every unpinned agent on
  Anthropic. Verified by reintroducing a literal and watching it fail.
### Added — `ailang models` and registry publishing (D5)

M-MODEL-REGISTRY-SINGLE-SOURCE M4b + M7 (decision D5, ratified by Mark 2026-08-27).

- **`ailang models role <role> [--lane local|cloud]`** — the ordered chain, one row per entry as
  `friendly-name<TAB>wire-string<TAB>harness`. Chain goes to **stdout**, the provenance line to
  **stderr**, so the mission driver (D3(a)) can read field 2 without parsing around a log line.
- **`ailang models source`** — which registry this binary is using, on demand.
- **`ailang models publish [--dry-run]`** — validates and CAS-writes the registry to the config
  bucket as `registry.yml`.

**Why a separate object terraform never declares (D5)**: `config.cloud.yaml` is repo-canonical in
ailang-multivac and uploaded by `google_storage_bucket_object.coordinator_config`. Because
terraform *owns* that object, direct writes to it are reverted on the next config push — that is
what clobbered two writes on 2026-08-26. `models.yml` is canonical in this repo, so it publishes to
an object outside terraform's state: never reverted, and never a second copy that can drift.
Accepted consequence: it is the one config-bucket artifact `terraform apply` cannot recreate; the
embedded floor covers an outage and re-publishing is manual.

`writeRegistryCAS` deliberately does **not** reuse `writeConfigCAS` — that one validates
coordinator-config invariants (agent ids, inbox uniqueness) a registry does not have and would fail
on. Same shape, different schema; a validator passing the wrong document type is worse than none.
### Added — registry precedence inversion, provenance, and publish validation

M-MODEL-REGISTRY-SINGLE-SOURCE M3 + M4 (decisions D1(a), D3(a) ratified by Mark 2026-08-27).

- **`roles:` in models.yml** — model POLICY beside model FACTS, superseding `model_routing` in
  `config.cloud.yaml`. Chains name FRIENDLY NAMES, not wire strings: the live routing entries map
  back to the registry many-to-one (`openrouter/deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` is both the motoko
  and the opencode twin) and are not even derived uniformly (`gpt-5.6-sol` comes from `api_name`,
  since the codex row has no `agent_model_name`). Chains were transcribed byte-identically from
  the live table; changing which models a role runs is an explicit Non-Goal.
- **`ResolveRole(role, lane)`** — ordered chain with each entry's harness and exact wire string.
  Loud on an unknown role, an unresolvable name, and a chain emptied by lane filtering.
- **Precedence inverted (the D1 defect)**: `InitModelsConfig` used to try the embedded registry
  FIRST, which is why a published registry was ignored by every installed binary and adopting a
  model needed a release. Order is now `AILANG_MODELS_PATH` → published (gcsfuse mount) → embedded
  floor. A broken published registry degrades to the floor but never silently — `Source.Degraded`
  names the rejected path.
- **Provenance line at startup** — `models registry: source=… version=… path=…`, emitted from one
  place inside the loader rather than at the six call sites. Version is a content digest, since
  models.yml carries no version field.
- **`Validate()`** gates publishing and covers **pricing**, not only roles. D1(a) was ratified with
  the cost accepted that pricing now reaches cost accounting without a rebuild; a validator that
  waved it through would guard the new feature and leave the newly-exposed surface open. Zero
  pricing stays legal — local ollama rows are genuinely free.
### Changed — `internal/modelreg`: the model registry becomes a leaf package

M-MODEL-REGISTRY-SINGLE-SOURCE M1 (decision D4(a)). `models.go` and `models.yml` moved from
`internal/eval_harness` to a new `internal/modelreg`, which imports no other ailang package.

**Why a move rather than a new file.** The sprint's later milestones need executors to resolve
their model through the registry. `internal/eval_harness` already imports `internal/executor` for
ten symbols (`Task`, `Result`, `EventHandler`, the cost classes, `ValidateTaskCapabilities`), so a
registry living there would have closed the cycle `executor → eval_harness → executor`, which does
not compile. The dependency is structural and cannot be inverted, so the registry moved down.

- `IsOllamaCloudRoute`'s string grammar moved from `internal/executor/cost.go` **down** into
  `modelreg`; `executor` now delegates to it. Still exactly one implementation.
- `eval_harness` keeps type/function aliases (`models_alias.go`), so the ~66 call sites naming
  `ModelsConfig`, `LoadModelsConfig`, `ResolveModelName` and friends are unchanged.
- `GlobalModelsConfig` is deliberately **not** aliased. It is a mutable package variable that
  `InitModelsConfig` writes; a second declaration would have been a stale copy of the pointer. Its
  ~40 call sites now name `modelreg.GlobalModelsConfig` — one variable, one source.
- 40 path references to `internal/eval_harness/models.yml` across tools, scripts and skills were
  updated to `internal/modelreg/models.yml`.
- New `TestModelregIsALeaf` guards the boundary in the window before the compiler can (an import
  cycle only becomes a build error once `executor` imports `modelreg` in M6/M7).

### Added — two day-one OS model registrations: GLM-5.3-Flash and Qwen3.8-Flash-Next

Both released 2026-08-26, registered 2026-08-27 as opt-in standard-mode rows (no suite
promotion — that is a `--tier core` decision against the incumbents, not yet run):

- **`or-glm-5-3-flash`** (`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, $0.075/$0.25 per 1M): 320B-A18B MIT,
  previewed as Ox Alpha. Thinking probed `always_on` (explicit disable is an API error —
  same taxonomy as GLM-5.2). **Smoke gate PASSED**: 22/23 first pass at $0.055 for the
  whole tier, zero truncation; the sole failure (adt_option, runtime "expected float
  arguments" — the known Int/Fractional crash class) passed 2/2 on immediate retry.
  **Core head-to-head vs `or-glm-5-2` (same day, one command, AILANG): TIE after N=3
  de-flake** — 22/23 vs 23/23 at N=1 with the only discordant cell (ast_patch_roundtrip,
  a missing-import slip) going 3/3 PASS for both models on escalation; zero both-fail
  cells, zero truncation. Cost: **$0.072 vs $0.920 for the tier ($0.0033 vs $0.0400 per
  pass, ~12x)**. **Frontier N=3** (second discriminator): 8/24 vs 7/24 at $0.155 vs
  $2.772 — 4 vs 3 discordant trial cells, sign test **p=1.000, indistinguishable**; both
  sit well below the frontier ceiling (5/8, Opus 5 / Fable 5), and both fail
  `docx_reimplement`/`markdown_reimplement` 0/3 on the fleet-wide standard-mode
  `input_files` harness gap. **ELO 1994.8 (provisional, n=31), rank 9 of 19** — above the
  retired `or-glm-5-2` (1910.2), below `claude-sonnet-5` (2022.3). **Ratified: replaces
  `or-glm-5-2` in `extended_suite`** (5.2 retired to opt-in); `agent_suite` unchanged
  pending an `opencode-` twin.
- **`or-qwen3-8-flash`** (`qwen/qwen3.8-flash`, $0.16/$0.47 per 1M): the open-weight
  **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next** (Qwen4-architecture preview, 125B-A6B multimodal MoE); Qwen
  serves the production route under the plain `qwen3.8-flash` name, and the OpenRouter
  pricing matches the announcement exactly, pinning the identity. Thinking probed `on`
  (51 reasoning tokens by default, disableable). **Smoke gate DEFERRED**: the sole
  provider (Alibaba) 429'd roughly half of day-one requests — a first smoke attempt
  banked pure `api_error` rows and was aborted rather than recorded as capability data.

### Fixed — 21 drifted OpenRouter pricing rows repriced to the live catalogue (2026-08-27)

`make verify-model-pricing` found 21 `provider: openrouter` rows stale, 40 rate values in
all. Most drift was downward (vendor price cuts — banked costs overstated, benign), but
two rows **understated** real spend: `or-glm-5-2`/`opencode-or-glm-5-2` (input 2.38x —
z.ai's promo rate ended) and `or-deepseek-v4-pro` (2.58x/3.87x). Rates corrected going
forward; banked results NOT recomputed, per the pricing banner. The six
`opencode-or-glm-5-2` rows banked 2026-08-22 inside the repricing uncertainty window are
annotated in `eval_results/baseline_v1_0/CAVEATS.md` (v0.30.0 precedent).
### fmt — M3: the second corpus reformat (M-FMT-PRINTER-LINE-WIDTH-LIMIT complete)

- **50 `.ail` files reformatted** under the width predicate landed by M0/M1/M1b/M2. Corpus
  lines over 120 runes: **159 -> 100** (design target <= 105); longest line **1315 -> 316**
  (target <= 350). `LET_CHAIN_2PLUS` residual closed completely (20 -> 0).
- **Nothing else moved.** Comment totals unchanged (7,865 -> 7,865 over 450 joined pairs,
  with a poisoned control that fires); `ailang check` return codes unchanged on all 450
  files (374 rc=0 / 76 rc=1 both sides); the 45 fail-closed files (38 attach-refusals,
  7 parse-failures) are byte-identical; a second `fmt --write` pass is a no-op.
- The per-construct residual table is published in the design doc and is the input to the
  follow-on reflow-engine decision. `IMPORT`, `STRING_DOM` and `RECORD_LIST` are untouched
  at 10 lines each by design — import-list wrapping needs a new canonical form plus a new
  attach list, and is deferred with the typedecl work.
- Design doc and sprint plan moved to `design_docs/implemented/v0_35_0/`.

### Tested — three unpinned hunks in `internal/format`, pinned before M2 extends them

Two judge-sourced findings (iterations 285 and 287) said the width-measurement code shipped
hunks that nothing exercised. Both reproduced first-party: mutant `att: nil` &rarr; `att: p.att`
and mutant neutering both `if p.measurementErr != nil` guards each **LANDED, BUILT and left the
entire `internal/format` suite green**.

- **Attachment isolation is now pinned.** `TestMeasurementIgnoresInheritedAttachments` compares
  the isolated measurement against a hand-built inheriting shadow, so it observes the value the
  mechanism writes rather than one set alongside it. It is the mutant's **sole killer**.
- **The `measurementErr` redundancy is now measured, not reasoned, and declared.**
  `TestMeasurementErrorAlwaysAccompaniesRenderError` fault-injects `ast.Error` at **312** corpus
  sites across widths {120,40,20} and asserts the joint cell `measurementErr && p.file()==nil`
  stays empty &mdash; it does, 312/312, with a pristine control (1329 renders, 0 `measurementErr`)
  proving the instrument alive. The guards are **kept and declared**, not deleted: an unreachable
  branch is acceptable when declared, and these were not.
- **A third, previously unnamed hunk is pinned.** `interp.go:167-169` discards `sub.expr`'s error
  without setting `measurementErr`; dropping `p.measurementErr = err` at `width.go:46` was itself
  unpinned and is now sole-killed.

Production changes are **comments only** &mdash; corpus-neutral, verified by compiled-product sweep:
405 formattable files byte-identical, **0 divergent**, 45 fail-closed, against a known-positive
control (`defaultMaxWidth` 120&rarr;60) that produces **21** divergences on the same sweep.


### Fixed — the driver pin refused unconditionally, because its onboarding key no longer exists

`tools/launchd/lib/pin-root.sh` gated the launchd driver pin on
`~/.claude.json`'s `.projects[<path>].hasCompletedProjectOnboarding`. Claude Code has since
retired that key. Measured 2026-08-26 on the rig, three readings in one call: the legacy key
appears in **0 of 15** live project entries (whole-file grep: 0), `hasTrustDialogAccepted`
in **15 of 15** (positive control fires), an invented key in **0** (negative control fires).
So the predicate returned `false` for every path, the "neither path onboarded" branch was
unconditionally true, and **every fire of every mission refused to pin** — running whatever the
source clone's working tree happened to hold. Simulated across all three missions before the fix:
motoko, v1 and world all `REFUSE-TO-PIN`. That is the class issue #558 exists to prevent,
reinstated by the gate written for #558.

The suite could not catch it: `test_pin_root.sh` writes its own synthetic `~/.claude.json`
fixtures still carrying the retired key, and one arm actively **asserted that the only key which
now exists must be refused** — a green gate pinning the production defect as correct.

- `_pin_onboarded` now accepts either the legacy key or `hasTrustDialogAccepted`.
- **Anti-vacuity floor**: when `.projects` is non-empty and *neither* key appears in *any* entry,
  the gate reports Claude Code **schema drift** — a distinct, fail-closed diagnosis naming both
  keys — instead of telling the human to onboard a directory that is already onboarded. The next
  rename is loud on its first fire rather than silent forever.
- **Three refusals, three sentences.** The evaluator found the drift diagnosis was also emitted
  for a missing, malformed or non-object `~/.claude.json` — telling the next reader the *gate*
  needs a new key when the *file* is broken, which is this gate's own original defect one level
  down. Unreadable now gets its own sentence; an empty `.projects` map routes to the ordinary
  "nothing onboarded" refusal. All three stay fail-closed; only the diagnosis moves.
- Suite 35 → 53 arms. The drift and unreadable arms assert the message TEXT, not just
  `STATUS=STALE` — every refusal produces STALE, so status alone cannot tell them apart, which
  was the whole defect.

After the fix, simulated against the real `~/.claude.json`: motoko `PIN-OK`, v1 `PIN-OK`,
world still `REFUSE-TO-PIN` — a true verdict, since that clone genuinely carries neither key.

### Fixed — `ailang fmt` had no line-width limit, and then briefly had one that miscounted (M0, M1, M1b)

`ailang fmt` collapsed multi-line bodies onto one line with no width limit at all: across the 342
files reformatted for `D-38`, the longest line went 267 → 1315 chars and lines over 120 chars went
57 → 147. Three commits, and the third exists because the first two shipped a green suite over a
live violation of the feature's own central property.

- **M0** (`f7bae93b2`): width plumbing plus a mode-locked measurement printer — a `measuring` flag
  and a `measurementErr` channel, so a width probe can never recurse into a second printer.
- **M1** (`da885e1e8`): widened the existing multi-line emitter's gate. The emitter already existed
  (`letChainMultiline`); it was reached only via `p.hasAnyAttachment(n)`, i.e. a chain collapsed onto
  one line **regardless of length** unless it happened to carry a comment. A predicate widening,
  not a new layout engine.
- **M1b**: the width predicate read a column the indentation had not reached yet. `hardline()` sets
  `atBOL` and resets `col` to 0, **deferring** the indent runes to the next `write()` — so
  `exceedsWidth`, consulted at beginning-of-line, undercounted by exactly `depth × runewidth(indent)`
  and emitted a **122-rune line under `MaxWidth=120`**. Fixed with `writer.effectiveCol()`. Measured
  on the corpus: 43 of 61 `letIn` width decisions are taken at BOL, so this was the common path, not
  a corner. Byte-neutral on all 405 formattable files (sha256 over path+contents identical either
  side of the fix).

The round-1 test for that site could not have caught it: it faked the column with
`p.w.write(strings.Repeat(" ", n))`, which flushes `atBOL` and sidesteps the whole mechanism. The
replacement drives a real `hardline()` path and derives its boundary from the emitter's own wide
rendering rather than a hardcoded pad. Known residual: over-long lines remain that only continuation
layout (M2) can reach, and 45 corpus files fail closed.

### Added — M-MESSAGE-PLANE-FAIL-LOUD: the message plane's silent seams now say so (4 milestones)

A 2026-08-26 triage found 41 feedback tickets back to 2026-04-27, the newest 19 never seen —
and every contributing defect was a component that was alive, reporting healthy, and doing
nothing. Four milestones, each ratified by Mark (D1 fatal exit, D2 explicit triage_only,
D3 explicit execution_lane):

- **M1** (`879865a8e`): a coordinator that cannot initialize task processing **exits non-zero**
  instead of idling in "standby mode" — measured 20 standby vs 11 healthy startups since
  2026-05-25 on the rig. `NewWorktreeManager` now validates a *supplied* repo path at
  construction instead of failing on every later poll.
- **M2** (`0b817ab5d`): `triage_only_inboxes` config — an unrouted inbox is now either a
  *declared* human-triage inbox or a logged **CONFIG GAP**; the third, ambiguous state is gone.
- **M3** (`f2b5599a7`): `execution_lane: local|cloud` + a separate `repo` coordinate, ending
  `workspace`'s double life as both a worktree path and a GitHub coordinate (each reading broke
  the other's consumer, both failures measured the same day). A fixture test pins that all 39
  live cloud agents still infer `cloud`.
- **M4** (`d3f4d4c6a`): `ailang coordinator config get|set|diff` — shared-config writes now
  carry a GCS generation precondition; a write built on a stale read is **refused naming both
  generations** (a real lost update was demonstrated the same day: a 14:24:33Z write clobbered
  at 14:37:10Z with no error on either side). Validation (YAML + agent invariants) runs before
  any write; `--force` must name the generation it discards.

### Fixed — four live defects caught by the pipeline's own e2e verification (2026-08-26 evening)

Running the reconciled chain end to end on the rig surfaced, in order:

- **Provider roulette** (`816bc80d3`): local provider selection took the first registered
  provider (registration order varies per process) and ignored the agent's `provider:` config
  entirely — an eval-rig task declared for claude executed under motoko-cli. A declared provider
  now wins by name, and a declared-but-unregistered provider fails loudly instead of silently
  running on another billing lane.
- **Handoffs delivered to nowhere on Firestore** (`5ff60b25e`): `CreateMessage` writes only the
  thread-messages collection; the task poller reads inbox rows. SQLite's tables coincide, so the
  gap became reachable the day the first local-mode coordinator ran on shared storage. Handoffs
  now insert a real inbox row as THE delivery.
- **Coordinator approvals were silent in Discord** (`6b198f457`): `InsertInboxMessage` never
  published the Pub/Sub notification the notify daemon listens for, and local-mode coordinators
  had no publisher at all — approvals were approved blind from a context-free queue (#921).
  Shared-plane coordinators now require a publisher; approval and handoff inserts publish.
  Verified live: `🔔 Approval needed` reached Discord 1s after row creation.
- **Late verdicts were dropped** (`6b198f457`): a human approving while the evaluator was still
  scoring caused a PASS score=95 to be discarded (loudly — the only reason it was seen). Both
  stores now late-attach to the resolved approval, marked "(late: attached after resolution)".

Full-chain proof: task → claude (declared) → approval + Discord ping → per-edge auto-handoff →
evaluator → `PASS score=98` attached to the still-pending approval. Zero Cloud Run executions
across all probes — local-lane isolation held. Payment posture aligned the same evening
(ailang-multivac terraform: metered OpenAI keys removed from pi/opencode/motoko job lanes;
claude lanes verified already subscription-OAuth; codex documented as the explicit metered lane).

### Added — M-PIPELINE-RECONCILIATION: one pipeline definition, two execution lanes (7 milestones)

The mission loop and the coordinator chain were the same four-stage pipeline implemented twice,
diverged for months. Same-day sprint, all D1–D4 ratified (attended):

- **M1/M2**: closed evaluator verdict type (`PASS|FAIL|UNAVAILABLE`, absence unrepresentable) on
  approval records across both stores; per-edge auto-approved executor→evaluator handoff; verdicts
  attach to the parent's pending approval — a DEAD evaluator writes `UNAVAILABLE`, and
  FAIL/UNAVAILABLE block automatic progression but never the human gate.
- **M3**: Lane B gains the discrete `sprint-evaluator` stage (config, CAS-written; plugin PR
  [ailang_bootstrap#6](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang_bootstrap/pull/6)).
- **M4**: `pipelines:` chain-as-data — stage list declared once, projects bind in one line. The
  dormant stapledon/twilight clone sextet is deleted; the expansion-equivalence fixture caught that
  all three twilight entries invoked templates under a path that has never existed on any deployment.
- **M5**: one `model_routing` role→chain table in the CAS-managed config, read by both lanes;
  static opus pins deleted; `ailang coordinator routing <role>` for shell drivers. A role missing
  from the table fails dispatch loudly.
- **M6**: the `approvals` inbox is the single "waiting on Mark" spine — `coordinator pending`
  unions it (its store half is local-SQLite-only and was silently showing a subset), and
  mission-control posts `awaiting_approval` stages there.
- **M7** (from a live incident mid-sprint): a dashboard (cloud) approval of a local-lane task
  resolved the record, hit "worktree missing" — the worktree is on another machine — and returned
  success, stranding the task. The owning coordinator now sweeps approved-but-stranded tasks and
  runs the local finalize with original attribution. The live stranded task was recovered by the
  sweep on its first healthy tick.

Windows CI exposed two real defects in the new code (platform-relative lane inference; a leaked
SQLite handle on the fatal-init path) — fixed in `aaa222743` + `20d01ed5a`.

### Fixed — approval requests reach Discord (`6345f2dc1`)

The `approvals` inbox — the one message class whose entire purpose is a human decision — was
the one human-triage inbox not routed to Discord; a live "Approval Required" sat unread ~3h.
Now delivered as "🔔 Approval needed" with the `ailang coordinator approve <id>` command in the
body. The rig's Pub/Sub *sender* was also still dev-pinned after the daemon moved to prod, so
rig-coordinator approvals published notifications nothing watched; sender moved to prod and the
path verified end-to-end.

### Fixed — cloud prompt templates versioned; stale monorepo assumption removed (`7f5dfac84`)

`tools/cloud-config/templates/` is now the source of truth for the bucket-served pkg-agent
templates. `pkg-feedback.md` (untouched in the bucket since 2026-04-28) asserted every package
lives in the ailang-packages monorepo — false since `ailang_parse` moved to its own repo.

## [v0.34.0] - 2026-08-26

### Fixed — `make test-stdlib-freeze` now delegates to the live stdlib interface gate

`make test-stdlib-freeze` previously aborted at prerequisite resolution with rc=2 because
its `goldens/stdlib` directory has never existed on any ref. The historical target now
delegates to the CI-wired `verify-stdlib` implementation over `.stdlib-golden/`, and the
dead five-module recipe plus its orphan `STDLIB`, `FREEZE_DIR`, and `TOOLS` variables have
been removed. `verify-stdlib-selftest` now also proves that a missing golden is reported
and that the historical alias still reaches the live gate; the missing-golden arm owns an
independent backup and EXIT trap so it restores the moved golden on success and failure.

### Fixed — every pi lane was running at 32,000 output tokens, not the declared 65,536

pi-ai's `buildBaseOptions` (`dist/providers/simple-options.js:4`) computes
`Math.min(model.maxTokens, 32000)` whenever the caller passes no explicit `maxTokens`,
and pi-coding-agent never does for main agent turns. Every provider routes through it, so
the clamp applied to all nine pi lanes regardless of what any config declared. pi exposes
neither a max-tokens flag nor a request hook to raise it.

Proven on the wire rather than inferred from source: declaring 20,000 sent 20,000;
declaring 65,536 sent 32,000 — exactly `min(declared, 32000)`.

The pi rows in `models.yml` and the canonical pi registry now declare the truth. Restoring
65,536 would only reinstate a fiction CI was pinning. Not currently binding: zero
`finish_reason=length` across the whole Broadcast corpus, and the most any pi lane has
ever emitted is 11,602 output tokens. The two `pi-claude-*` rows are a separate case and
are annotated rather than changed — the anthropic path adds a thinking budget on top of
the clamped base, giving 33,024–48,384 depending on thinking level, so no single static
number is right.

`TestPiModelsConfigMatchesRegistry` had claimed to close this gap and did not: neither
file it compares is the wire. Its premise is corrected, and its comparison is now scoped
to pi-lane rows — a model's effective budget is harness-dependent, so pi declaring 32,000
while opencode declares 65,536 for the same slug is correct, not drift.

New `scripts/check_pi_wire_budget.sh` / `make check-pi-wire-budget` asserts against the
actual request, read back from OpenRouter's own Broadcast trace. Deliberately not a CI
gate (real API call). Mutation-drilled: restoring the 65,536 declaration makes it fail
with exactly the original defect.


### Fixed — the unattended mission loop spawned no sub-agents, so five iterations ran with no independent judge

Iterations 278-282 each recorded the same deviation: designer, planner, executor and
**evaluator** were never spawned, because a session-level operating instruction forbids the
Agent tool unless the user requests it. Iteration 281 named the consequence — a formatter
change that silently deleted user comments would have shipped on the controller's own verdict,
and what caught half of it was `TestCorpusCommentGate`, a repo test, not a reviewer. Iteration
282 then landed a 342-file corpus change under the same conditions.

The instruction is not in any local configuration: `~/.claude/settings.json`,
`~/.claude/remote-settings.json`, `~/.claude/policy-limits.json` and the repo's `.claude/`
all match zero times (control: the same grep over `tools/launchd/` finds the driver's own
text). It is harness-injected, so no settings edit reaches it — and the loop could never have
diagnosed it, because the cause was absent from every file the loop can read.

The fix uses the instruction's own escape clause. It reads *"unless the user requested it"*,
and under `claude -p` the driver's `$PROMPT` **is** the user turn, while
`tools/launchd/mission-control.sh` never mentioned the Agent tool at all. The prompt now
carries an explicit standing request to spawn the four roles per the skill's routing table,
states that generator-not-equal-judge is non-negotiable, and requires any un-spawnable role to
be recorded by name with its error rather than passed over silently.

Verification is deferred to the next unattended fire by design: if its routing block still
reads `NOT spawned`, the escape-clause theory is refuted and `D-40` re-opens. `ailang-motoko`
takes this by sync; `ailang-world`'s driver is a hand-synced fork and needs a port.

### Fixed — V1's driver read its GitHub directives from a closed issue

`tools/launchd/mission-control.sh` resolved `GH_ISSUE_FILE` to the fleet-shared bare
`mission-gh-issue` for `MISSION_NAME=v1`, while every sibling mission used the namespaced
`mission-<name>-gh-issue`. Measured on the rig: the bare file holds **745** (CLOSED), the
namespaced `mission-v1-gh-issue` holds **852** (open); motoko and world read 850 and 89
correctly. Gate 0 reads Mark's directives from that value, so the export pointed at a dead
thread and a directive sweep would have reported "0 directives" truthfully and uselessly.

The loop only ever saw real directives because the controller distrusted the env and
re-derived the namespaced path by hand — found and reported by iteration 282, fixed here.
This deliberately breaks the "legacy paths, bit-for-bit compat" rule in that branch, which is
why the exception is commented at the site.
### Fixed — `reasoning_stall` is no longer banked as `api_error` or `compile_error`

A reasoning model can return HTTP 200 with empty content and
`output_tokens == reasoning_tokens`: it engaged with the task, reasoned correctly,
and never answered. The harness handed that empty string to the compiler, so the run
was banked as `compile_error` and attributed to the model's ability to write AILANG.
It now has its own `error_category=reasoning_stall`, detected structurally rather than
from `finish_reason` — which read `length` before 2026-08-13, a clean `stop` after, and
was absent on cancelled runs, so it fired on 0 of 4 real failures.

Measured from OpenRouter Broadcast traces, the provider's own side of the wire: across
the whole 08-18..08-22 corpus, 3 of 173 generations had no `finish_reason` and **all
three** matched the signature, while the other 170 carried content or tool calls. Not
model-specific — `deepseek-v4-flash-0731` under pi and `z-ai/glm-5.2` under OpenCode both
produced it, on different provider hosts.

### Fixed — the pi executor lane's guard was measuring pi's encoding, not the model

The mission pi lane now runs through `scripts/mission_pi_run.sh`, which returns a typed
verdict (`ok` / `empty_worktree` / `reasoning_stall` / `stream_dead` / `wall_timeout`)
instead of `rc=0` for all of them. The previous guard polled the NDJSON file size and
killed at a few hundred MB — but pi's `message_update` replays the whole accumulated
message per delta, so bytes are *quadratic* in emitted tokens. 7,130 reasoning tokens
produced 330 MB; at the declared 65,536-token budget that is ~28 GB. The ceiling had
silently capped the lane at roughly 7,000 reasoning tokens, which is why two earlier
prompt-level anti-runaway fixes changed nothing — they addressed a model problem that
did not exist. Filtering `message_update` out of the banked stream makes size linear and
turns the file's mtime into a progress clock that freezes exactly during a content-free
reasoning turn.

### Documentation

- `docs/docs/guides/debugging.md` gains a section on reading an LLM call from the
  provider's side via OpenRouter Broadcast traces — including how to recover the request
  actually sent on the wire, which is how pi was found still sending
  `max_completion_tokens=32000` against a declared 65,536.


### Added — provider-neutral AI browser sessions

Agent evals can now opt into isolated Playwright MCP browser sessions with
`--browser-provider local-playwright` or `browserbase`. Codex is the first
MCP-capable executor. Connection credentials stay in the child environment,
browser lifecycle and artifacts are banked in a secret-safe manifest, and
cleanup runs after success, error, cancellation, and timeout. The local backend
pins `@playwright/mcp@0.0.79`; the Browserbase adapter has hermetic API tests and
an explicit billable live-contract gate.

### Added — persistent browser auth profiles

Browser sessions can start already authenticated from a named, versioned profile
without any model ever receiving a password or canonical profile material:

```bash
ailang browser-profile bootstrap crm-readonly-eu \
  --state-file ./crm-state.json --origins https://crm.example.com --egress-ack

ailang eval-suite --agent --browser-provider local-playwright \
  --browser-profile crm-readonly-eu@latest
```

Saved browser state is treated as a **credential, not an artifact** — cookies,
local storage, IndexedDB tokens, and virtual passkeys impersonate an account
just as well as the password that produced them. Material is sealed with
AES-256-GCM at rest, materialized to an owner-only `0600` file for exactly one
run, and destroyed on success, error, cancellation, deadline, and panic.
`SanitizeDiagnostics` now redacts storage state, provider context IDs, sealed
blobs, passkeys, and recovery codes alongside the existing markers.

Ordinary runs are always read-mode: they load an immutable snapshot, cannot
persist anything back, and have trace and video recording disabled, because
private page content is as sensitive as the login state. Write-back is a
separate audited operation under an exclusive lease that publishes a **new**
version only after post-login verification.

Every policy field fails closed when unset — no origins means no navigation, an
unset artifact policy denies the session, and an absent egress boundary denies
it outright. `latest` resolves to a concrete version before the run starts, so a
banked result records the identity that actually ran.

No generic, model-driven password filler ships, and the guarantee is enforced
rather than asserted: `SecretValue.Reveal()` is the only path to a raw
credential, and a test fails the build on any unlisted caller in the shipped
tree.

**Authenticated production use remains blocked** on M-BROWSER-EGRESS-BOUNDARY
and M-BROWSER-ARTIFACT-DATA-POLICY; `--egress-ack` records an explicit operator
decision to proceed without enforcement. See
[Browser Auth Profiles](../docs/docs/guides/evaluation/browser-auth-profiles.md).

For local Playwright, canonical state is an AES-256-GCM envelope rather than a
persistent Chrome profile directory. A fresh data key and a fresh nonce are
drawn for every seal, the envelope header is bound into the authentication tag,
and any tampering — ciphertext, nonce, wrapped key, key id, or version — fails
closed as `browser_auth_materialize_failed` with no plaintext returned at all.
The key-management backend stays behind a `KeyProtector` interface; the shipped
implementations are an in-memory one and a clearly-marked single-host key file,
because the durable backend is still a deferred decision.

A run decrypts into a `0600` file inside its own `0700` directory and the
provider passes `--storage-state <path>` alongside `--isolated`, so the path
reaches child argv but the contents and the canonical object reference do not.
The materialization handle owns that directory and destroys it on success,
error, cancellation, timeout, and panic; a cleanup failure is reported as
`browser_auth_cleanup_failed` in its own field so it can never mask why the run
actually failed. A startup orphan audit sweeps materializations that survived a
crash and records one `orphan_audit` event per orphan through the same
`AuditSink` the profile decisions use — with no alias or profile hash on it,
because the materialization path deliberately encodes no profile identity and
the sweep must not invent one.

### Fixed — `ailang fmt` emitted source that could not be re-parsed when a function type took a record parameter

`std/cognition.ail` was valid input (`ailang check` rc=0) whose formatted output failed
to re-parse, and the formatter said so itself: *"formatted output failed to re-parse
(formatter defect)"*. Reduced to two lines:

```ailang
export func f(cb: ({a: string}) -> ()) -> () { g(cb) }
```

The printer drops the parentheses around a function type's sole parameter, because
`int -> int` is the taught spelling and always emitting `(int) -> int` rewrote ten of the
teaching prompt's own examples. `bareArrowSafe` decided when that was legal as a
BLACKLIST — parens were kept for `FuncType` (arrow associativity) and `TupleType`
(read as multiple parameters), and dropped for everything else. A record parameter
therefore emitted `{ a: string } -> ()`, whose leading `{` the parser reads as a BLOCK:
`PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN ... expected ), got -> instead`.

`bareArrowSafe` is now a WHITELIST. `SimpleType`, `TypeVar`, `ListType`, `ArrayType` and
`TypeApp` may drop their parens; `FuncType`, `TupleType` and `RecordType` may not; a
`LabelledType` recurses on its base, since `{a: int}<L>` still opens with `{`; and
anything else defaults to KEEPING them. The direction matters more than the one case
fixed: under a blacklist a type node added later is silently unsound, while under a
whitelist it is merely verbose.
