  Also adds a replay regression pin over **real ollama wire bytes**: the committed
  `ollama_v1_stream_toolcall.sse` capture (a qwen3.6:35b tool-calling turn — 48
  `reasoning` deltas with empty `content`, then one chunk carrying the whole tool
  call) is parsed through `ParseChatStepSSEStream` and asserted end to end, so a
  future ollama emission change or parser regression fails a sub-second unit test
  instead of a GPU run. The three streaming knobs and the split
  `AILANG_OLLAMA_HTTP_TIMEOUT_SEC` semantics are documented in
  `docs/docs/guides/debugging.md`.

- **Property tests now derive generators for ADTs, with bounded recursion and a
  size budget** — Lane B1 milestone M4 of
  [#517](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/517). A contract property
  over a same-file algebraic type now runs its 100 cases instead of reporting a
  vacuous `no generator` skip: each constructor becomes an `ADTGenerator` and the
  type derives as a choice over all of them. If **any** field of **any**
  constructor is underivable the whole type refuses (`nil` generator, loud skip)
  rather than silently dropping that constructor, which would have biased the
  generated distribution invisibly. `TypeApp` over a user type substitutes its
  arguments into the declaration's type parameters before deriving, and the
  existing `list` arm still matches first.

  Recursion is bounded two ways, because a depth budget alone does not bound
  cost. At the depth bound the choice is restricted to **non-recursive**
  constructors (recursiveness computed once per declaration and memoised), and a
  type with no non-recursive constructor (`type Endless = Wrap(Endless)`) derives
  to nothing — an honest vacuous skip rather than a hang. Separately, list
  lengths *inside* a derivation are capped by the remaining depth instead of
  `MaxSize`; top-level `list[<scalar>]` deliberately keeps its `0..MaxSize`
  behaviour so existing measured results are unchanged. Measured on the contracts
  corpus: `park.ail` goes 6 vacuous → 0 and rc 1 → 0, and the mutually-recursive
  `record_adt_cycle_verify.ail` goes 1 vacuous → 0 and rc 1 → 0, completing well
  inside a 60 s bound; `hof_verify.ail` and `list_verify.ail` are byte-for-byte
  unchanged, including the latter's one deliberate failure.

- **Property tests now derive generators for records, tuples, unit and type
  aliases** — Lane B1 milestone M3 of
  [#517](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/517). A contract property
  over an anonymous record (`{x: int, y: int}`), a nested record, a tuple, a
  same-file named record type, or any `type X = ...` alias now runs its 100
  cases instead of reporting a vacuous `no generator` skip. Derivation lives in
  the new `internal/testing/derive.go` behind the existing seam, so all three
  property paths (`forall`, `requires`, `ensures`) pick it up with no call-site
  edits; every descent is bounded by a depth budget, and anything underivable —
  an ADT (M4), an imported type, a too-deep nesting — still refuses honestly
  (`nil` generator, loud skip), never substitutes a fabricated value. Fixes
  measured on the contracts corpus: `record_pattern_verify` and
  `nested_record_verify` go 5 vacuous → 0 and rc 1 → 0; only record/tuple/unit
  rows move, ADT files are untouched.

  Two defects fixed alongside, both found by measurement rather than review:
  `RecordGenerator.Generate` **ranged a Go map while drawing from the RNG**, so
  a fixed seed did not reproduce a counterexample (field order is now sorted
  once at construction — the F-3 determinism fix, pinned by a 200-draw
  byte-identity test that reds on the map-range revert); and the preserved list
  arms derived their element with a **fresh root depth budget**, so a record
  type recursive through a list (`type Tree = { val: int, kids: [Tree] }`) —
  reachable for the first time now that named types derive — overflowed the
  stack at derivation time (elements now derive within the descended budget,
  pinned by a regression test that crashed pre-fix).

- Contract property tests can now splice **unit, record, tuple and constructor**
  values into the generated program, not just scalars and lists
  (`internal/testing/value_splice.go`). This is Lane B1 milestone M1 of
  [#517](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/517) and is deliberately
  **inert on its own** — no generator yet produces these shapes, so no test's
  behaviour changes. It is the prerequisite half of a mandated ordering: the
  splice arms must exist before the silent fallthrough (which today turns any
  unrecognised value into `()`) can be converted into a loud error, because that
  fallthrough is currently what makes unit values work. Two details are
  load-bearing and each is pinned by a test that fails when it is removed: record
  fields are emitted in **sorted** order (`RecordValue.Fields` is a Go map, so
  ranging it directly would make a fixed seed stop reproducing a counterexample),
  and a **nullary** constructor is emitted as a bare identifier rather than a
  call — `injectADTConstructors` binds nullary constructors to a value, not a
  closure, so a call would die with `cannot apply non-function value`.

- `std/bytes.toInts(b: bytes) -> [int]` — every byte value as a list of ints, the
  exact inverse of `fromInts`. This is the missing half of
  `design_docs/implemented/v0_21_0/m-bytes-toints-byteAt.md`, which specified
  **both** `byteAt` and `toInts` and shipped only `byteAt` in v0.21.0. `byteAt` in
  a loop could already reach every byte (verified — the reported "unreachable"
  case transcodes correctly at HEAD without this change), so the gain is shape,
  not reach: `toInts` hands the whole slice to `map`/`filter`/`foldl`, collapsing
  a 12-line recursion into one `flatMap` for charset transcoding, checksums, and
  byte-level parsing. Also adds the round-trip property test
  (`toInts(fromInts(xs)) == xs` across all 256 values) that the design doc listed
  as an acceptance criterion and that never landed. See
  [#609](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/609).

- `eval-suite` now prints a **cost tally** when a run finishes — per model/mode,
  then totals split by provenance. The split is the point: a single grand total
  is misleading on a rig where agent-mode `codex` and `claude` authenticate by
  subscription, so their `cost_usd` is real arithmetic over real tokens that
  nobody paid. `METERED (actually billed)` is the only line that answers "what
  did this cost us"; list-price-equivalent and unlabelled rows are surfaced
  alongside it and never folded in. Best-effort by construction — a malformed
  result file is skipped and a cost report never fails an otherwise-complete run
  (`eval_harness.TallyCosts`).

### Changed

- Agent-eval budgets are now gated on **work**, not dollars. A dollar ceiling
  buys tokens in inverse proportion to model price, so the nominally-uniform
  `$0.30`/`$0.50` caps across `agent_suite` spanned **24x** in actual work
  (audited 2026-07-31): `opencode-or-deepseek-v4-flash` got 3.40M tokens while
  `claude-sonnet-4-6` — the longitudinal **anchor** every other model is scored
  against — got 0.14M, the tightest gate in the suite. It had no `budgets:`
  block at all, so it fell through to the default formula, which at `$3/$15`
  buys almost nothing. A suite asking "does the agent loop rescue weak models"
  was handing the weakest models the most iteration and the reference the least.

  `budgets.max_tokens_per_bench` (new, per-model, overrides the global
  `--max-tokens-per-bench` flag) sets one shared **3.0M blended-token** ceiling
  across the suite. Subscription lanes — `codex` on `auth_mode: chatgpt`,
  `claude` on OAuth — gate on tokens alone and now sit at an identical 3.00M;
  their dollar caps were raised purely so the token gate is the one that binds
  (`claude-sonnet-4-6` `$0.50` → `$10.80`, `claude-haiku-4-5` `$0.30` → `$3.60`,
  `gpt5-6-luna` `$0.30` → `$0.75`). This is **not** a spend increase: those lanes
  are subscription-authenticated, so the figure is a list-price equivalent.
  ⚠️ Under `AILANG_AUTH_MODE=apikey` (cloud dispatch) `claude` *is* metered and
  those numbers become a real per-benchmark exposure ceiling — noted inline.

  Metered `opencode` lanes keep their existing dollar ceiling as a genuine spend
  control and take the token gate as a *second* bound, whichever binds first.
  Their comparison stays cost-limited, but now for a stated reason rather than
  by accident. Net effect: the metered lane that was over-favoured
  (`deepseek-v4-flash`, 3.40M) is pulled into line at 3.00M with no spend change.

- `Task.MaxTokensPerBench` is now enforced by `codex` and `claude`. The field has
  been plumbed to `executor.Task` since M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL Phase 1, but only
  `opencode` ever honoured it — the other executors silently ignored it. Both now
  abort on the cumulative-token ceiling, report `ThrashKilledAt`, and set
  `FinishThrashAborted`, which outranks `FinishCostExhausted` because on a
  subscription lane the dollar figure is notional and "did too much work" is the
  truthful reason a run stopped.

### Added

- Cost provenance: every cost figure now records whether anyone was actually
  charged. `executor.CostProvenance` is one of `metered` (API key, OpenRouter
  credits, Vertex ADC), `list-price-equivalent` (real arithmetic over real
  tokens on a subscription/OAuth lane — never billed), `free-local` (on-device,
  no marginal cost), or `unknown`. Each executor classifies its own auth lane:
  `codex` reads `~/.codex/auth.json` (`auth_mode: chatgpt` → subscription),
  `claude` mirrors the `AILANG_AUTH_MODE` branch, `managed_agents` is metered
  via ADC, and `opencode`/`motoko`/`pi` report provider-billed cost. Zero
  resolved rates win over the lane, so a free local model is never labelled as
  spend. Standard mode reaches metered HTTP APIs and is labelled accordingly.

  This closes the gap where a subscription run and a metered run were
  indistinguishable once both landed in the same column — the v1.0
  `cost-per-verified-success` KPI defines its numerator as attributable
  **metered** dollars, and on this rig most agent-mode dollars are not that.

  Surfaced end to end: banked as `cost_provenance` on eval result rows; stored
  on chain stages (schema v17, `chain_stages.cost_provenance`); classified by
  `ClassifyStageCost` into a new `subscription` status that `CostRollup` keeps
  **out** of `TotalKnownCost` and reports separately; and exported as a
  `costProvenance` breakdown alongside `totalCostUSD`. An absent label — every
  row banked before 2026-07-30 — reads as `unknown`, never as metered; no
  provenance is backfilled by guess. `dollars_per_pass`, `p90_cost_per_success`
  and `cost_overhead_vs_best` are documented as list-price figures rather than
  silently re-scoped.

### Fixed

- **Property-test seeds are now deterministic and replayable ([#535](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/535))** — the hidden
  wall-clock seeding is gone. `ailang test` used to seed every property RNG from
  `time.Now().UnixNano()`, so unchanged contracts generated different samples on
  every run (and could flip verdicts run-to-run). Property seeds are now derived
  deterministically from a master seed via a stable v1 derivation
  (`ailang-property-seed-v1`). Two flags control the master seed:
  `--seed N` (any signed int64, including `0`) and `--random-seed` (reads one
  master from `crypto/rand` and reports it for replay). They are mutually
  exclusive — passing both exits 2 with an error on stderr. The default (no
  flag) is derived mode: a fixed, reproducible sample stream per property.
- **Additive JSON seed/replay fields.** The JSON report now carries top-level
  `seed_mode`, `seed`, and `seed_derivation`, plus a per-property `seed` and (on
  failures) a copy/paste **runnable** `replay` command that reproduces the exact
  invocation — the runnable form the old emitted command never was (it printed
  the aggregate display label `All Tests` instead of the actual target).
- **Three intentional incompatibilities** (design doc
  `m-property-seed-determinism.md`): (1) out-of-contract `ensures` failures are
  now discarded attempts rather than violations, so affected contracts may pass
  or skip; (2) default samples change **once** and then stabilise; (3)
  `GenConfig.Seed == 0` now means exactly zero rather than "seed from the
  clock".
- **Known limitation:** `forall`-style properties still fail on their first
  generated input (`evaluation failed: empty program`) on the legacy
  source-synthesis path, so their seed is derived and reported but no sample
  stream is produced. Pre-existing and tracked separately from #535.

- Agent-mode `cost_usd` now bills the model that actually ran. Executors that
  compute cost from token counts were using their own hardcoded table — the
  codex executor billed **every** model it ran at gpt-5-codex's `$1.25/$10` per
  1M, so `gpt5-6-luna` and `gpt5-4-mini` were banked at identical rates despite
  really costing `$0.20/$1.20` and `$0.75/$4.50`. `Task.Pricing` now carries the
  per-model rates from `models.yml` and `executor.ResolveCostModel` prefers them,
  falling back to the executor's table only when a caller supplies none (a
  present-but-zero rate is honoured, so free local Ollama models are not billed
  at cloud prices). Applied to the two executors that compute cost — `codex` and
  `managed_agents`; `claude`, `pi`, `motoko` and `opencode` bank the figure their
  CLI reports and were never affected, and their `CostModel()` doc comments now
  say so.

  The cost-**kill** path was already correct — `executor.CostBudget` is built
  from `models.yml` pricing in `agent_runner_multi.go` — which means banked cost
  and kill threshold were computed from two different price tables. A v0.30.0
  row shows the split directly: `gpt5-6-luna`/`graph_bfs` banked `$0.34259` at
  codex rates while the `$0.30` budget that spared it saw `$0.26980` at luna's.
  Do not compare `cost_usd` against `max_cost_usd` on any pre-fix agent row.

- `codex` HealthCheck no longer treats a missing `OPENAI_API_KEY` as a warning.
  Codex authenticates from `~/.codex/auth.json` (`codex login`); an env key does
  not override it — probe-verified against codex-cli 0.145.0 with
  `auth_mode: "chatgpt"`, where a deliberately invalid key in the environment
  still ran clean. Its presence was never evidence that runs are metered.

### Changed

- Eval pricing: OpenAI cut the GPT-5.6 family on 2026-07-30 and `models.yml` now
  matches. `gpt5-6-luna` -80% (`$1.00/$6.00` → `$0.20/$1.20` per 1M) and
  `gpt5-6-terra` -20% (`$2.50/$15.00` → `$2.00/$12.00` per 1M); `gpt5-6-sol` is
  unchanged at `$5/$30`. Verified against
  `developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.6-{sol,terra,luna}`. Two
  consequences are recorded inline: Luna's explicit `max_cost_usd: $0.30` is now
  load-bearing (the default formula resolves to `$0.051` at the new prices, down
  from `$0.256`), and every banked Terra/Luna cost figure dated before 2026-07-30
  was measured at the old prices, so cross-boundary cost comparisons need
  normalizing. Luna is now 3.75x cheaper than the `gpt5-4-mini` incumbent it lost
  to on 2026-07-11 — the quality half of that gate (23/26 vs 25/26, N=1) still
  stands, so the `dev_models`/`lang_harness_suite` slot is unchanged pending an
  N≥3 `--tier core` re-gate.
## [v0.33.1] - 2026-08-06

### Security

- **Embedded MCP replay now asserts its own response labelling instead of inheriting it
  (`#603`, CodeQL `go/reflected-xss` high).** `embeddedMCPHandler.serveTransport` buffers
  the MCP SDK's response and replays its headers, status and body wholesale. Triage
  measured 12 hostile request shapes through the real handler: the taint trace is
  **correct** (2 of 12 reflect a literal `<script>` payload into the body, so the finding
  is not imaginary), but every response carries a Content-Type and the two reflecting
  paths carry `nosniff`, so nothing is browser-renderable — **not exploitable at HEAD**.
  All three protections were inherited from the dependency's internals, though, with
  nothing local asserting them. The replay now defaults `Content-Type` to
  `application/json` when the buffered response omits one, and always sets
  `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Guarded by a 12-shape regression battery with an
  anti-vacuity control that fails loudly if the battery ever stops reflecting, plus an
  injected-transport test covering the default branch — which the public path cannot
  reach, and whose deletion previously reddened no test at all. `writeMCPEnvelope`, the
  file's other response path (oversized bodies, surface errors, panic recovery), echoes a
  request-controlled JSON-RPC `id` and set `Content-Type` but not `nosniff`; the two paths
  now agree. Not exploitable there either — `encoding/json` escapes the id — but "the
  encoder escapes by default" is precisely the kind of inherited guarantee this change
  exists to stop relying on.

### Added

- **`pi-or-deepseek-v4-flash` — pi-harness OpenRouter lane (opt-in, mission-fleet
  trial).** First OpenRouter model wired through the pi minimal harness, as the
  codex-quota offload candidate (its opencode twin ranked #2 agent-mode ELO on the
  v0.32.0 baseline at ~1/100th Sonnet price). Requires the model id in the
  `~/.pi/agent/models.json` openrouter provider block (same mechanism as the local
  ollama block). Probe 2026-08-06: the 0731 checkpoint **thinks by default** (75
  reasoning tokens unprompted via Venice), unlike the April preview — recorded as
  `default_thinking: "on"`. Not in any suite until it clears the pi agent smoke gate.

### Mission infrastructure (no user-facing language or CLI change)

- **Launchd drivers now run committed code, and failing to costs you a notification instead
  of nothing** ([#558](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/558)). launchd invokes
  each driver by absolute path into the shared clone, and *everything* a fire reads hangs off
  that one root (`mission-control.sh:40`): the driver, the skill under `.claude/skills/`, and
  the charter under `design_docs/`. Nothing keeps that clone current and nothing reported the
  drift, so three artefact classes went stale together — measured as `#556`'s retired qwen3.5
  running 24/24 two days after retirement (iter-131), a stale skill (iter-128), a stale charter
  (iter-129), and again on 2026-08-12 when `564cc4640`'s lane-degradation fix sat inert on V1,
  12 commits behind, while both sibling missions had it.

  Previous repairs patched individual artefacts, which fixes one third of the bug. New
  `tools/launchd/lib/pin-root.sh` pins the **root**: it re-execs the driver out of a worktree
  pinned to committed `origin/dev`, moving script, skill and charter together. It re-points
  `MISSION_WORKDIR` as well — `mission-motoko.env:8` and `mission-world.env:5` pin it, so
  pinning only the script would leave those two running a fresh driver against a stale charter,
  a half-fix that reports green. Sprint worktrees are unaffected (created by absolute path).

  A failed pin is **loud, not fatal**: aborting would make network availability a hard
  dependency of every fire, so the fire proceeds and posts *"driver ran UNPINNED"* to both human
  channels. Silently continuing would have rebuilt the very defect `564cc4640` removed one layer
  up. Escape hatch `AILANG_DRIVER_PIN=0`; `MISSION_DRY_RUN=1` reports `pin=<status>(<n> behind)`.
  The two emit blocks share one `_mc_notify`, because two near-identical notifiers is how one of
  them silently rots. First-ever automated coverage for `tools/launchd` (37 tests, `make
  test-launchd-drivers`, bash 3.2.57): both pin arms, both notify arms, the fetch-failure and
  driver-missing-from-ref refusals, and a control proving the stale clone really was stale.
  Stated plainly: this cannot help until the shared clone receives it once, and that first
  reconcile is human (Principle 0).

  **Follow-up, and it is the reason the first cut would have taken the loop down.** A pin
  worktree is by construction a path Claude Code has never seen, and an un-onboarded checkout
  makes headless `claude -p` block on a trust dialog it cannot display — so *every* model probe
  hangs to its timeout and the driver refuses with "NO usable model in prefs", indistinguishable
  from a quota outage. That had just cost the motoko mission its entire first unattended fire
  (charter V22, `76ee4056c`). The pin now checks
  `projects[<wt>].hasCompletedProjectOnboarding` in `~/.claude.json` and **refuses to pin**
  when it is absent or undeterminable, because stale-but-working strictly beats fresh-and-dead.
  It reads the *measured* flag, not the `hasTrustDialogAccepted` the error text names —
  `ailang-world` runs with trust=false/onboarded=true, the control proving the named flag is not
  the gate.

  **Corrected the same day, by measurement.** That first predicate checked the pin worktree's
  *own* entry, and it was wrong: `claude -p` runs fine from `~/.ailang-driver-pin/v1` while
  `~/.claude.json` holds **no entry for it at all** — a git worktree inherits its source clone's
  trust, having no separate identity to onboard. Checking the worktree would have refused a
  demonstrably working target on every fire, leaving the pin permanently off — safe, but
  permanently off is not the goal. The predicate is now the **source clone**, accepting when
  either path is onboarded. The two measured cases discriminate it cleanly: `ailang-motoko`
  (fresh clone, nothing onboarded anywhere) hangs and is refused; a worktree of an onboarded
  clone works and is accepted. Consequence: on a rig whose main clone is already onboarded,
  pinning needs **no human action at all** — the earlier `cd … && claude` instruction was an
  artefact of the wrong predicate.

  The same pass fixed a latent one-shot bug the new tests caught: worktree existence was decided
  by string-matching `git worktree list`, which never matches git's recorded realpath when any
  path component is a symlink (`/var` → `/private/var`). Fire 1 would pin, fire 2 would hit
  `worktree add` on an existing directory and refuse — a fix that disables itself after first
  use while reporting loudly. Now decided by asking the worktree itself. 52 assertions.

- **`dev.ailang.mission-recovery` — a probe-stall costs ~20 min of loop time instead of
  90.** When every controller probe times out the driver refuses, and with
  `StartInterval` at 5400s that slot is gone for a full 90 minutes even if the stall
  clears in 20. Measured 2026-08-10: refused 07:59, next fire probed OK **in 24s** at
  09:29 — roughly 85 minutes idle for a stall that was already over. Over the prior 7
  live-fire days that pattern cost 17 refusals against 38 runs (~31% of fires).
  `tools/launchd/mission-recovery.sh` polls every 4 min and kickstarts mission-control
  once its new `.blocked` marker is up, gated by a 20-min cooldown (the driver writes
  its PIDFILE only *after* the probe phase, so a fire that is still probing is invisible
  to the overlap guard; the cooldown must exceed the ~13-min observed / ~16-min
  worst-case probe cycle or retries stack into concurrent probe storms).
  Deliberately does **not** probe the API itself — during these episodes small requests
  pass while heavy repo-cwd session creation stalls, so a cheap probe would go green and
  kickstart into a driver that refuses anyway. The driver's own probe decides; a failed
  retry costs zero tokens. Paired driver change: refusals now announce **once per
  blocked episode** rather than once per refusal (marker cleared the moment a probe
  succeeds), otherwise faster retries would post a dozen identical GH comments during
  one incident. Kill switches: `mission-recovery.disabled` for the watcher, and the
  mission's own `mission-control.disabled` also stops it — a deliberately disabled
  mission is never resurrected. Verified by 7 decision arms against a fake mission label
  (kill-switch-wins, no-marker, live-pidfile, cooldown, stale-pidfile, action) plus a
  3-fire driver cycle under stubbed `claude`/`gh`/`ailang`: announce → quiet → cleared.
  V1 only; the world mission runs from a separate checkout and is unchanged.

- **Every Claude Code hook now carries an explicit timeout, and the SessionStart brain
  lookup is bounded (mission D-3).** `ailang cache search` issues an Ollama embedding
  request, which blocks ~30s whenever an eval job holds the GPU with a large model —
  inside a SessionStart hook that had no timeout at all, so session startup inherited
  the stall. `scripts/hooks/session_start.sh` gains `run_bounded` (perl `alarm` + `exec`;
  macOS ships no GNU `timeout`) capping both brain searches at `AILANG_BRAIN_TIMEOUT`,
  default 3s, degrading to empty brain context rather than a hung hook. All 14 hook
  entries in `.claude/settings.json` now declare a timeout. Verified: alarm survives
  `exec` on Darwin (30s sleep killed at 2.04s, rc=142), output and exit codes pass
  through unchanged, hook end-to-end 0.51s against a 10s budget, `bash -n` clean under
  the rig's bash 3.2.57. Note this is NOT the cause of the mission loop's iteration
  refusals — those are 120s controller-probe timeouts on new-session creation, measured
  independently and unrelated to hook latency.

- **`make check-skills` — a CI gate for skill frontmatter, after two skills spent an
  unknown period advertising their titles as their descriptions.**
  `motoko-analyzer/SKILL.md` and `ailang-packages/SKILL.md` had no `---` frontmatter
  block at all, so the loader fell back to the H1 heading for the description. Nothing
  errored: the skills loaded and stayed selectable, they just under-triggered, and
  `motoko-analyzer` is the motoko mission diagnostic loop. Surfaced incidentally by a
  codex probe emitting `SKILL.md: missing YAML frontmatter delimited by ---`, not by
  any check we own. Both files fixed by lifting their existing intro paragraph into a
  real `name:`/`description:` block; the new `scripts/check_skills.sh` gate asserts
  frontmatter present, terminated, non-empty, `name:` matching the directory, and
  `description:` non-empty across all 39 skills, wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
  beside the other `check-*` gates. Verified by five mutation arms (each asserted to
  land and restore byte-identical), which is how the first draft was caught passing an
  unterminated-frontmatter mutant. Deliberately NOT a strict-YAML gate: two live skills
  (`cli-doc-maintainer`, `mission-control`) carry an unquoted `": "` inside their
  description that PyYAML rejects and the real loader accepts — asserting YAML validity
  would fail two working skills and needs a parser this repo does not carry.

- **The mission executor lane moves to pi + DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731 for BOTH missions
  (v1 + world), per Mark 2026-08-06 — codex-quota relief.** Gate trial: agent smoke
  23/23 clean + the #590 mission replay (12/13 held-out landed tests, $0.076 true
  cost, 12.6 min vs the planner's ~1.5-2-day estimate). Three pieces: a Gate-3
  `PROVIDER=pi` recipe in the mission-control skill (bounded 30-min run, no-sandbox
  containment rules, mandatory NDJSON→metered-ledger spend posting), a role-generic
  `pi:*` pre-flight probe in `tools/launchd/mission-control.sh` (bash-3.2-safe,
  opus fallback, mirrors the codex loop), and `MISSION_EXECUTOR_MODEL` pins in both
  mission env files (one-line rollback, driver default unchanged). Runaway bounds:
  30-min hard kill (< $0.50/run at observed burn) inside the $5/iteration metered
  ceiling. Dry-run verified end-to-end through the env-file delivery path.

### Fixed

- **Agent-mode runs no longer bank $0 cost when the executor can't price the
  model.** The multi-executor agent runner trusted executor-self-reported cost
  verbatim; pi reports $0 for `~/.pi/agent/models.json` custom providers (e.g.
  OpenRouter lanes), so metered runs banked as free. When the executor reports
  zero but models.yml prices the model, cost is now recomputed from banked tokens
  (same helper as standard mode). Known undercount: cache-read tokens are priced
  at $0 (models.yml has no cache-read rate; OpenRouter bills ~20% of input rate) —
  understates ~25-30% at agent-loop hit rates until a `cache_read_per_1k` field
  exists.

- **models.yml: GLM-5.2 pricing refreshed after OpenRouter's ~60% price cut.**
  `z-ai/glm-5.2` now bills $0.56/$1.76 per 1M (verified live 2026-08-06 via
  OpenRouter `/api/v1/models`); both `or-glm-5-2` and `opencode-or-glm-5-2`
  still recorded the $1.40/$4.40 launch price, so cost accounting overstated
  GLM-5.2 runs ~2.5x. Also rewrote the `opencode-or-glm-5-2` notes that still
  carried the June "REJECTED … not in any suite" verdict — retracted 2026-07-19
  as a harness truncation artifact — which contradicted the entry's actual
  agent_suite membership. With the cut, GLM-5.2 is now cheaper than the
  GLM-5.1 it replaced ($0.95/$2.99). Pricing/docs only; no suite changes.

- **`test "name" { assert <cond> }` now executes instead of failing 100% of the time**
  with `PAR_NO_PREFIX_PARSE` (#590). Named test bodies are printed back to AILANG source
  and re-run through the general pipeline, which has no `assert` prefix parselet, so the
  keyword could never survive that round-trip — leaving `assert` a reserved word with no
  working call site anywhere in the language. Top-level `assert` statements are now lowered
  in `FoldTestBody` (`internal/testing/test_body_lowering.go`) into a short-circuiting `if`
  chain over an int sentinel, so a failing assert stops the body and reports **which** check
  failed, with its source text and its original position — e.g.
  ``assertion 2 failed: `assert (add_one(3) == 99)` (at m.ail:9:3)``. A non-final false
  assert now fails the test instead of being silently discarded. A trailing bare expression
  is reported as ``check N failed`` rather than being quoted back as an `assert` the user
  never wrote. Bodies containing no `assert` keep the previous code path unchanged.

- **`internal/smt` hard-timeout tests no longer red on a lost startup race (#602).** The fake
  solver has ~3s to record its child PID before the process group is killed; measured over 500
  trials, 1 spawn in 300 exceeds that under full-suite load, and when it does the pidfile can
  never appear. A trial that never recorded a child PID now counts as inconclusive and the
  scenario is retried (bounded, 3 attempts, loud failure on exhaustion) instead of failing. A
  genuine process-group-kill regression still reds on every attempt.

### Added

- **Embeddable serve-api surface complete — #498 Lane B M3 (final milestone).** The public
  `serveapi` package now serves both protocols from one caller-supplied descriptor set: A2A
  agent cards and `tasks/send` dispatch are projected from the same request-scoped authorized
  surface as MCP, and `Mount` wires `/mcp/`, `/.well-known/agent.json` and `/a2a/` onto a
  caller-owned `http.ServeMux`. Hosts own identity resolution, per-session tool membership,
  invocation, middleware, listeners, TLS and shutdown; AILANG supplies validated deterministic
  projections, bounded callback execution and frozen wire-error envelopes. Endpoint exposure is
  now decided by a single membership gateway, with `@nomcp` kept as an MCP-only *projection*
  filter applied after membership — so a `@nomcp` export remains present in the A2A card and
  OpenAPI. Standalone `ailang serve-api` behaviour is unchanged. Completes the session-owned
  protocol surface the Ailang World mission is blocked on (M1 in v0.33.0, M2 PR #592).

### Changed

- **Deterministic test gating and egress posture (#583, #494, #509, #587, #561).**
  `go test -short ./...` no longer skips first-party tests: the `testing.Short()` convention has
  been removed and live-internet tests now skip by default, running only when explicitly opted
  in with `AILANG_LIVE_NET=1`. `make test` now uses a poisoned HTTP(S) proxy at
  `127.0.0.1:9`, matching CI so accidental public-internet access fails locally as well.
- Added the shared `internal/testutil` live-network gate, deadline-derived hang guards, and
  bounded subprocess runner; `gatelint` prevents the banned gate idioms from returning, and an
  egress-posture probe records both the proxy-covered routes and the deliberately open residuals.

## [v0.33.0] - 2026-08-04

### Added

- **Recorded-stream API (`std/ai.stepWithStreamRecorded`) — adopted from @arniwesth's prototype (#546, PR #577).**
  New sibling to `stepWithStream` returning `RecordedStream = { chunks: [StreamChunk],
  outcome: Result[StepResult, AIError] }`: per-chunk callbacks still fire immediately (live
  rendering unchanged), and the exact ordered chunk sequence is returned on BOTH outcomes — a
  stream failing mid-way still yields every chunk observed, the case deterministic replay
  depends on. Shared decode/validation core with `stepWithStream` (no drift), fail-loud
  encode path with a locally bounded drain (no interface change). Enables motoko_agent's
  deterministic simulation testing (DST). Experimental stability. Follow-up: #578.
- **Embeddable serve-api contract — #498 Lane B M1 (PR #585).** A public, caller-owned
  surface for mounting MCP-HTTP/A2A handlers: authorized tool surface (the caller supplies
  the exact visible descriptors), bounded callback runner, no built-in tool exposed unless
  supplied. First milestone of the session-owned protocol surface the Ailang World mission
  is blocked on (design doc #582, sprint plan #583-era; Lane A — `--no-feedback-tool` +
  `--caps` docs — shipped in v0.32.0).

### Changed

- **v0.32.0 benchmark curation cycle applied** — 4 benchmarks promoted, 8 demoted
  (`chore(eval)` f574c4b58); CI tier drift gates re-centered accordingly (d434a9be9).
- **`agent_suite`**: dropped `opencode-or-deepseek-v4-pro` (fa00eb3f6).
- **Mission tooling** (repo-internal): planner role moved to the codex lane with a
  derivation gate and role-generic driver probe (cd76499c5); benchmark dashboard unified
  with the local rig rotation for v0.32.0 (274dc5bed, 9b65b6a83).

## [v0.32.0] - 2026-08-03

### Added

- **Confidence-gated standard release baselines + a real cost estimate/budget cap for `eval-suite` (M-EVAL-STANDARD-CONFIDENCE-GATING).**
  Every AILANG release ran the full `core,stretch,frontier` tier (56 benchmarks)
  against all 18 `extended_suite` models for standard eval, every time —
  ~$98-103/release on banked `cost_usd` data (v0.30.0, v0.29.2), 71% of it
  concentrated in the 5 most expensive models. The ELO confidence-select
  mechanism that fixes this already existed (`--benchmarks-by-confidence`,
  M-EVAL-RATING-EFFICIENCY, v0.26.0) but had only ever been wired into the
  local single-GPU rig's nightly script, never the cloud release path, and
  `observatory.db` had zero standard-mode ratings.
  - `.claude/skills/post-release/scripts/run_eval_baseline.sh --full` runs now
    confidence-gate `core,stretch` **by default** (flipped from opt-in same-day,
    after the first live comparison below was reviewed): models with
    standard-mode rating history get the confidence-gated `core,stretch`
    selection (drops Trivial-band/saturated benchmarks) plus an always-full
    `frontier` call — frontier's curation contract requires routine
    full-coverage failure data, so it is never gated. Models with no rating
    history (new/swapped flagships) always get the full tier. Falls open to
    today's exact full-tier behavior if the ratings DB is unseeded, missing,
    or corrupt. Self-seeds `observatory.db` from each `--full` release's own
    results afterward, closing the loop for the next release. New
    `--no-confidence-gate` forces the old full-tier behavior (periodic
    full-audit cadence, hand-auditing a roster change).
  - `ailang eval-suite --dry-run` now prints a real, computed `$` estimate
    (historical mean tokens per benchmark from the most recent baseline ×
    `models.yml` pricing; benchmarks with no history are explicitly flagged,
    never silently folded into the total) — replacing a cost table the skill
    doc itself already flagged as stale and understated.
  - `ailang eval-suite --budget-usd <N>` adds an aggregate cost ceiling
    (0/unset = no cap, today's behavior unchanged). On breach: in-flight
    trials finish, no new trial is scheduled, a `budget_stopped.json`
    sentinel is written, and `run_eval_baseline.sh` merges
    `budget_stopped: true` into `baseline.json` so a partial baseline is
    never silently presented as complete. Release runs default to a $150
    cap (~15% headroom over the highest real combined baseline seen).
  - First live comparison (dry-run projection, not yet a real release spend):
    full-tier standard eval $49.57 vs gated $35.28 — **~29% projected savings**
    on the gated portion.
  - Design doc: [m-eval-standard-confidence-gating.md](../design_docs/implemented/v0_32_0/m-eval-standard-confidence-gating.md).

- **DeepSeek V4 Flash repointed to the official `-0731` release.** All 3
  entries (`or-deepseek-v4-flash`, `opencode-or-deepseek-v4-flash`,
  `motoko-or-deepseek-v4-flash`) pointed at the April preview slug
  (`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`). DeepSeek shipped an official release on
  2026-07-31 (`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731`) — same architecture,
  re-post-trained, "substantially enhanced agentic capabilities" per their
  release notes, native OpenAI Responses API support. Also cheaper: $0.14/$0.28
  → $0.09/$0.18 per 1M. Existing pass-rate notes on these rows predate the
  repoint and are flagged stale pending a re-gate.

### Fixed

- **`cmd/ailang` tests build the `ailang` binary once per run instead of ~16 times
  (#564).** `buildAilang`'s doc comment claimed it built "once per test run", but it
  had no memoization — each of its 14 call sites re-linked the ~93 MB binary into a
  fresh `t.TempDir()`, and two further call paths linked their own copies. The
  redundant linking was 86% of the package's test runtime and pushed it past the 300s
  CI ceiling, turning `test-windows` red on a docs-only commit. All three paths now
  share one `sync.Once` builder (the pattern `budget_scoping_e2e_test.go` already
  used): `cmd/ailang` drops from 110.8s to 15.5s. Test-infrastructure only — no
  runtime, CLI, or language behaviour changes, and the 300s timeout is deliberately
  unchanged since it is a real hang-detector.

- **`ailang test` no longer corrupts the temp modules it generates (#548).**
  The internal strip that removes effectful functions before re-elaboration
  deleted only the *first line* of a declaration and treated any function not
  written with the `pure` keyword as effectful. Multi-line bodies, `requires` /
  `ensures` clauses, and `@verify` annotations were left behind as orphaned
  top-level tokens, so the generated file failed to parse and the error was
  reported against a `_namedtest_body_*.ail` the user never wrote. Any file
  combining a named `test "..." { ... }` block with a contract-bearing or
  multi-line function was affected, as was every module-less file carrying a
  contract. The strip now removes whole declaration spans (including leading
  annotations), treats an explicit empty effect annotation `! {}` as pure, and
  keeps the function currently under extraction. Purity inference is local to
  the test harness — `ast.FuncDecl.IsPure` and `ailang fmt` are unchanged, so
  no user source is rewritten. Hidden wall-clock seeding (#535) remains open.

- **Property `ensures` checks now respect their function's complete `requires`
  domain (#547).** Generated tuples that fail any comma-separated precondition
  are discarded before the function is called. A property passes only after
  100 in-contract cases; reaching the 1,000-attempt cap first reports an honest
  `out_of_contract`/`unverified` skip instead of a false failure or vacuous pass.
  Property JSON adds integer `generated_inputs` and `discarded_inputs` counters,
  and human output reports accepted, discarded, and generated totals. Hidden
  wall-clock seeding (#535) is intentionally unchanged and remains open.

### Changed

- **Agent DX: trimmed always-loaded context ~34% and gated the per-turn μRAG
  injection.** Long Claude Code sessions were exhausting context. Audit of the
  repo-controlled startup budget found ~10.2k tokens loaded every session, plus
  a **per-turn** tax: the `UserPromptSubmit` μRAG hook injected ~430 tokens on
  *every* turn regardless of relevance (~17k tokens across a 40-turn session —
  more than every static file combined).

  The μRAG relevance floor was calibrated against an assumption that was never
  measured: the code comment claimed off-topic prompts score ~0.45, so the floor
  sat at 0.50. Measured against an 18-prompt labelled panel on the current
  corpus, real off-topic repo-ops prompts reach **0.59** — the hook was firing
  on **7 of 10** off-topic prompts. A margin-based gate (top-vs-median
  peakedness) was tried and **rejected: it overlaps** (on_min 0.020 vs off_max
  0.090). The absolute floor separates cleanly, so the floor moves 0.50 → 0.60
  (on-topic min 0.61, off-topic max 0.59).
  - `userPromptRelevanceFloor` 0.50 → **0.70** with the real panel data recorded
    in the comment, in `internal/microrag/userprompt.go`. (An intermediate 0.60
    was calibrated on short prompts only and still admitted real 525-char
    repo-ops prompts scoring 0.65; the panel now spans both prompt lengths.
    Long *on*-topic prompts score 0.81+, so relevance still dominates length.
    Cost, stated explicitly: three terse short-form syntax questions
    (0.61–0.65) no longer inject.)
  - `scripts/hooks/brain_on_prompt.sh` — a SECOND, previously ungated
    `UserPromptSubmit` retrieval hook — floor `AILANG_BRAIN_MIN_SCORE`
    0.55 → 0.70. It searches ALL namespaces and injects up to 3 hits, and was
    firing on 2 of 8 short off-topic prompts and on every long one.
  - Dedup bypass is now derived from the live floor
    (`userPromptBypassFor`). The configured default bypass is 0.70, so raising
    the floor to 0.70 collapsed the dedup band to zero — every injection would
    have bypassed the ledger and the same chunk could re-inject every turn,
    defeating the floor's purpose.
  - New `AILANG_MICRORAG_USERPROMPT_FLOOR` env override — the floor is
    corpus-dependent, so re-tuning after a reindex must not need a rebuild.
    Unparseable or out-of-range values fall back to the default rather than
    silently widening the gate.
  - New `TestUserPrompt_FloorSeparatesCalibrationPanel` pins the panel so
    corpus drift can't silently reopen the gate.
  - Verified end-to-end through the real hook: 4/4 off-topic now silent, 4/4
    on-topic still inject.

  Context files were restructured so information loads where it is used, not on
  every session:
  - `CLAUDE.md` 10,533 → 5,661 bytes. Removed the hand-maintained skills list
    (duplicated the system-prompt skill listing, and named a `skill-builder`
    skill that does not exist), the conditional-rules table (the `paths:`
    frontmatter mechanism is automatic), and the session-start routine (the
    SessionStart hook already prints those commands).
  - New path-scoped `.claude/rules/architecture.md` (`internal/**`, `cmd/**`,
    `ui/**`) now carries the layer map, the enforced import directions, and the
    `internal/ai` vs `internal/executor` table.
  - Claude Code LSP plugin setup moved to `CONTRIBUTING.md` — it is one-time
    human setup, not per-session agent context.
  - Dropped the stale `Skill(skill-builder)` permission from
    `.claude/settings.json`.

- **Brain: wired the learn-from-commits half back up, retired three dead hooks,
  and un-shadowed the brain in git worktrees.**
  - `scripts/hooks/brain_resolution.sh` had been orphaned since 2026-03-16 by
    commit `555320e8` (message: `brain`), which deleted its only invocation
    while *simultaneously* rewriting the script by 81 lines to be a "user-level
    hook — works in any git project". The promotion to `~/.claude/settings.json`
    never landed (no hooks block there), and the μRAG design doc still expected
    it to fire on PostToolUse(git commit). Accidental drop, not retirement — now
    re-wired as `PostToolUse` matcher `Bash`. All 613 frames were indexed docs;
    the `resolutions` namespace had never been written to.
  - Added a HEAD-moved guard to that hook. It matches `git commit` anywhere in
    the command string, and `put-resolution` keys frames by timestamp
    (append-only by design), so any Bash call that merely *mentioned* the phrase
    banked a duplicate frame of whatever HEAD already was — reproduced: two
    duplicate frames of a stale commit from one command. A high-water mark in
    the common git dir (shared across worktrees) makes it idempotent per commit.
    Verified: 5 invocations on an unchanged HEAD bank 0 frames; a moved HEAD
    banks exactly 1; a non-commit command banks 0.
  - `getProjectBrainPath()` is now git-worktree aware. Because
    `.ailang/state/{evaluations,sprints}/*.json` are git-tracked, every linked
    worktree materialises `.ailang/`, so the project-root walk stopped there and
    opened an EMPTY brain that shadowed the main checkout's. Agent work here is
    deliberately worktree-isolated, so the sessions that most need a warm brain
    were the ones running cold. All 6 worktrees went from 0 results to a 0.75
    hit on the acceptance query.
  - Deleted three genuinely retired hooks: `claude_telemetry.sh` (a stale,
    divergent fork of the `//go:embed`-ed `internal/executor/claude_telemetry.sh`
    that is actually installed — keeping a drifting duplicate of a live embedded
    asset invites editing the wrong one), `check_inbox_on_prompt.sh`
    (self-documented as TEMPORARY, "should be REMOVED and only SessionStart hook
    used", never wired), and `agent_handoff.sh` (superseded by mission-control's
    explicit design-doc → sprint-planner routing; its documented config file
    `.claude/hooks.json` no longer exists). Stale `agent_handoff` section removed
    from `.claude/gemini.md`. Every remaining hook script is now wired.
  - The **user** brain tier is confirmed working-as-designed: `cache promote` is
    a documented manual curation path with nothing automating it. Zero frames is
    the normal state, not a fault. Documented in `docs/docs/guides/brain-cache.md`.

### Fixed

- **M-FMT-DIALECT-ALIGNMENT: `ailang fmt` no longer teaches a dialect
  `ailang prompt` never taught.** Every eval model is handed the prompt as its
  system text and then told by the formatter, on every write, that its correct
  code is non-canonical — and it obeys, because the formatter speaks last. A
  model wrote "the file type-checks but the formatter wants canonical style",
  switched dialect, and broke working code. Measured on arms differing only by
  the fmt extension: **+62% output tokens, median +2,693, 18/23 pairs, Wilcoxon
  p=0.0112.** No type-check can catch this — both spellings parse and mean the
  same thing, so `Parse(fmt(x)) ≡ Parse(x)` holds throughout. Five spellings
  were realigned, each verified to parse to an IDENTICAL AST so the taught form
  round-trips exactly:

  | fmt used to emit | it now emits | where |
  |---|---|---|
  | `concat_String(concat_String(show(a), " x"), …)` | `"${a} x…"` | `internal/format/interp.go` (new) |
  | `(int) -> int` | `int -> int` | `internal/format/types.go` |
  | `now(())` | `now()` | `internal/format/expr.go` |
  | `{name: name, age: age}` | `{name, age}` | `internal/format/pattern.go` |
  | `{ email: string \| _r0 }` | `{ email: string, ... }` | `internal/format/types.go` |

  `_r0` was an internal compiler-generated row-variable name leaking into
  user-facing source. Interpolation re-sugaring is deliberately conservative:
  a hand-written `concat_String` chain — one with a bare operand, with no hole,
  with adjacent literals, or nested to the right — is left exactly as written,
  because re-sugaring those would change what the source re-parses to.

- **Parser: a block statement may begin with a literal.** `ailang fmt` drops `;`
  and relies on the R2 newline soft separator, whose statement-starter set
  omitted every literal — so a block whose last statement began with one simply
  did not parse:

  ```ailang
  export func f() -> string {
    let a = "x"
    "${a} y"        -- PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN: expected }, got STRING_PART
  }
  ```

  That is both what fmt emits once interpolation is re-sugared and what a model
  writes when taught newline separators and `"${x}"` together. It failed for
  string, interpolated-string, int, float and bool tails alike. `(`, `[` and `{`
  stay excluded — they genuinely continue an expression (call, indexing,
  record-update), which is the ambiguity R2 was built around.

- **Eval runs now bank tool RESULTS, not just tool calls.** The harness builds
  its own `agent_transcript` containing tool CALLS only, while
  `observatory.ImportMotokoSession` has parsed `tool_result` all along — but ran
  only from a manual `ailang chains import-motoko`. So what the agent was *told*
  (every compiler diagnostic, and the fmt hook's "canonical AILANG would differ
  here") never reached eval data. This is why the dialect bug stayed invisible
  for two weeks while three sessions of analysis blamed the model for syntax it
  had been instructed to use. The motoko executor now surfaces its session JSONL
  path and the eval path imports it, as the Claude branch beside it already did.

- **Teaching prompt v0.16.4: a false claim about `letrec`, and one untaught list
  spelling.** The dialect gate cuts both ways — two of its residual hits were the
  *prompt* being wrong, not the formatter. v0.16.3 taught:

  > ❌ WRONG: letrec doesn't work with `= ...` body (parse error)

  That is false. The form type-checks **and runs correctly** (verified with
  `ailang check` and `ailang run`, not assumed); the claim went stale when
  M-SYNTAX-AI-FORGIVING R1 made an equation body a `;`-separated statement
  sequence. Teaching a model to avoid valid syntax is the same failure class as
  the formatter contradicting the prompt. The section now says both forms work
  and recommends the block, because that is what `ailang fmt` emits. Also
  `List[T]` → `[T]` in 4 places: `[T]` is taught 64 times and is what fmt emits,
  so `List[T]` was a spelling fmt would rewrite (both parse to the identical
  `TypeApp`). Shipped as a **new version** rather than an in-place edit — eval
  rows record `prompt_version`, so mutating v0.16.3 would silently change what
  that label means for every historical row. v0.16.3 stays served byte-identical
  for pinned baselines. Note `prompts/` is `//go:embed`-ed, so serving v0.16.4
  requires a rebuild.

- **`ailang fmt` no longer forces a blank line after `module` or between
  imports.** Found by running the fmt A/B for real and reading the transcript.
  The teaching prompt writes the header as ONE contiguous block — `module`
  directly followed by `import` in 21 examples (2 blank-separated) and `import`
  directly followed by `import` in 55 (ZERO blank-separated) — while fmt inserted
  a blank line at every one of those boundaries. So on essentially every file a
  model wrote, the motoko fmt extension reported the file non-canonical.

  Worse, because the only difference WAS a blank line and the extension diffs
  LINES, the diff body rendered EMPTY: measured live
  (`session_recursion_fibonacci_151c55bc`), the model received `Canonical AILANG
  would differ here:` followed by nothing at all — a "your code is wrong" signal
  carrying zero information about what to change.

  This was invisible to `TestFmtDoesNotDriftFromTeachingPrompt` by construction:
  that gate compares token MULTISETS so layout never registers, and a blank line
  contributes no tokens. `TestFmtIsAByteNoOpOnPromptHeaderShape` closes the gap
  with the property the extension actually keys on — a prompt-shaped file must
  format to itself byte-for-byte. Re-running the ON arm afterwards: fmt messages
  2 → 1, empty messages 1 → 0, and the surviving one is an actionable layout hint.

- **`ailang eval-elo` no longer silently swallows unknown flags — and that is why
  agent benchmark selection had never worked.** Its argument loop understood only
  `--json`; every other token fell through to `resultsDir = a`. So
  `--mode agent --persist <db>` ran as a **silent no-op**: it printed a table,
  exited 0, and wrote nothing.

  That is the exact command `eval_confidence.go` and `eval_saturation.go` print
  when the ratings DB is empty. Following the instruction appeared to work, so
  agent-mode ratings were never seeded, `--benchmarks-by-confidence` could never
  be used, and every agent A/B fell back to a HARDCODED benchmark list that goes
  stale as the subject's ELO moves — the mechanism behind the repeated
  "both arms 100%, 0 discordant pairs" nulls.

  Unknown flags now exit non-zero. `--persist`/`--mode` get a specific message
  pointing at `tools/eval-elo`, which is where persistence correctly lives:
  that tool fits ONE blended rating per mode (the scale the ratings DB and the
  confidence selector compare against), whereas this command fits AILANG and
  Python separately — subject 2664 here vs 1789 there over the same rows.
  Persisting from here would have silently corrupted the selector's frame of
  reference.

- **Both nightly A/Bs now choose their benchmarks by headroom, not by tier or by
  a frozen list.** With agent ratings finally seedable (see above), the damage
  was measurable: of the 32 rated benchmarks in the microRAG arm's smoke+core
  set, **17 sit in the Trivial band** — every model passes, so the pair is always
  concordant and contributes exactly zero to McNemar. Over half the night's GPU
  was buying no information. The fmt arm had the opposite failure: a list
  hand-picked correctly against subject ELO 2196, then frozen while the subject's
  rating moved.

  Both now call `--benchmarks-by-confidence`, which drops the saturated band and
  ranks the rest by proximity to the field's median rating — where discordance,
  the thing McNemar actually consumes, is maximised. And **no silent fallback**:
  if selection is unavailable the A/B is SKIPPED with an alert, because a null
  from the wrong benchmarks is indistinguishable from a null from a working
  treatment, and that ambiguity has already cost weeks.

- **A failed agent run now banks its transcript too.** Found by running the
  thing rather than trusting the unit tests: when the executor returns a Go
  error (crash, wall-clock kill, non-agentic result),
  `RunAgentBenchmarkWithExecutor` returned `nil` for the result, so the session
  import above could never fire — on exactly the runs that matter most. The fmt
  dialect bug itself was found in a run that thrashed. Those two post-execution
  error paths now return a diagnostics-only result carrying the session path and
  nothing else, and the caller banks the transcript before recording the
  `api_error` row.

### Changed

- The `ailang fmt` dialect ratchet (`TestFmtDoesNotDriftFromTeachingPrompt`)
  drops from **9 to 2** (9 → 4 by fixing the formatter, 4 → 2 by fixing the
  prompt), and its `knownDivergence` allowlist is deleted. That
  allowlist waved through any block whose output gained a `concat_String`, which
  hid COMPOUND cases — blocks drifting for interpolation *and* another reason
  were excused on the interpolation alone, so the true residual had been 16, not
  9. An allowlist that excuses nothing is a rubber stamp. The remaining 4 are
  diagnosed in the test, not unknown.

### Benchmark Results (M-EVAL)

**Overall Performance**: 79.7% success rate (1711 total runs, 1715 standard + 298 agent files banked)

**Standard Eval (0-shot + self-repair), vs v0.30.0:**

| Metric | v0.30.0 | v0.32.0 | Change |
|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| 0-shot (first attempt) | 73.2% | 74.3% (840/1130) | +1.1pp |
| Final (with repair) | 78.2% | 80.3% (908/1130) | +2.1pp |
| Repair effectiveness | +5.0pp | +6.0pp | +1.0pp |
| Python (final) | 83.6% | 83.5% (700/838) | -0.1pp |

**Agent Eval (multi-turn), AILANG only:** 94.9% (244/257) vs v0.30.0's 78.5% — **not a clean
apples-to-apples comparison**: the agent_suite roster changed release-to-release (this release
removed `opencode-or-deepseek-v4-pro` mid-run for reliability, per the confidence-gating design's
new-model/rated-model split logic), so the jump partly reflects roster composition, not a pure
capability gain. Per-model this release: `claude-sonnet-4-6` 94%, `claude-haiku-4-5` 92%,
`opencode-or-deepseek-v4-flash` 92%, `opencode-or-glm-5-2` 80%, `gpt5-6-luna` 59%.

**ELO leaderboard (standard mode, all 18 `extended_suite` models, first run with full Anthropic
coverage):** `claude-opus-5` leads at 2163 ELO (56-benchmark coverage), `gpt5-6-sol` 2147,
`or-kimi-k3` 2115 (narrowly ahead of `gemini-3-1-pro` 2113 — a strong open-source showing),
`gemini-3-6-flash` 1986, `claude-sonnet-5` 1972.

**Tag analysis:** 24 benchmarks show AILANG beating Python by ≥10pp (see `--ailang-wins`) —
notably `list_comprehension` (11 models), `tree_transformation_pipeline` (7), `higher_order_functions`
(6). Thin/weak tags worth attention: `bitwise` (0/2 AILANG vs 2/2 Python), `argv`/`fs_read`/
`programbench_probe` (all -33pp) — small-N, not yet statistically meaningful, but a repeated
pattern worth a benchmark-manager look.

**Demote candidates** (saturated ≥95% across standard+agent, both languages): STRICT —
`contract_leap_year`, `effect_composition`, `float_eq`, `state_machine_elevator`. ADJUSTED
(excluding `api_error` runs) additionally — `ast_patch_roundtrip`, `cli_args`,
`csv_to_json_converter`, `merge_sort`.

**Known data-quality caveat**: standard-mode tier scope was not uniform across all 18 models
this release (a same-day resume pass gave 3 Anthropic models the full 56-benchmark tier instead
of the intended gated 37; `gpt5-4-mini`/`gemini-3-flash` additionally picked up some out-of-tier
rows from an accidental unscoped rescan). Documented in `baseline.json`'s `standard.note` field.
Design doc: [m-eval-standard-confidence-gating.md](../design_docs/implemented/v0_32_0/m-eval-standard-confidence-gating.md).
